## Handover (globe to street)

Goal: one system. The user zooms from the world to a city in the same 1-bit pixel look, and the swap between
renderers is not noticeable. Code: `app/globe/handover/` (+ small extensions in `engine/` and `street/`).

**One camera, owned by the globe.** `GlobeRenderer` keeps the view in its internal zoom `zu`, whose range now goes
past the Three.js globe's maximum (6.5) up to the street map's maximum: above 6.5 the same number is the street
camera through the registration `zoom_map = zu + log2 cos(lat)`. The globe canvas stays the pointer target at every
scale (drag, wheel, pinch, tap, inertia, flights are the existing, tuned code), the street map is `embedded`
(transparent root, no pointer events, host-driven camera). So a gesture that crosses the threshold is the same
gesture: nothing is handed over, only what is drawn changes. After every camera tick (`onFrame`) the controller
pushes the registered camera and the globe's animated inset into the street map and renders it synchronously
(`StreetMap.setCamera(..., { sync: true })`), so both renderers show the same instant. Measured registration
(`__handoverDebug.registrationError`, projection of a place by each renderer, across wheel zoom and flights): under
1e-9 px in the dissolve band; the pixels agree too (below).

**Cut (default) or dissolve** (`HANDOVER.dissolve`, default `false`). The pixel-art dissolve between the renderers is
parked until the fade is mastered; the code path is intact (`HANDOVER.dissolve = true`, or `?globe-debug&dissolve=1` for
tests). In cut mode there is no blend at all: the street map is created opaque and shown or hidden as a whole.
Past `cutZoom` (3.7; back below `cutBackZoom` 3.45, hysteresis) the controller renders the street map synchronously for
the exact camera and waits until its tiles for it are loaded (`map.areTilesLoaded()`, polling frames; after
`cutMaxWaitMs` = 1200 it swaps anyway, so the globe never stalls; and never while the camera is past the globe's own maximum zoom
6.5, see "One camera per frame" in `docs/street-architecture.md`), then swaps in a single task, hence one paint:
street root visible, globe labels hidden, street markers and labels shown, Three.js suspended, all transitions off. The
swap is a ~300 ms tone cross-fade of the picture (`HANDOVER.crossfadeMs`, `seedFrom` / `crossfadeTo`; instant under
reduced motion), not a click. Going
back, the Three.js frame (and its labels) is drawn synchronously for the current camera BEFORE the street map is hidden.
No frame ever shows both label layers or neither (asserted per animation frame by `scripts/globe/framing.mjs cut`).
The two renderers are registered to under a pixel, so only the line style changes at the cut.

**Bands** (`HANDOVER`, zoom `zu`; hysteresis everywhere, a function of position, not a state machine). Rows marked
(dissolve) apply only with `HANDOVER.dissolve = true`:

| zu | |
| --- | --- |
| below `mountZoom` 2.6 (`unmountZoom` 1.9 on the way back, after 2.5 s) | street map not loaded / released |
| 2.6 | chunk requested, map created (also when a place is selected) |
| `followZoom` 3.0 | the map follows the camera while invisible (trailing 140 ms), so its tiles are ready |
| `blendStart` 3.3 to `blendEnd` 4.1 (dissolve) | pixel-grid dither dissolve: `blendAt(zu)` (smoothstep) drives `StreetMap.setBlend`, slew-limited to 450 ms per full swing |
| 3.7 and up (cut; 4.1 with the dissolve) | the street map alone; the Three.js scene is suspended (no draw calls, the camera still ticks) |
| 2.5 to 3.3 (`HANDOVER.routeFlat`) | the globe's lifted route arcs flatten onto the ground (`routeLift`), ahead of the dissolve |

The dissolve is on the art-pixel grid (Bayer, the street pass's `blend`), so lines morph rather than cross-fade:
the same coastline in both renderers is simply the same cells. Measured at several zooms and three regions
(`handover-shots.mjs seam`, art pixels of 3 CSS px, DPR 2): 95.9 to 99.9 % of the globe's ink cells have street ink
within one art pixel of the same view, no doubled lines (the missing few percent are the dotted graticule and the
route's different dash phase); the other way round (street to globe) 77 to 96 %, the rest being street-only detail.
The route stroke was made 2 art pixels with the globe's 7 px dash period so its weight survives the handover.

**Overlay**: markers and labels are one behaviour. The street map's overlay uses the globe's look when embedded
(`OverlayLook "globe"`: same marker sizes and ring, same type, same box and pixel-label drawing (`engine/box-scene.ts`, `pixel-labels.ts`), same
priorities: at street scale every label may show, collisions decide). Ownership flips once at dissolve 0.8 (back at
0.65), as a 120 ms opacity cross-fade of the two DOM layers (reduced motion: instant); picking follows the owner
(`pickOverride`). `focusedSlug` and the selection are forwarded to both.

**Tile state** (`StreetMap.onTileStatus`): the zoom limit is the street map's maximum only while a tile source works
(`primary`/`fallback`), else the globe's 6.5. While the chunk or the first probe is pending a flight toward street
scale waits at 6.5 with its clock paused and resumes (no spinner, no blank: the globe is on screen). If the sources
die (`capped`), the street map is lost or its context is lost while the camera is at street scale, the camera eases
back to 6.5 (the controller flies it; the engine's own ease is off when embedded), then the dissolve returns to the
globe, then the limit drops. `capped` (or a failed chunk, or no WebGL2) shows the small notice "Street detail is
unavailable right now." (a `role="status"` paragraph at the bottom left of the map, empty otherwise) when the user is
within 0.6 of the limit or a place is selected. When tiles come back the limit lifts and the notice goes. A place
outside the fallback archive's bounds is not flown to at street scale (`StreetMap.covers`).

**Framing: the bounding box.** A place with a published `bbox` (`[west, south, east, north]`, the true extent of the city or area
it sits in) is framed on that box, and the camera CENTRES ON THE CENTRE OF THE BOX, not on the recorded GPS point (which can
sit anywhere in the city). `placeFraming(place)` (`engine/framing.ts`, pure) returns the centre and the radius to fit: the
centre of the box (the plain mean of its longitudes and latitudes; boxes crossing the antimeridian are not supported and
are rejected as invalid) and `bboxFitRadiusKm` (the box's larger half extent, so the whole box is on screen with the 25 %
margin, fed to the formula below unchanged). Every camera move to a place takes its centre and zoom from it: the selection
flight, the direct-load fit view (`routes/shell.tsx`, `handover/controller.ts`), the retreat and the reduced-motion jump, the
street map's coverage test (`covers`) and the latitude correction of the street zoom. `lib/projection.ts` also hands the same
radius to the globe as the place's `viewRadiusKm`. The marker, its label and the routes stay on the recorded point
(`GlobePlace.lat` / `lon`); only the camera moves to the box. Without a (valid) `bbox`, `placeFraming` returns the point and
its view radius, and the next paragraph applies, as before.

**Framing: the view radius.** A place's framing comes from its published `viewRadiusKm` (optional, 0.5 to 500; default
`DEFAULT_VIEW_RADIUS_KM` = 12 km, a typical city-wide framing: a mid-size city is seen whole, so you can tell where
you are, with streets still legible). `engine/framing.ts`: the circle of that radius must fit the FREE area (box width
minus `insetRight`, by height) with a 25 % margin:

```
circlePx = min(width - insetRight, height) / 2 / 1.25
zu       = log2( circlePx * 6371.0088 / radiusKm * 2*pi / 512 )          // = radiusPxToZoom(px per radian)
```

The unified zoom has one scale at the view centre (`512 * 2^zu / (2 pi)` px per radian, any latitude: the Mercator
`log2 cos lat` cancels the stretch), so the formula is latitude independent. The result is clamped to the whole-globe
fit below and the street map's maximum above (`selectionZoom`); without a street map it stays the regional select zoom 3.2
(never zooming out). The renderer uses the inset it is HEADING for (`getInsetTarget`), so a selection that opens the
panel lands on the framing for the panel's width (the canvas applies the inset before the selection). Examples at
1440x900 with the panel open (free 720x900): Lisbon (10 km) zu 11.1, Paris (14 km) 10.65 and Ho Chi Minh City (18 km)
10.3. Selections, list selections and direct loads all use it.

**Selecting a place** (marker, label, list, `setSelected(slug, true)`): a flight to the place's framing, to street scale
when `tiles` are configured and the street map is not known to fail (the street map is mounted at once and the
flight waits at the limit if it has to), else to the select zoom 3.2 as before. Street flights keep the pan ahead of
the zoom (the target stays near the centre while the scale explodes), pull back to the cruise zoom between two
distant street-scale places, and last 0.9 to 6.5 s (about 5 s from the world). Direct load or reload of
`/locations/:slug` does NOT fly: the shell derives `initialView = { lon, lat, fitRadiusKm }` (`GlobeFitView`) from the
loader's places, the renderer computes the framing on its first sized frame, and from the very first frame the camera
is the final one (constant; asserted by sampling every animation frame). The street map may not be ready at first
paint: the Three.js globe is drawn at its own maximum (6.5) centred on the place, with the panel's inset, and the cut
happens when the street map is ready (never a flight, never a blank). A saved view (mobile remount) wins over the fit view. With the panel open (`insetRight`), both renderers use the same shifted centre; the selected place's
bounding box (its framing centre; the point when it has no box) lands in the middle of the free left half. Mobile: the slide-over unmounts the globe, so there is no flight with the panel open
and a direct load of a place URL shows the world after the panel is closed (as before).

**Focus circle** (`HANDOVER.revealFocus`, one constant, now `false`): at street scale, 450 ms after arrival, the street
pass can open `setReveal` around the selected place: inside a circle (up to 280 CSS px, feathered, Bayer-masked) the
vector render shows instead of the pixel art. With the cut and the city-wide framing it looked wrong on the
screenshots (anti-aliased source lines over the pixel art read as doubled lines, e.g. the Seine in Paris), so it is
off by default; the code stays and comes back with the dissolve.

**Lifecycle**: both renderers are created and disposed by the controller. The mobile slide-over unmounting the
globe disposes the street map too (two contexts); the view comes back identical including the street scale
(`GlobeViewState.street`): a restored street view (or a framed start view) mounts the street map at once while the globe is drawn at 6.5, and
swaps without a dissolve once ready (12 s cap, then the view is clamped to 6.5). Context loss: Three and the street
renderers keep their own recovery; either one lost sets `data-state="lost"`; a lost street context makes the street
map unusable (retreat to the globe). WebGL2 missing for the street map = street unavailable, the globe is unaffected.

**Reduced motion**: flights are jumps; the swap is instant (as always); no focus circle; overlay swap instant;
routes static. **Idle**: zero rAF calls, zero ticks, zero street renders in all states (measured at world, held
mid-dissolve and street scale; the follow debounce is a timer, not a frame loop). The one exception is the world view after 8 s without input: the idle rotation ("Idle rotation" below), off in every debug page. **Phones**: art pixel 2 CSS px
(both renderers use the globe's rule); the street map renders at native art resolution by default (`renderScale` 3);
`highResolution` is the device-resolution path (see docs/street-architecture.md).

**Accessibility**: unchanged. All overlays and canvases are `aria-hidden` and inert (the street root is `inert`
while it is not visible); the one exception is the map credits button, a sibling of those layers (not inside an
`aria-hidden` subtree) that is always focusable, and the places list is the keyboard path to
street view (verified: Enter on a list link, focus stays on a real element through the whole flight). No live
region announces the switch to street view (it would be noise); only the unavailable notice is a live region.

**Measurements** (Apple M4, Chrome for Testing 153 headless, production build, local PMTiles, 1440x900@2; 390x844@3 emulated):
Ho Chi Minh City flight from the world: 4.9 s; rAF interval p50 16.7, p95 16.8, max 33.4 ms; 2 frames over 25 ms on
desktop and 1 on mobile, all at the flight START (zoom 2.5 to 2.7, where the street map is created; the dissolve and
street scale have none); JS heap 11 to 37 MB. GPU-synced frame in the dissolve band (Three.js render + registered
street render + pass, circular pan, 300 frames): desktop p50 10.1 / p95 12.2 / max 17.5 ms, mobile 6.7 / 8.2 / 11.9 ms.
Contexts: 20 slide-over open/close cycles, 0 live while open and 1 after (22 created, 21 lost); 20 world to street to
world round trips, 3 live at street scale (Three + 2) and 1 after (43 created, 42 lost). `scripts/globe/handover-*.mjs`.

### Performance behaviour of the handover (see `docs/performance.md`)

- The street map is created at unified zoom 2.6 (`mountZoom`, or at the click of a place) and released 2.5 s after the camera leaves its range. While the globe is the shown renderer the map is **inactive** (`StreetMap.setActive(false)`): tiles keep loading, but there is no canvas copy, no pass and no overlay work, and the camera is only pushed to it once the camera rests (140 ms debounce: invisible, so nothing on screen can disagree with the camera; a swap that waits for tiles pushes it, hidden, on every tick). From the cut it is pushed on every tick, snapped to the art-cell grid (`street/core/snap.ts`) while the zoom is steady.
- The street map renders at native art resolution (3 map pixels per cell per axis), like the globe. `highResolution` follows `HANDOVER.revealFocus` (off).
- The street chunk is fetched and evaluated in an idle period 2.5 s after the globe is up (not on data-saver/2g connections), so the first flight or zoom does not pay for it.
- **Pixel density is constant** (owner, 2026-10-12: "we should keep a constant pixel density"). There is no frame governor: the art cell is `TUNING.pixelSize(minSide, dpr)`, a function of the viewport and the device pixel ratio only (`engine/tuning.test.ts`), the street render scale (3) is read once at creation, and `engine/density.test.ts` plus `scripts/globe/handover-perf.mjs density` pin that nothing at runtime changes either (docs/performance.md, "Constant pixel density"). The cell changes only when the viewport crosses the 520 px phone rule or the window moves to a display with another DPR. A device that cannot hold the frame budget drops frames at full density; `__globeDebug.cell()` and `__streetDebug.cellCss()` read the cell.

### Behaviour notes

- There are no dot markers any more: every place and group is a RECTANGLE on the pixel overlay ("Detection boxes"). A
  rectangle is shown or hidden as a whole, from its place's centre only (see "Marker clipping fix" below, which still
  describes the rule); picking and labels use the same answer, so a hidden place can be neither seen nor clicked.
- Routes: only the lines in the `routes` prop are drawn, as great-circle arcs lifted above the surface through
  the route's ordered points, and ONLY the routes that have the SELECTED place as one of their stops
  (`routesForPlace`, engine/geometry.ts; the stop is matched by coordinates, the seam type has no slugs on routes):
  none on the world view, none while nothing is selected, in the globe and the street map alike. The shown route plays
  the 2.2 s draw-on once when motion is allowed; under reduced motion it is complete and static. Its stops are drawn
  even inside a closed group (`LodTree.setExtraForced`). Hidden lines are not rendered at all (`RouteLayer.show`).
- Borders: ON from internal zoom 3.25 (a hysteresis of 0.05 each side), off below; the fade between is a timed TONE ramp (the faintest palette
  level stepping up to full ink over 200 ms, and the same backwards), never a dither and never a grey at rest (`TUNING.borderZoom`,
  `engine/palette.ts bordersWanted / borderLevel`, `GlobeScene.syncCamera`; "Binary visibility" below).
- Labels: drawn on the pixel overlay canvas (`aria-hidden`, no tab stops: a visual duplicate of the places list, extra tab
  stops would only repeat it), see "Detection boxes". The canvas is `pointer-events: none`; the canvas pointer handler
  hit-tests the targets of the drawn nodes (`BoxScene.hit`: the convex hull of a box and its label plus slop, "Targets and labels"),
  so a drag that starts on a label or a box still rotates, and a click or tap on one selects (a place) or flies to frame it (a group).
- Input: drag (inertia stops cleanly; none under reduced motion; none after a pinch), tap/click, and the Apple Maps model of the next section (pinch and
  mouse wheel zoom about the pointer, a two-finger swipe pans). `touch-action: none` and `overscroll-behavior: none` are set on the canvas only; the container,
  the places list and the panel keep default touch and wheel behaviour. There is no keyboard handling on purpose (the canvas is not focusable).
- Debug introspection: `window.__globeDebug` exists only with `?globe-debug` in the URL or
  `sessionStorage["globe-debug"] = "1"`; the browser checks use it.

### Input model (2026-10-19: align with Apple Maps)

| Input | Does | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Drag (mouse, one finger) | pans (rotates the globe), lets go with inertia | `engine/controls.ts` |
| **Two-finger swipe** on a trackpad (Magic Mouse surface, precision touchpad) | **pans**, like a drag; **never zooms** | `wheel.ts` classifies it `swipe` |
| **Pinch / spread** on a trackpad (`wheel` with `ctrlKey`; Safari `gesturestart/change/end`) | zooms about the pinch point | `pinch` |
| Pinch / spread on a touch screen (two pointers) | zooms about the midpoint of the fingers; the midpoint moving pans | `controls.ts` |
| **Classic mouse wheel** (discrete notches) | zooms about the cursor (a notch of 100 px is 0.16 levels) | `wheel` |

The zoom is **anchored**: the point of the map under the cursor (or the pinch midpoint) stays under it while the zoom changes, instead of zooming into the centre.

**Anchor maths** (`engine/anchor.ts`, pure). The camera is `projectUnit`'s: a perspective camera at distance `d` from the centre of the unit sphere. For a screen offset `(u, v)` from the projection centre (the inset's and the lift's shifts are inside it: the renderer subtracts `canvasLeft + cw/2 - shiftBuf` and `ch/2 - liftBuf`), with `rho2 = (u^2+v^2)/f^2`, a surface point exists iff `1 - rho2 (d^2 - 1) >= 0`. `unprojectOffset` is the ray against the sphere; `solveCentre` is the INVERSE in closed form: the view centre that puts a given surface point under `(u, v)` at another zoom (`g = (d rho2 + sqrt(disc)) / (1 + rho2)`, then `lon' = lonP - atan2(a, Kh)`, `lat' = atan2(Py, Kh) - atan2(b, g)`; north-up fixes the roll). It uses `atan2` only, so it is exact to a thousandth of a pixel from the world to the street map's maximum zoom (1e7 px per radian); an `acos` version lost 0.1 px at street scale. No iteration, no state. The unified zoom `zu` is the same scale at the view centre at every latitude, so the zoom step is a plain `zu += d` and only the centre is solved; the street map is driven by the globe's camera (it is `embedded`, no pointer events), so the anchor is continuous across the handover by construction (measured below). Where the pointer is OFF the planet the anchor is the limb point in its direction, asked to stay under the pointer clamped into the NEW silhouette: no jump, and a solution exists; if the centre still cannot follow (it would need a latitude past 90) only the zoom changes. Zoom limits are applied first (at a limit nothing moves, but the input still counts as activity: idle rotation stops). `clampView`'s latitude limit (82) can bend the fixed point at the poles.

**The standalone street map** (`/dev/street`, `StreetMap` not `embedded`) takes the wheel through the same router: `scrollZoom` is off, a zoom is `map.easeTo({ zoom, around: unproject(pointer), duration: 0 })`, a swipe is `map.panBy([-dx, -dy], { duration: 0 })` (the content follows the fingers); a touch pinch stays MapLibre's, which already zooms about the midpoint. No synthetic inertia there.

**Telling the wheel's devices apart** (`engine/wheel.ts`, documented rule by rule in the file; `wheel.test.ts` runs a stream per device). A browser does not say which device a `wheel` event is from, so it is a heuristic with a safe default (a swipe pans: the wrong answer that must not happen is a trackpad swipe that zooms). Per event: `ctrlKey` is a pinch (unless it looks like a notch: ctrl + mouse wheel zooms as a wheel); then, per STREAM (events less than 300 ms apart; decided once by the first event, kept until 300 ms of silence):

| Device, browser | `deltaMode` | `deltaX` | `deltaY` | `wheelDeltaY` | Cadence | Rule | Kind |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Mouse wheel, Chrome/Edge, Windows | 0 | 0 | 100 (66.67 at 150 %) | 120 | a notch at a time | multiple of 120 | wheel |
| Mouse wheel, Chrome, Linux | 0 | 0 | 53 | 120 | a notch at a time | multiple of 120 | wheel |
| Mouse wheel, Firefox, Windows/Linux | 1 (lines) | 0 | 3 | 120 | a notch at a time | line mode | wheel |
| Mouse wheel, Chrome, macOS | 0 | 0 | k * 4.000244140625 | -3 dY | a notch at a time | multiple of 4.0002 (the number maps use) | wheel |
| Mouse wheel, Safari/Firefox, macOS | 0 | 0 | whole, 4 and up | -3 dY | a notch at a time | ambiguous: held 40 ms; alone = wheel; a second event decides (`abs(dt * delta) < 200` swipe, else wheel) | wheel |
| Trackpad swipe, Chrome/Safari, macOS | 0 | any | fractions, momentum | -3 dY | 60 Hz, ramps up, decays | fractional / deltaX / under 4 | swipe |
| Trackpad swipe, Firefox, macOS | 0 | any | whole, momentum | 0 or -3 dY | as above | deltaX, under 4, or the second-event rule | swipe |
| Magic Mouse surface | 0 | any | as the trackpad | -3 dY | as the trackpad | as the trackpad | swipe (pans; see below) |
| Precision touchpad, Chrome/Edge, Windows | 0 | any | whole, no momentum | not a multiple of 120 | 60 Hz while the fingers move | deltaX, under 4, or the second-event rule | swipe |
| Trackpad pinch, Chrome/Edge/Firefox | 0 | 0 | small, fractional, signed | any | 60 Hz | `ctrlKey` | pinch |
| Ctrl + mouse wheel | 0 or 1 | 0 | as the mouse | 120 | a notch at a time | `ctrlKey` + a notch signature | wheel |
| Trackpad pinch, Safari | no `wheel`: `gesturestart` / `gesturechange` (cumulative `scale`) / `gestureend` | | | | | `wheel-input.ts`, step = `log2(scale / previous)` | pinch |

The rows are from each browser's documented behaviour and the maps' code, NOT from hardware: see "Not verified" in docs/handoff.md. What it gets wrong, on purpose: a whole-number mouse (macOS Safari/Firefox) spun so fast that its bursts look like a slow swipe pans instead of zooming (the cure is a notch signature or ctrl); a Magic Mouse pans (it sends what a trackpad sends). The zoom speeds are the tuned ones (`WHEEL_ZOOM_PER_PX` 0.0016 a pixel, `PINCH_ZOOM_RATE` 0.12 (see below), a line 33 px, a page 400, at most 0.75 levels a step); a macOS Chrome tick (4 px) counts as a whole notch (0.16).

**Pinch rate (2026-10-19, owner: "super slow").** Chromium synthesizes a trackpad pinch's wheel as `deltaY = -100 * ln(scale)` ([the review that added it](https://codereview.chromium.org/250923004/): "zooming in 2x is about -70"; the PDF viewer inverts it as `exp(-deltaY / 100)`), so a physical pinch is `log2(scale) = 0.0144` levels a px of `deltaY`: fingers 3 cm to 6 cm = about 69 px = one level. maplibre-gl's trackpad rate is 0.0072 a px (1/100 through a logistic, `scroll_zoom`) and d3-zoom's ctrl rate 0.029. The old 0.012 was 0.83 of physical, and a trackpad is only 5 cm across, so a pinch covered little: `PINCH_ZOOM_RATE` (`engine/wheel.ts`) is now 0.036 (2.5 times physical, Apple Maps amplifies): the same 3 cm to 6 cm pinch is about 2.5 levels; the per-event cap is 1.5 (a fast pinch sends deltaY of 20+). Touch pointers and Safari `gesturechange` are exact (`log2(d / d_prev)`, `log2(scale / scale_prev)`, no gain). Nothing swallows a pinch: `ctrlKey` is decided per event before any stream, hold or swipe default (`wheel.test.ts`, "pinch calibration"). Try a rate live: `?input-debug&pinch-rate=0.05`; the overlay shows each step and the pinch's running total. The Chromium formula is from a 2015 review and was not re-read in current source.

**Pinch rate, second round (owner on a real Mac trackpad, Chrome: 0.036 "still painfully slow").** Measured in the browser (`input/check.mjs effective`): the EFFECTIVE camera zoom equals the requested one (14 events at deltaY -1.3 to -2.8: asked 3.6664, gained 3.6664; six events dispatched in one task add up, nothing is coalesced, smoothed, lerped or capped per frame; the app has no zoom damping at all). So the cause was the gain against what Chrome really sends, which is evidently far smaller than Chromium's `-100 ln(scale)` suggests, not anything in the pipeline. One real defect was found and fixed: with `ctrlKey`, a delta that looked like a notch (a multiple of 120 in `wheelDeltaY`, or of 4.0002) took the WHEEL branch, 75 times slower (0.0016 a px against 0.12); ctrl now takes the wheel branch only for `deltaMode` line/page. `PINCH_ZOOM_RATE` is now 0.12; the per-event cap stays 1.5. Try rates on any page: `?pinch-rate=0.2` (kept in localStorage; `?pinch-rate=reset` forgets it); with `?input-debug` the overlay shows each step, the pinch total, the effective zoom in levels a second and the wheel events per frame (max, average). With the formula taken literally a 3 cm to 8 cm pinch (deltaY about -98) would now give about 12 levels; if the owner's numbers show that real events are as small as feared that is fine, if not, lower the rate.

**Pinch rate, third round (owner, Mac trackpad, Chrome, private window: 0.12 and 0.4 far too fast, 2 absurd; 0.01 "the sweet spot").** The default is 0.01 levels a px of ctrl-wheel deltaY, about 0.7 of the physical gain (0.0144 from Chromium's `-100 ln(scale)`): the real trackpad sends bigger deltas than the earlier rounds assumed, and the first round's "too slow" came from the pinch being handled as a mouse wheel (the 22x smaller gain, fixed in round two), not from the rate. `?pinch-rate=N` still tries another live (kept in localStorage).

**A swipe is a drag.** Each event is `panPixels(-dx, -dy)` (the drag's own scale: content follows the fingers, scroll down moves the content up). When the stream goes quiet for 70 ms its last samples are handed to the drag's release (`releaseVelocity`, `fling`): a trackpad's OS momentum arrives as events that decay to nothing, so there is nothing left to carry and no inertia is added (no double momentum); a device that stops at speed (a precision touchpad without momentum) lets go like a drag.

**The page keeps its scroll.** The wheel and the Safari gesture events are `preventDefault`ed (`passive: false`) on the map's canvas ONLY, never on the document or the container: over the map the page neither scrolls nor goes back on a horizontal swipe (the canvas also has `touch-action: none` and `overscroll-behavior: none`); over the hero's panels, the lists and the rest of the page nothing changes.

**Checking a device: `?input-debug`** (or `sessionStorage["input-debug"] = "1"`) shows an overlay with the last eight events (kind, the deltas, `deltaMode`, `wheelDeltaY`, what it did, and the rule that decided) and fills `window.__inputDebug.entries`. Checks only: `sessionStorage["input-kind"] = "wheel" | "swipe"` on a debug page declares every plain wheel event one or the other (Chrome's synthetic `page.mouse.wheel` has `wheelDeltaY` 60 at a device scale factor of 2, whatever the delta, so no signature; the scripts' libraries set `wheel` unless `INPUT_KIND=swipe|auto`).

**Checks.** `apps/web/scripts/input/check.mjs [classify swipe zoom anchor offdisc pinch safari fling guard street]` (synthetic events: see its header). Measured 2026-10-19 (Chrome for Testing 153 headless, 1440x900@2, demo content, local PMTiles): the point under the cursor stays under it within 5e-10 px on the globe model and 0.011 px in the street map's own projection over 160 notches from zoom 2.9 to 12.6 (across the cut at zoom 3.98) and back; the zoom step never exceeds 0.16; at the cut itself the street map's projection of the anchored point is 3e-7 px from the cursor. Unit: `anchor.test.ts` (fixed-point property at latitudes -80 to 78, longitudes next to the antimeridian, zooms 1.2 to 17) and `wheel.test.ts`.

### Idle rotation (2026-10-08; slowed 2026-10-13)

When the globe is **fully unzoomed** and nobody has touched anything for **8 s** (`SPIN.idleMs`), the earth turns slowly **eastward** (its surface moves to the right, like the planet; the view's longitude decreases) at **0.2 degrees a second** (one turn in 30 minutes, `SPIN.degPerSec`; it was 4 until 2026-10-13, see the table below), **accelerating from rest** along a smoothstep over 3 s (`SPIN.easeInMs`) and **coasting to rest** in 1 s (`SPIN.stopMs`) when it must stop for a reason that is not an input. Code: the pure decisions and the velocity state machine in `engine/idle-spin.ts` (`spinBlock`, `IdleSpin`, `easeInAngle`, `stopAngle`, `spinFrameDue`), wired in `engine/renderer.ts` (`stepSpin`, `armSpin`, `chainSpin`, `noteActivity`, `noteChange`); tests `engine/idle-spin.test.ts`; browser check `scripts/globe/idle-spin.mjs`.

- **When it may run** (`spinBlock`, first reason wins): rotation not disabled (`?no-rotate`, below); no reduced motion; the tab visible; the GL context alive; the globe not suspended (the street map does not own the picture); no place selected; no place focused from the list; no inset (detail panel closed; the mobile slide-over unmounts the globe anyway); **zoom at most 0.15 levels above the whole-globe fit** (below it too: the ring of type's regime, "Ring of type", where the home page opens, turns with the planet; `SPIN.unzoomedSlack`: a wheel at the minimum lands exactly on the fit, the slack absorbs rounding; the fit zoom is per screen and per inset, 2.70 at 1440x900, so "unzoomed" means the whole globe in the window); no drag, pressed pointer, flight, inertia or inset slide.
- **The idle clock** restarts on any pointer down, move, up or cancel, wheel, touch start or key press anywhere on the window (passive capture listeners, `renderer.onInput`), on every camera change (`setView`, `flyTo`, a tick that advances a flight, inertia or the inset slide) and on every change of a condition above (selection, focus, inset, reduced motion, visibility, suspension, zoom limit, context loss and restore). Transitions of the boxes that the rotation itself causes are NOT activity.
- **Why 0.2 degrees a second, and why a frame per refresh** (2026-10-13). The owner found 4 degrees a second a bit too fast, and the labels flickered while it turned. A pixel-art globe cannot move smoother than one art pixel per frame, so the picture is redrawn on every display refresh and the motion is spread over the frames (the features cross their art-pixel boundaries at different times: at 0.2 degrees a second each frame changes about 40 pixels, every frame changes the picture, the longest gap between two pictures is 20 to 26 ms), and the speed is chosen by what the LABELS do, not by the picture. Measured with the real engine (`?globe-debug&rotate&spin-idle=100&spin-speed=X`, the preview of the owner's 146 places, 1440x900@2 dark, a read of the labels after every drawn frame; eight starting longitudes of 60 s each at the world view, and one run of Europe and of the Balkans, which are driven by a scripted camera at the same speed since the rotation only runs unzoomed; `idle-spin.mjs cadence` and `groups.mjs spin-flicker` are the checks that stay):

  | deg/s | one turn | an art pixel at the centre every | world: slot changes / min | of them back to the slot just left | labels appearing or disappearing / min | Europe: slot changes / min (back) | Balkans: slot changes / min (back) |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 4 (shipped until 2026-10-13) | 90 s | 68 ms | 52.2 | 11.6 | 113.7 | 83.7 (0) | 442.9 (59.8) |
  | 1.2 (the first version's speed) | 5 min | 227 ms | 28.5 | 8.2 | 39.1 | 25.1 (0) | 176.0 (28.7) |
  | 0.6 | 10 min | 0.45 s | 22.1 | 7.1 | 20.1 | 19.8 (1.8) | 90.1 (12.6) |
  | 0.3 | 20 min | 0.9 s | 5.6 | 0.5 | 13.2 | 6.3 (0) | 43.2 (6.3) |
  | **0.2 (chosen)** | 30 min | 1.35 s | 4.4 | 0.9 | 8.6 | 7.2 (1.2) | 30.0 (1.8) |
  | 0.15 | 40 min | 1.8 s | 3.0 | 0.4 | 4.9 | 9.5 (2.7) | 27.9 (5.0) |

  All rows are with the label changes below; before them (same speeds, world view) the slot changes were 75.7, 58.1, 44.7, 18.7, 13.4 and 11.0 a minute, of which 28, 34, 29, 11, 9 and 8 swapped back. Reading: above 0.3 degrees a second the labels change places every second or so somewhere on the globe and the visibility events (a label crossing the limb, a group opening into its places) come every half second; below it the globe is calm (a label event every 5 to 7 s at 0.2 on the whole world, most of them a label going over the limb), and 0.15 gains little more for labels while an art pixel takes 1.8 s to cross, which starts to read as stopped. 0.2 is the slowest that still reads as alive: the centre of the disc moves an art pixel (2.5 css px) in 1.35 s, the limb less, and a still screenshot taken 30 s apart shows the earth has moved by 6 degrees. One line, `SPIN.degPerSec`, moves it (0.3 is the next candidate if 0.2 feels too still; both are inside `idle-spin.test.ts` bounds: one turn in 20 to 60 minutes, an art pixel every 0.8 to 2 s).
- **What had to change in the labels for the table to look like that.** Slowing the globe alone was not enough (the "before" column): two neighbours' boxes snap to art cells one at a time, so the camera "rests" for a second between two cell steps and the label tracker ran its plan AT REST in each pause (no upgrade margin, no dwell, `restMs` 160 ms): the label that had given its place up took it back, and lost it again at the next step, two labels swapping places for as long as the globe turned (two neighbouring cities every 2 to 3 s at 0.15 degrees a second). Three small changes, all in the planner and tracker, none touching the camera or the picture: (1) `LabelTracker.setDrifting` (called every frame from the renderer's `spinInfo().spinning`): while the globe turns on its own the camera counts as moving, so plans keep the dwell and the upgrade margin, and a plan at rest is owed when it stops; (2) `PlanUnits.keepSlack` (1.1 cell of room): a label keeps its slot while it is free and clear of the neighbours' boxes with that much LESS room than a new position needs, so a cell of jitter in a neighbour does not take it away (not applied by a plan at rest, where every label settles on its best place with the full room; a real collision, more than the slack, still moves it at once); (3) a nested position (inside the box's corner) needs room to spare as an upgrade, so a box whose width snaps between two cells around the plate's cannot make the label flip in and out. Tests: `label-plan.test.ts` (keepSlack, nested upgrade), `label-track.test.ts` ("a globe that turns on its own").
- **The velocity state machine** (`IdleSpin`: `idle` / `easing-in` / `cruise` / `stopping`, in `engine/idle-spin.ts`, dt-based and frame-rate independent): each frame turns by the exact integral of the velocity profile over the elapsed time (`dt` capped at `SPIN.maxStepMs` = 100 ms, so a stalled frame cannot jump), so a 30, 60 or 120 Hz display turns the same angle at the same time. Ease-in: speed = cruise x smoothstep(t / 3 s), zero acceleration at both ends, distance `V*E*(u^3 - u^4/2)`. Stop: speed = v0 x (1 - t / 1 s)^2, from whatever speed it has (also in the middle of the ease-in): deceleration starts at once (it feels like inertia), the end is soft, the distance coasted is v0 x 1 s / 3 (1.33 degrees from cruise), checked on the real page to three decimals.
- **Stopping**: an **input** (the listeners above) calls `activity`, which zeroes the velocity in the same task: no coasting, the user's own gesture takes over, not one more degree is applied (the view is exactly where the last frame left it; a frame already queued draws an identical picture). A **change that is not an input** (`noteChange`: `setSelected`, `setFocused`, `setInset`, `flyTo`, `setZoomLimit`, a tick that advances a flight, inertia or the inset slide) calls `settle`: the clock restarts and the globe coasts to rest over `SPIN.stopMs`; hidden tab, context loss, reduced motion, the street map and `?no-rotate` stop it hard (nothing is animated any more). While a flight is under way the coasting turns the flight's start instead of the view (the flight is sampled from its endpoints each frame), so it fades out as the flight takes over and the flight lands exactly on its target (checked: the same view with and without the rotation); a flight that lands before the coast is over drops what is left. A pointer held down keeps it from starting; a hover highlight is dropped when it starts (the box would slide away from the pointer). The next start is a full delay after the last input or change.
- **Cost, through the existing on-demand loop**: a `setTimeout` wakes the loop for the idle delay (a timer is not a frame: rest stays at zero frames before it); then, while it turns or coasts, an rAF chain draws one frame per display refresh, with no ceiling (`SPIN.minFrameMs` = 0; the first version's floor of 14 ms skipped a frame that nothing else asked for when it came too soon, which at 120 Hz drew 60 a second but at 90 Hz drew 45 and at 75 Hz 37, choppier than the display allows, to save about 0.3 ms of JS and 0.3 ms of GPU per frame; the knob stays, `spinFrameDue`). Each frame is the normal one with the camera's longitude changed: the cut, the borders and the labels follow as for any camera move. Measured (production build, 1440x900@2, headless Chrome): 60 frames a second, about 0.3 to 0.4 ms JS and 0.25 to 0.34 ms GPU per frame, 4 draw calls, no frame over the 16.7 ms budget, no long task (docs/performance.md). Nothing measured feeds back into the art pixel (there is no frame governor), so a throttled display cannot degrade it, and a coasting or turning globe ends the chain by itself: back to zero frames. The boxes' own transitions that the limb crossings start run at the same pace as the turn while the camera itself is not moving.
- **Flags** (`applySpinFlags`, `?`-param or sessionStorage key): `no-rotate` switches it off on any page. Pages in debug mode (`?globe-debug`, which every browser check runs in) have it OFF by default, because the checks assert zero frames at rest for longer than 8 s; `rotate` turns it back on there, `spin-idle=MS` (100 to 600000, debug pages only) turns it on with a shorter delay, `spin-speed=DEG` (0.01 to 20 degrees a second, debug pages only) turns it on at another cruise speed (what the speed table above was measured with). `__globeDebug.spin()` reports `{ spinning, phase, speed, blockedBy, timerArmed, idleMs, degPerSec, easeInMs, stopMs, enabled }`.
- **The zero-frame idle checks** (`perf` idle, `groups.mjs idle`, `handover-perf.mjs idle`, `check.mjs` section 9) therefore run unchanged and still assert zero frames: they run in debug mode with the rotation off. `idle-spin.mjs` asserts the new behaviour with the delay shortened to 1.5 s: zero frames and zero rAF calls before the delay, the ease-in (speed per 500 ms follows the smoothstep, monotonic, starts from rest), the configured cruise speed eastward, a frame per refresh with no gap over 25 ms between drawn frames (no ceiling), every drawn frame changes the picture, no step longer than 80 ms between two different pictures, an art pixel at the disc's centre every 0.8 to 2.5 s, a stop on a pointer move, a key, a wheel notch and a click in the same task, coasting on a focus change (from the middle of the ease-in and from cruise: only slower, to zero, by the profile's integral) and on a selection, a selection flight that lands on the same view as without the rotation, no start while a pointer is held, never with a place selected or focused, with the panel open (direct load), zoomed past the slack (and it turns inside it), under reduced motion (Chromium's emulation) or in a hidden tab, `?no-rotate`, and a tap on a phone viewport. `groups.mjs spin-flicker` runs the real rotation for 40 s from three longitudes of the world view and counts slot changes (at most 16), swaps back to the slot just left (at most 4) and blinks (0). `SPIN_SPEED=0.5 node idle-spin.mjs` runs the same checks at another speed (the "slow enough" and "alive" expectations of `cadence` then say which candidates they accept: 0.1 to 0.3).

### Skybox (2026-10-08)

Owner: a skybox around the earth in the globe view, like Google Earth's Milky Way and stars, but pixel art and very faint. Code: the pure parts in `engine/sky.ts` (tests `sky.test.ts`), the Three.js objects in `engine/sky-layer.ts` (`SkyLayer`, owned by `GlobeScene`), the tuned numbers in `SKY` (`engine/tuning.ts`, table in [design-tokens.md](design-tokens.md), "Sky"), the renderer's `era`; browser check `scripts/globe/sky.mjs`.

**What it is.** A band (the Milky Way) and a star field, drawn in the globe's own drawing buffer, which IS the art grid, so every pixel is a cell and every tone a palette level: the band is a baked map looked up per pixel and quantised to the sky's two palette tones with a 4x4 Bayer dither anchored to the cell (the stipple of `pixel-line-rules.md` section 3.7: a function of the screen cell and the tone only, so it does not swim); the stars are single cells. No gradient exists anywhere. The band's two tones are palette levels 1 and 2 (`skyTop`: one below the graticule's `faint`, level 3), so the band is dimmer than the graticule in both themes: dark 20 and 31 on the page's 10 (graticule 43), light 240 and 227 on 251 (graticule 213). The stars have a third tone, the graticule's own level (`starTop`, `starTones`): the cap of the whole sky (2026-10-08, owner: the band looked like one gradient and the stars were too few and too even, 'not super bright, just a bit brighter'). Everything is deterministic (seeded noise lattice, seeded star list, integer hash, so the picture is the same on every machine and in every browser).

**Frames and what moves what** (`sky.ts` header, tested). The sky lives in the INERTIAL celestial equatorial frame: north up is celestial north, a point has a right ascension and a declination. The app's earth-fixed axes (`geo.ts`: lon 0 / lat 0 is +Z, lon 90 is +X, north +Y; one handedness for the whole app) are the same axes turned about the polar axis by the earth rotation angle (ERA, the GMST, the right ascension of the Greenwich meridian): a direction of right ascension `ra` has the earth-fixed longitude `ra - ERA`. The chain per pixel is view ray -> earth-fixed (`x east + y north + z c`, the view basis) -> inertial (longitude plus ERA) -> galactic (the Hipparcos matrix, published constants: the galactic pole at RA 192.86 / Dec +27.13, the centre toward RA 266.40 / Dec -28.94, the plane inclined 62.87 degrees to the earth's equator; all asserted in `sky.test.ts`) -> a lookup by galactic longitude and latitude. One 3x3 matrix per frame (`viewToGalactic`) does all of it. Sky handedness is not mirrored: behind the earth, right ascension grows toward the LEFT of the picture, as on a star chart (tested).
- A **camera orbit** (drag, inertia, flights, the places list) changes the view's longitude and latitude and leaves ERA alone: the camera moves through inertial space, the stars behind the earth turn on the screen with the view, as for an observer going round the planet. The ray through the middle of the picture points at RA `lon + ERA + 180`, Dec `-lat`.
- The **earth's own rotation** (the idle rotation, "Idle rotation" above) turns the earth under a camera that stays where it is: `stepSpin` takes the yaw off the view's longitude AND adds it to ERA, so the camera's inertial longitude (view + ERA) is constant and the sky does not move on the screen at all (checked: not one sky pixel differs across a 3.5 s rotation, `sky.mjs spin`). The stars are not dragged, through the ease-in and the coasting stop alike (the same yaw goes to both). The rotation's frames draw an identical sky. (A coast that overlaps a selection flight turns the flight's start instead of the view, and the camera is moving anyway.)
- ERA starts at `SKY.eraDeg` (70), a constant: the sky is the same on every visit and in every screenshot, it is NOT the real sidereal time. At the opening view (lon 15, lat 28) the band crosses the wings of the globe on a slant. A saved view that comes back from the mobile slide-over remounts the globe with ERA at its constant again (the earth was unseen meanwhile); ERA is not part of `GlobeViewState`.
- Under a sky at infinity the camera's distance does not matter, only its orientation (no parallax): zooming changes how much of the sky the earth covers, never the stars' directions.

**Shape of the band** (`bandValue`, baked once into a 256 x 64 byte map over galactic longitude 0..360 and latitude +-45 degrees, about 16,000 noise evaluations, a few ms at start, memoised for remounts): brightness and thickness fall off from the galactic centre (brightest and thickest at l = 0, 5.5 degrees sigma at the anticentre against 10 at the centre), an extra nuclear bulge, the Great Rift (a thin wavy dust lane down the middle from l = -25 to 80), coarse 3-octave value noise for mottling and dust breaks, zero beyond the map. Stars: `SKY.stars.count` (20,000, about 430 to 510 in view at the whole-globe view on a desktop: the visible sky is a 56 x 37 degree window minus the earth) uniform directions (seeded, no grid: tested by the spread of nearest-neighbour distances) thinned by the same map so the density along the band is up to 1.8 times that away from it, each with one of three tones by a heavy-tailed share (`SKY.stars.tierShares`, 60 % level 1, 28 % level 2, 12 % level 3, the graticule's: many faint, some middling, a few brighter; `starTier`). One cell each: no 2-cell sparkles (the owner found them too much). The brightest real stars are NOT embedded: I could not cite or check a catalogue offline and a wrong constellation is worse than none.

**Dimming towards the earth.** The sky is drawn right up to the earth's silhouette and only DIMMED there (2026-10-08; it was removed inside 1.06 radii and faded in to 1.55, which the owner found read as masking out the background around the planet). `skyFade(rho)` is `SKY.fade.limb` (0.55) from the silhouette (`fade.from` = 1) and eases up to 1 at `fade.to` (1.55 radii from the centre of the disc, measured on the screen from the silhouette's circle `f / sqrt(d^2 - 1)`, `skyGeometry`), a smoothstep: the band's brightness is multiplied by it BEFORE the quantisation (so it dims through the dither, density falling away, and at the limb the band only reaches level 1) and a star is kept while the factor exceeds its fixed random rank (a thinning, never a dimmer star: 55 % of the field at the limb). The disc's pixels fail the depth test, so the sky ends exactly at the silhouette; the horizon outline (level 2, `outlineLevel`) is drawn over it and keeps its contrast because the band is level 1 and the rare stars thin out there. The projection centre of the inset (detail panel) is taken into account.

**Binary, timed, zero idle cost.** The layer is on while the farthest corner of the picture is more than `SKY.onRho.rho` (1.75) earth radii from the globe's centre, off when it comes within 1.70 (hysteresis; measured: at 1440 x 900 it goes off at internal zoom 3.14 (rho 1.7; 2.14 at the whole-globe fit), on a 390 x 844 phone at 2.07 (2.71 at the fit)), so the sky is gone before the street map takes over at 3.7 and a globe that fills the picture draws no sky. The on/off value runs by TIME (`FadeArray`, `FADE_MS`, same primitive and clock handling as the borders, instant under reduced motion): a resting frame is fully on (drawn) or fully off (not drawn, no draw call), never half; `GlobeScene.animating` keeps the frame loop alive until the transition ends. The sky adds no frame to an idle page: it is drawn only inside frames that happen anyway, and nothing in it is time-based but that switch (verified: `sky.mjs rest`, `groups.mjs idle at-rest` and `handover-perf.mjs idle` with `SKY=1`: zero rAF, zero frames, zero `gl.clear`). It disappears with the globe at the cut (the globe canvas is suspended and transparent while the street map shows).

**Cost** (docs/performance.md, "Skybox"). One full-buffer triangle with a short fragment shader (a matrix, an `atan` and an `asin`, one 8-bit texture fetch, a Bayer lookup) plus 20,000 one-pixel GL points, drawn after the earth's disc with the depth test on at the far plane: the pixels the disc covers fail the depth test before the shader runs, a globe that fills the picture hides the layer entirely, and a zoomed-out view pays for about 50 to 80 % of a buffer of 576 x 360 (desktop) or 195 x 422 (phone) pixels. Measured (GPU-synced `renderNow` + `readPixels`, 360 frames of a turning globe, ANGLE Metal on an M4, p50): 0.4 to 0.5 ms with and without the sky on desktop and 0.5 to 0.6 ms on the phone viewport, differences inside the noise of the timer (0.1 ms). Not measured on a real low-end phone. No cache texture is used: a cached sky would have to be redrawn on every orientation change, which is every frame in which the sky moves, and the direct pass is cheaper than the copy would be. Memory: a 16 KB texture and 20,000 x 5 floats (the star list grew from 7,000 on 2026-10-08; one-pixel points, the vertex work is the same few instructions, not re-measured).

**Flags** (`applySkyFlags`): `?no-sky` (or sessionStorage `no-sky` = 1) switches it off on any page; a page in debug mode (`?globe-debug`) has it OFF by default because the older checks assert palette-only pixels, nothing outside the boxes and markers (`markers.mjs` compares the whole buffer pixel for pixel), and `?sky` (or sessionStorage `sky` = 1) turns it on there. `SKY=1` runs any of the scripts with it on (`_lib.mjs`, `handover-lib.mjs`). `__globeDebug.sky()` reports `{ era, value, on, rhoMax, drawn, cx, cy, radius, fadeFrom }` (null when off), `setSkyEra(deg)` moves the sky against the earth.

**Checks** (`node apps/web/scripts/globe/sky.mjs [flags|rest|pixels|parity|spin|orbit|timed|reduced|mobile]`): flags; zero frames at rest; only the page colour and the sky's tones outside the globe (the band's two fainter than the graticule's level, the brightest stars AT it and nothing above), lit pixels all the way to the silhouette (no blank halo), the brightest tone the rarest, desktop dark and light and mobile; GPU equals the CPU twin pixel for pixel (16 of 125,037, 0 of 125,088, 2 of 125,106 band pixels differ at three views; 507/507, 450/450, 426/428 projected stars lit, 505, 446 and 426 of them in the tone of their tier (>= 95 % required); needs a dev server because it imports the engine's modules); no sky pixel moves during the idle rotation; an orbit changes the sky and going back restores it bit for bit; the timed switch (a ramp of several frames, a stopped loop, fully on or off at every settled zoom from the whole globe to the cut); reduced motion.

Not verified: Safari / iOS and any GPU but the M4's (the GLSL uses 3.00 ES features: array constants and integer vector bitwise `&`, which every WebGL2 implementation must support), a real phone's frame time, real reduced motion in a browser (Chromium's emulation only).

### Travel paths (2026-10-15)

Every route leg is drawn faintly on the globe and the street map, the selected place's legs in ink: one instanced draw call on the globe, one GeoJSON source and a few filtered layers on the street map, a "Paths" switch and a "Journeys" list in the place panel. Details, numbers and limits: [paths.md](paths.md). `engine/route-layer.ts` (`RouteLayer`) replaced the per-route lines; `routeBuffers`, `routesForPlace` and `isRouteStop` are gone. Debug: `?globe-debug&paths-stress=N`, `__globeDebug.pathStats()`.

### Ring of type (2026-10-13, experiment; flat version 2026-10-13)

Owner: a ring of small spinning type that circles the planet when the globe is zoomed far out. Round 1 built it as a tilted ring in the globe's perspective (a near arc, a small mirrored far arc hidden behind the planet); the owner's reference was 2D, so round 2 (this) is **a flat circle of text concentric with the planet's disc**: the circle of the globe's silhouette, a little larger, the letters' baselines on it and their tops pointing outward, running clockwise, all one size, facing the viewer, none hidden (the reference's dashed square frame is not built). Code: the pure maths in `engine/ring.ts` (tests `ring.test.ts`), the 2D canvas layer in `engine/ring-layer.ts`, the tuned numbers in `RING` (`engine/tuning.ts`, ONE block), the words in `app/globe/ring-text.ts` (`RING_PHRASES`: the one constant file; `lib/site.ts` is not imported, the globe imports nothing from the app, and the engine only receives the joined string through `createHandover({ ringText })`); browser check `scripts/globe/ring.mjs`, perf scenarios `ring-rest` and `ring-wheel` (`scripts/perf/scenarios.mjs`).

**What it is.** `ELLIOT NÉGREL-JERZY · FRONT-END ENGINEER · DATA VISUALISATION · CARTOGRAPHY · DESIGN SYSTEMS · PARIS ·` in capitals, in the labels' own monospace (`--font-mono`), weight 500. Glyph k of `repeats * line length` sits at the angle `phase + k * 2pi / M` clockwise from twelve o'clock, on the circle of radius `r (1 + RING.gap)` (the baseline; `r` is the silhouette's radius now, `silhouetteRadiusPx`; `gap` 0.06 = 17 px at the lowest zoom of a 1440 x 900 screen) plus half a cap height, turned about its centre so that its top points away from the planet. So the text reads clockwise, is upright on the right-hand side of the circle and upside down at the bottom (as text on a circular path is, and as the reference's). Everything is a ratio of `r`: the ring scales with the planet, follows it (and the panel's inset) and never overlaps the disc.

**A ring that closes.** `ringLayout(lineLength, rFloorPx)`, once per viewport and settled inset (not per zoom, so nothing reflows while zooming or while the panel slides): C is the baseline's circumference at the lowest zoom; the count `n` of times the line goes round is the whole number whose em at the nominal tracking (0.14 em on the monospace's 0.6 em advance, the nav's) is nearest `RING.type.targetEmPx` (11 px); the em is that value held within `emSlack` (15 %) of the target and the tracking takes up what is left, so that `n * length` advances of `em * (0.6 + tracking)` are exactly C and the seam (`PARIS · ` into `ELLIOT`) is spaced like every other letter. Both are kept as ratios of `r`, so the ring is closed at every zoom. Measured at the lowest zoom: 1440x900 2 turns, em 12.6 px; 1024x700 2 turns, 10.2 px; 1920x1080 3 turns, 10.5 px; 390x844 1 turn, 11.0 px; 844x390 1 turn, 11.4 px (the tracking never below 0.02 em: a ring too small for the line shrinks the type instead).

**Where it lives in the zoom range: below the whole-globe fit.** The world view (zoom 0 of `GlobeViewState`, the idle rotation's "unzoomed", the hero's `rest`) is the whole-globe fit and, on a page without the showcase (`?no-ring`, a debug page without `?showcase`), what the page loads at: there the ring is found by zooming OUT. With the showcase (the home page) the page OPENS at the lowest zoom, below the fit (`GlobeRenderer.startView`: `minZoom`), where the ring is already shown ("Caption on the sphere"). At the fit the world view already carries its group boxes and labels, so the ring is gone there (`RING.zoom.fade`: absent for the first 8 % of the way from the fit to the lowest zoom, full from 60 %, a smoothstep, a function of the zoom alone, tone-quantised: `ringAlpha`, `ringTone`) and the zoom range grows below the fit (`ringFloorZoom`): the lowest zoom is `RING.zoom.floorBelowFit` (0.5) levels below the fit (the planet 0.71 of its fit size), or lower when the planet and the whole ring (letters' tops and a glyph of room) would not leave `RING.zoom.fitMargin` (12 %) of the smaller half-side free: only on a screen much narrower than 4:3 with a big ring, which no checked viewport is (all five sit at exactly 0.50 below the fit; ring 645 px across at 1440x900 with 127 px to spare, 275 px at 390x844 with 58 px). With the panel open the free width is halved and the floor follows it. No new state: `GlobeRenderer` keeps `fit` (the old `minZoom`, the 0 of the app's zoom, the floor of programmatic framing: `fitZoomFor`, `fitZoomForGroup`, the flights' dip, the start view, the idle rotation's `minZoom`) and `minZoom` (the clamp, the floor); `getFitZoom()` / `getMinZoom()`. **`GlobeViewState.zoom` reports 0 for every zoom below the fit** (it was clamped at 0 already), so the saved view, the hero's world view (`zoom <= 0.03`: the ring's regime is part of it, the hero collapses on the gesture and returns after 8 s at rest, over the ring) and the mobile slide-over's remount behave as before; a view zoomed out into the ring does not survive a remount (it comes back at the fit). Without the ring (`?no-ring`, a debug page, no text) `minZoom` is `fit`, byte for byte as before.

**Layering.** `RingLayer` appends one `div[data-ring][aria-hidden]` (`pointer-events: none`, `overflow: hidden`, the panel's edge mask) to the globe's stage after the WebGL canvas: above the earth and the sky, below the labels' root (`z-index: 1`), the street map's overlay and the hero, so it is behind every place label and box and behind the hero's text, and a press on it reaches the globe's canvas (`ring.mjs layers`). It holds one `<canvas>` (2D) sized to the ring's bounding square, drawn at `min(devicePixelRatio, 2)`, positioned by `transform: translate`. There is no ring text in the page's DOM (nothing for a crawler, a find-in-page or a screen reader to skip; not selectable, which is the point).

**Why a 2D canvas, and an atlas.** DOM text would be 170 elements with a rotation each and as many style writes a frame; the pixel buffer cannot turn a 5 x 7 bitmap font and the box labels left it for device-pixel text for the same reason (`label-text.ts`). A canvas is one element and one clear. But `fillText` under a rotation is drawn as outlines on the main thread: measured in round 1 (headless Chrome, GPU on) at 6 to 13 ms of the main thread's commit per 250-glyph frame, a cost no JS timer shows (the layer's own `performance.now()` said 0.19 ms). So every distinct character is drawn once, upright, into a small atlas per palette tone (24 px em at the device's resolution) and a frame is `drawImage` of one cell per glyph with its rotation (a textured quad, mipmapped down): the text is as crisp as `fillText` (checked on DPR 2 screenshots) and the commit is a fraction of a millisecond.

**Time.** Its own clock, independent of the globe's idle rotation: `RING.degPerSec` 3 clockwise (the globe's idle rotation is 0.2, eastward; one turn in 120 s, one letter's width every 0.9 s: slow enough to read, as in the reference), `RING.direction` 1. It draws on the frames the globe draws anyway (the ring is where the planet is in the same frame, whatever the zoom does) and on its own rAF chain at most every `RING.frameMs` (32: 30 a second) while it is shown, not under reduced motion (a static ring at its start phase: no rAF call, no timer), not in a hidden tab, and not at all while its strength is 0 (the wrapper is `visibility: hidden`, the canvas released to 0 x 0, no timer, no listener but the page's visibility). It never asks for a frame of the globe (`ring.mjs spin`: no tick, no `gl.clear`), and a dt is capped at `RING.maxStepMs`.

**Colour.** One palette tone, no colour of its own and no alpha: the `coast` role (3.1:1 dark / 3.2:1 light on the page, the loudest tone of the MAP itself, never above a coastline); the fade is a staircase of tones up to it (`rampLevel`, "a fade is a tone ramp"). Decorative: not WCAG, `aria-hidden`; the boxes (5.2:1) and the labels (ink) are louder by design.

**Flags.** `?no-ring` (or sessionStorage `no-ring` = `1`) switches it off on any page; a page in debug mode (`?globe-debug`) has it OFF by default (the older checks assert that the lowest zoom is the fit, zero rAF at rest, palette-only pixels), `?ring` (or `ring` = `1`) turns it on there; `RING=1` runs any of the `scripts/globe/*.mjs` checks and the perf scenarios with it on. `__globeDebug.ring()` is `{ shown, level, alpha, repeats, tracking, glyphs, em, baseline, radius, looping, phaseDeg, frames, lastMs, totalMs, canvas, bounds, zoomAlpha, fit, floor, silhouettePx }` (null without a ring; `bounds` is the square of the circle the letters' centres lie on), `__globeDebug.minZoom()` and `fitZoom()` are the whole-globe fit (what every older check sweeps from: `minZoom` keeps its old meaning, "the world view"), `floorZoom()` the lowest zoom the camera can reach (the ring's floor).

**Checks** (`node apps/web/scripts/globe/ring.mjs [flags|rest|fade|fit|layers|spin|reduced|panel|wheel|cost|mobile]`): see the file's header; 82 assertions, all passing on a production build and on the dev server (per viewport: the whole ring inside the screen and below the header, concentric with the planet, the baseline the silhouette plus the gap, a whole number of turns, the seam closing). Unit: `ring.test.ts` (20: placement on the circle, clockwise from twelve, equal steps across the seam, tops outward, scaling with the planet, the layout's closure and bounds, the floor, the fade). Screenshots: `docs/screenshots/ring-{1440,390}-{dark,light}-{1-rest,2-fading,3-zoomed-out,4-zoomed-out-hero-back,5-zoomed-in}.png` (1-rest is the page as loaded: no ring; 4 is the hero back at rest, 8 s after the gesture, over the ring; 2 is dark only).

Not done / not verified: the reference's dashed frame; Safari, iOS and any GPU but the M4's (canvas atlas drawing and `imageSmoothingQuality` were exercised in Chromium only); real low-end phones; the ring does not react to the drag (it is a ring of the planet's disc: the planet turns under it); the hero's text and the right-hand list sit over the ring when the hero is at rest (behind them, dim, as designed: see "Landing hero").

**Highlights in the ring (2026-10-15).** `RING.highlight` (`mode`, `phrases`, `inkWeight`): the first phrase of the line (the name: `ringPhrases` puts it first) is picked out, sparingly. Three options were rendered in both schemes at 1x and 2x (`ringhl-*.png`): (a) `blue`, the caption's BSoD Blue (faded with the ring by the same tone staircase), (b) `ink`, the page's full ink at weight 700 instead of the ring's softened coast tone, (c) `none`. Recommended and the default: **(a) blue**: it ties the ring to the caption's two blue words and is quiet; (b) shouts against the softened ring and reads as a different element. The layout, the geometry and the spacing do not depend on it (a monospace: the advance is the same; `GlyphBuffer.idx` says which phrase a glyph is in); the earlier rounds' "the ring is main's, unchanged" is now "main's, with the name picked out". `__globeDebug.ringHighlight("blue" | "ink" | "none" | null)` switches it. `ring.mjs` asserts geometry and rates, not pixels: there was no byte-identical check to update (it now also accepts the 15 a second repaint of the energy pass).

### Caption on the sphere (2026-10-14, round 3 of the hero; reworked in rounds 4 to 6)

Owner (feedback round 3, item 3): the home screen opens fully zoomed out, the boxes and labels fade out, and "beautiful text orbiting (in 3D) right above the globe" says what to do instead of the how-to line; the planet is lifted so more of it shows. The flat ring of type was NOT to change (it is back exactly as above, text from `SITE`). Rounds 4 to 6 (owner): the rows lie on REAL parallels of the globe, in a literary serif with a few weights, an italic, the code face and a calligraphic script, outlined and extruded, floating clearly above the surface, drawn crisp, calm in colour (greys of the globe's own palette, no accent), four rows in view, two of which take turns. Code: the pure maths in `engine/caption.ts` (`caption.test.ts`), the Three.js objects in `engine/caption-layer.ts`, the tuned numbers in `CAPTION` (`engine/tuning.ts`), the words `siteCaption()` in `app/lib/site.ts` (handed in through `GlobeProps.captionPhrases`), the home screen's side docs/landing.md "Showcase"; browser checks `scripts/home/hero.mjs` and `scripts/globe/ring.mjs`; perf scenario `caption-enter`.

**What it says, and four rows at once.** `GlobeCaption = { sets, fixed }`: `fixed` is the call to action in two rows ("Drag to explore." / "Pick a place.", pinned at the bottom, the same size as every other row: the hierarchy is the weight and the face), `sets` are phrase sets of two rows that take turns above it: (1) "A personal archive," / "organised around places.", (2) the sections' own public names, from `SECTIONS`, two to a row joined by their dots ("Software" in the mono face, "Poetry" in the script), (3) the counts, from the projection's own collections with the sections' nouns ("33 software . 12 articles" / "9 artworks . 5 poems", singular and plural right; left out when the page does not know them). The app wraps its own phrases: a row is one line of type (`splitRuns` is the helper for an app that wants a long phrase cut at its best delimiter). Words are `GlobeCaptionSpan`s: `plain` (Newsreader 500), `heavy` and `heavy-italic` (800), `mono` (JetBrains Mono 700, scaled to the serif's caps), `script` (Imperial Script 400, thickened by `embolden`): the highlight words are "personal archive" (heavy), "explore" and "place" (heavy, upright: the call to action has no italic), "places" (heavy, upright since round 7), "Articles" (heavy), "Artworks" (heavy italic). **Round 7 (owner readability requests):** every row is drawn in CAPITALS (`CAPTION.upper`: the layer upper-cases the runs when it makes the textures, so the app's words, the page's text and the accessible text are untouched) with a little more letter spacing (`CAPTION.tracking`: serif +0.07 em, mono +0.04, script +0.03), one cap height for all rows (`CAPTION.capR` 0.075), and the rows are re-fitted to their chords by the same solver (the whole block shrinks when one would overflow; at the 1440 x 900 load view the scale is 1). Each strip is built ONCE, all of them, ahead of time, from the faces' own ink (`measureText` bounding boxes, so a script that overhangs its advance is not clipped) and the layer keeps them all in the scene: a swap or a tilt is a few uniforms.

**Fixed parallels on the equator, and a camera held in a band (round 7, replacing the per-tilt solver).** In the earth's frame a point of the shell is `P(a, L) = r (cos L sin a, sin L, cos L cos a)` (`a` from the view's central meridian); a row's strip is one latitude band centred on that meridian, turned about the earth's axis by the view's longitude plus the entrance's turn. The vertex shader computes the strip from a handful of uniforms per row (`uBaseLat`, `uHalfSpan`, `uBelow`, `uAbove`: `bandPoint` is the same formula, tested), so one unit strip serves every row. The rows sit on FIXED parallels (`rowLatitudes`, `placeRows`): centred on the equator (the middle of the block's capitals is on latitude 0), `CAPTION.rows.stepCaps` (1.7) cap heights from baseline to baseline at the ends, the middle rows moved so that the baselines are EQUALLY FAR APART ON THE SCREEN at the load view (`START_VIEW`, lon 0 lat 0): measured on the page at 1440 x 900 (see the round's report for the numbers). Nothing recomputes them per camera tilt (the old `solveParallels`, its mirror solve, its glide and tilt clamps are gone, with their tests). One STATIC fit (`fitScale`, on the planet's size and the camera's distance only) shrinks the whole block when a row would not fit, for any camera of the band, (1) the chord of its parallel (`visibleHalfSpan` at +-`band.deg`, `hardSpanDeg`) and (2) the planet's disc on the screen (`CAPTION.maxHalfWidth`: a row that overhangs the limb lands on the page's own text): at 1440 x 900 and at 390 x 844 the fit is 0.78 and 0.80 of `capR` 0.084 (an effective cap of about 0.066 / 0.068 planet radii: **the +10-15 % of type was not reached with the capitals tracked and inside the disc**; the shell's radius does not buy it, the projected width of an arc is the arc's length whatever the radius, so the shell was only lifted from 1.15 to 1.25 for a clearer float and a smaller angular span). **The band.** The rows being on the equator, the caption is shown only while the camera's |latitude| is inside `CAPTION.band.deg` (10) of it. `GlobeRenderer.guardCaption` (every frame, a few comparisons, and when the showcase changes): a showcase that comes back (after the idle delay, a return to rest) with the camera outside the band glides the camera's LATITUDE only (never the longitude, the zoom is the page's; `snapMs` 800, ease-out cubic) to the nearest edge, and `show` (the entrance) is called only when it has landed; a gesture ends the showcase (`dropShowcase`) and drops the glide and the entrance with it; a programmatic `setView` / `flyTo` drops the glide too, and a camera that leaves the band under a shown caption (a flight) FADES it (`hide(now, "fade")`, never a pop) and the caption comes back, entering again, once the camera is in the band (the guard waits for a flight in progress); under reduced motion the latitude is set at once. The page opens at `START_VIEW` (`engine/tuning.ts`: lon 0, lat 0, no tilt: the equator is a horizontal line through the disc's middle; it was lon 15, lat 28), and a saved or given initial view is held inside the band at mount; the glide's target is the band's nearest edge (`band.snapTo`: `edge`, or `equator` to return to exactly 0). Over a pole (|lat| up to the engine's 85): the glide is along the meridian, the longitude does not move, nothing is shown meanwhile. **The bug this closes:** the owner saw the type very small when the caption returned with the camera tilted to the south: the old solver shrank the block to fit the chords of its parallels at that tilt. Checked on the page by `scripts/home/caption-band.mjs` from latitudes -60, -45, -30, +30, +45, +60 and +85 (desktop and phone; also: at +-70 to +-85 degrees of orbit every projected point of every row is inside the second canvas, and the words are opaque: the pixels the caption's canvas changes are the same with and without the globe under them): nothing of the caption shows during the glide, the camera ends inside the band (+-20.00), the longitude and the zoom are unchanged, the cap height in planet radii and the fit are those of the load view, and the rows' on-screen heights are within foreshortening of the load view's (a row at the band's edge is that much foreshortened; `band.deg` is one constant, 5 is the fallback). Where the block sits on the screen follows the equator (inside the band the text is on the equator, which is the intended look), and the dimming's ellipse follows it (computed per frame from the camera's latitude while it is drawn).

**Floating, outlined, thick.** The shell is `CAPTION.radius` (1.25): a row floats 25 % of a planet radius above the surface, so the parallax against the coastlines is visible while the globe spins, and a letter near the limb is behind the planet's own disc (depth-tested against the occluder at 0.998; the camera's near and far planes reach `CAPTION.radius + 0.05` when there is a caption). Each strip is `slices` radial strips stacked from the front face (`r`) down to `r - depth` in ONE shared unit geometry with a per-vertex layer: in the front layer the texture's inside is the face colour and its outline the outline colour, every slice behind is the side colour, so the thickness is visible wherever the face is oblique to the view and nothing at the centre. The texture is drawn once per strip (inside in the green channel, the OUTSIDE outline in the red one, coverage in alpha, mipmaps, anisotropy), the three colours are uniforms: a theme change is three uniform writes and no rebuild. Colours (`captionColors`, all solid, round 9 as revised by the owner: the look is INVERTED): the letters are TEXT-coloured and the contour and the thickness are a MID TONE from the page colour towards the text colour, so a glyph is cut out of the globe's linework by a visible halo that gives it depth. The face is the secondary text tone (`--muted-foreground`: #595959 light, #a3a3a3 dark: 6.8:1 and 7.9:1 against the page; the dark face is a light grey, not white). The contour is the page colour 42 % (light) / 37 % (dark) of the way to the text colour (`outlineMix`): 1.94:1 and 1.99:1 against the page, 3.5:1 and 4.0:1 against the face (clearly less strong than the letters); the thickness a step closer to the page (34 % / 30 %: 1.7:1). BSoD Blue is the caption's ONE palette, as a muted stone-like lapis (a solid mix: 60 % palette blue and 40 % the text tone, #246192 light 6.3:1, #84adda dark 8.5:1 against the page), used sparingly (`CaptionRun.accent: "blue"`): at most two words on screen, set 1's "personal archive" and "places" and, in the counts set, the first numeral of each row; the call to action and the sections keep the face colour and their weight. The whole LETTER of a tinted word is one colour: its contour and its thickness derive from ITS face by the same shares as the neutral ones from the neutral face (`accentOutline`, `accentSide`; the texture's blue channel marks the letter and the shader picks the three tones: no per-vertex or per-run uniform, the cost is flat). The constants are in `CAPTION.colors.accent`, copied from feat/palettes (PR #289: `--palette-blue-700` and `-400` mixed into the text tone). Colour is never the only carrier: those words are also heavy. Texture channels: red is the outline, green the inside, blue the inside of an accent word. The strips are drawn from BOTH sides (`DoubleSide`): past the shell's horizon a row is seen from behind (mirrored, as a ring of type round a planet is), curls back towards the planet and goes behind its body; a strip is hidden whole only when its nearest edge is behind the planet's body (`lineHidden`, `occludedAngle`).

**Crisp, in a canvas of its own, above the hero's plate.** A second `WebGLRenderer` draws the caption onto a canvas laid over the globe's (same camera, same rectangle, the same edge mask, the device's resolution up to 2, MSAA) with the planet in it as a depth-only sphere, into a window round the planet only; hidden when no row is in view. The canvas is above the hero (z-index 15: the hero is 10, the credits 20, the panel 30, the header 40) because the hero's text plate is a wash over the lower half of the planet and under it the lower rows faded to nothing (a gradient the owner took for a shading of the letters); it takes no pointer events. The pixel pass of the first version (the globe's own art-pixel resolution) is gone: an outlined serif is not readable there. A page that cannot get a second WebGL context has no caption and no showcase (the page is the one without it: boxes and the how-to line).

**The dimming behind the words (round 7).** A faint "reverse shadow" in the page's colour lowers the globe's own linework where the words sit. It is drawn in the caption's canvas, after the depth-only planet and before the letters (so only over the planet: the ring, the labels and the page are never touched, and it fades, enters and swaps with the words because it is part of the same canvas and scaled by the rows' presence, `dimPresence(theta, start)`). `CAPTION.dim.mode`: **`ellipse`** (default) one quad in the camera's tangent space (a point's `(x, y) / -z`, which `shellXY` also returns), centred on the block's middle, sized to the ink's box times `sqrt 2 (1 + reach)`, a `(1 - smoothstep(r))^2` falloff, clipped by the silhouette's tangent radius `1 / sqrt(d^2 - 1)`, alpha 0.55 at the middle (light and dark), strength scaled by the mean presence of the rows; **`bands`** one soft band per row on its own parallel at the surface (the same unit strip, widened, max-blended so bands never add), turned with the row; **`none`**. Measured in screenshots (light and dark, three views: 35 N, the equator, 72 S over the Antarctic coast): `none` leaves coastlines running through the letters (Africa's through "PLACE", the Antarctic peninsula through "DRAG TO"); `ellipse` clears them behind the whole block with one smooth shape and no visible edge; `bands` clears the rows but leaves the gaps between them as busy as before and each row's edge reads as a stripe, so the ellipse is the default. Cost: one quad and no extra texture (a fragment shader of a few operations over the ellipse's own box: not measured on a real GPU); on the CPU a `renderNow` with the caption in view is 0.22 ms with it against 0.215 ms before round 7 (1440 x 900, the dev machine, 200 frames: inside the noise). `__globeDebug.captionDim("ellipse" | "bands" | "none" | null)` switches it for captures.

**The window of the second canvas** is a square round the planet that must contain the SHELL's silhouette, not a fixed share of the planet's: `shellWindowFactor(d)` (the shell's tangent radius over the planet's, 1.25 at the shell's radius) times 1.03. It was a fixed 1.2 planet radii, which was enough while the shell floated at 1.15 and cut the rows by a vertical edge near the limb once it floated at 1.25 (the owner's report); the check is geometric (`caption-band.mjs`: at +-70, 78 and 85 degrees every projected point of every row is inside the canvas's rectangle) and a unit test.

**Root cause of the rows "disappearing" at the sides of the orbit (round 8).** Not the canvas (the window was a real, separate bug, above). With the rows on the EQUATOR and the shell at 1.25, a row at 60 to 85 degrees of longitude is 1.1 to 1.25 planet radii from the centre on the screen, OUTSIDE the planet's disc, and the strips were drawn from the front only (`FrontSide`) and hidden whole at the shell's horizon (`acos(r / d)`, 84.5 degrees): the row therefore vanished in the open, beside the planet, at the shell's silhouette. In the round-2 build (8468110) the rows were on parallels near 28 degrees north and the shell at 1.15: the ends of a row curled in towards the disc and went behind the planet's body before the horizon cut them, which read as going round it. Fix: `DoubleSide` strips and `lineHidden` by `occludedAngle(d)` (the central angle at which the shell's projection falls back to the disc's edge: 125 degrees at the lowest zoom): past the horizon the row is seen from behind (its glyphs mirrored, the thickness from the side), curls back over the page towards the planet and goes behind it. `entranceStart` uses the same angle (a row starts fully behind the body).
