Frame strips: `orbit-compare.png` (old build left, the broken build right) and `orbit-new-after.png` (60, 85, 105, 115, 130 and 145 degrees, after) in the scratchpad; the guard is `caption-band.mjs`: glyph pixels of the rows alone from 0 to 180 degrees fall away without a cliff (desktop: 74 435 px at 0, 28 711 at 80, 26 659 at 110, 7 820 at 140, 2 249 at 150, 0 from 160; phone: 47 915 at 0, 13 332 at 120, 337 at 170, 0 at 180), a row is still drawn at 100 and 110 degrees, and it ends behind the body.

**The thickness is one solid colour** (round 7): `slices` 28 (it was 6, whose gaps showed the globe between the slices when a row was oblique), all opaque in the side colour, a step of 0.17 CSS px at the largest planet; the side is a solid colour from the palette's mix (`sideMix` per scheme: 0.3 light, 0.7 dark: lighter than before on both pages, the outline unchanged); face, outline and side are all alpha 1: opacity is used only for the fade of the whole canvas and for the dimming (which is meant to be translucent).

**The thickness (round 9), three options** rendered light and dark at 1x and 2x over busy coastlines (`look-*.png`): (a) `page`, the exact page colour: a perfect cut-out, but the thickness is invisible against the page and only the missing globe lines say it is there; (b) `mix`, 8 % toward the text colour: a quiet but readable spine in both schemes (recommended and implemented); (c) `edge`, page colour with a thin text-coloured line on the far two slices: the depth reads most, but it doubles every stroke and is busy on small type. `__globeDebug.captionSide("page" | "mix" | "edge" | null)` switches it. **The dimming ellipse** was re-checked with and without: with page-coloured contours it still lowers the linework in the gaps between letters and rows (the contour only cuts it round each glyph), so it stays at its tuned alpha; with it off the lines run between the words and the block reads busier.

**The idle settle (round 9).** Before the page holds still for inactivity (`ENERGY.idleFreezeMs`, 30 s) every row eases to the middle of the globe, fully visible, in `CAPTION.park.parkMs` (800, a smoothstep: no velocity at either end, from wherever the row is: mid-roll, mid-entrance, mid-exit or behind the planet), all of them showing the phrase set a row in view had (so the pair is never mixed), the call-to-action rows included; only then are the roll, the ring and the idle rotation stopped (`CaptionMotion.beginPark`, `GlobeRenderer.settleThenHold`). An input during the settle cancels it (`unpark`: the rows blend from where they are into the roll, which goes on from its middle, in `resumeMs` 600); an input after it does the same. `caption-band.mjs` checks the held state (four rows in view, every turn within 0.01 degrees of 0, one phrase set) and `caption.test.ts` the motion (any start moment, either side, no pop, no turn back).

**Smooth first, slices on demand and the quality governor (rounds 9 and 10, "Hitches").** The owner saw the roll stutter at the slow speed and the exits drop frames: the energy pass had put the caption's loop on a 20 a second TIMER (and the ring's on 15), whose period against the display's vsync gives uneven intervals (measured: 3 or 4 display frames between two draws, 7 % of them 4: visible judder) and 1.7 px steps at 2.9 degrees a second. Since round 10 the caption AND the ring are drawn on EVERY display frame while they move, by `requestAnimationFrame`, with positions that are a function of the frame's own timestamp (`CaptionMotion.tick`, never a per-frame step); `?caption-fps` shows what it is doing on a given machine. The energy comes from elsewhere: the idle hold, the slices on demand, the band-sized canvas, the paced idle rotation (the globe moves one art pixel every 1.35 s), the visibility pauses and the low-power hint. The thickness is `CAPTION.slices` (28) slices, each a full pass over the glyphs; at rest a row is within a few degrees of the middle and its side is edge-on, a fraction of a pixel wide, so a strip is drawn with only as many slices as its side needs (`CaptionLayer.slicesFor`: one per `CAPTION.sliceStepPx`, 0.8 px, of the thickness projected at the row's far end, quantised to 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 20, 28 and all made when the strips are built: about 6 at rest, all 28 only at the orbit's sides). The textures of every phrase set are uploaded when they are built (`initTexture`), so no upload lands in an exit. Positions are never snapped: the uniforms are fractional, the textures LINEAR with mipmaps and anisotropy, and the canvas has no CSS transform. `engine/quality.ts` (`CAPTION_LOAD`; named so because `density.test.ts` forbids a governor of the globe's pixel density, which this never touches) steps the caption down only on a REAL overload: the display's period is DERIVED (the median of the first 30 frame deltas: 16.7 ms at 60 Hz, 8.3 at 120, 6.9 at 144), a frame is late when its delta is over 1.5 periods (or its draw over 0.6 of one), and the rung changes when more than a fifth of the last 2 s are late (jitter never moves it); the ladder is slices 28, 14, 8, then the canvas's pixel ratio 1.5, 1, and only last every second display frame (exactly 30 a second at 60 Hz, aligned to the display); one rung back up after 4 s with at most a twentieth late. A delta over 250 ms (a throttled background tab, a window that lost focus) is not a sample and restarts the window; a blurred or hidden page resets it. Numbers: docs/performance.md, "Hitches".

**Alpha, and why the thickness was translucent (root cause, round 8).** The strips write premultiplied `(col * a, a)` and are BLENDED over one another (`CustomBlending`, one / one-minus-source-alpha), drawn back to front, with no alpha-to-coverage. The first versions used alpha-to-coverage: it turns the fragment's alpha into a coverage mask but ALSO stores that alpha in every sample it covers, and the visible wall of the extrusion is, at each pixel, the EDGE texel of the frontmost slice that covers it (a point of the wall is inside the slices behind and just inside the frontmost one's outline): so each wall pixel carried the partial alpha of an edge texel (0.7 measured over magenta) and the globe's graticule and coastline showed through the thickness, in both themes. Blending over keeps a pixel exactly opaque wherever anything opaque is behind it (interior and wall), and only the silhouette's own edge is partial. Checked by `scripts/home/caption-band.mjs` on a desktop and a phone, dark and light: the caption's canvas over magenta has no tinted interior pixel (140 of 80 467 on desktop, all of them the wall's antialiased silhouette) and the words are the same colour with and without the globe under them (6 of 100 710 interior pixels differ).

**Faces (round 9).** The serif is Old Standard TT (400 and 700, a file each, self-hosted next to its OFL licence: see design-tokens.md, "Caption"); JetBrains Mono is the page's own (declared in app.css, no file added); the script is Imperial Script (SIL OFL 1.1, `public/fonts/imperial-script-1.010-latin-400.woff2`, 19.7 kB). The serif and the script are added with the `FontFace` API only when a caption has a word in it (not in the stylesheet); the texture is built only after every face is there (a face that fails or does not arrive in 3 s is replaced by its fallback style, never kept as a negative result). The script was chosen earlier from nine calligraphic scripts in the real outlined and extruded render (the owner asked for something ornate: Imperial Script, a copperplate with swashed capitals). The serif was changed because Newsreader's heavy capitals, drawn by a canvas at the largest optical size, have slanted serifs on L, E and C; eight classic families were rendered in the real style (Cinzel, Playfair Display SC, Bodoni Moda, Cormorant SC, Old Standard TT, Libre Caslon Text, Marcellus, Newsreader at another optical size) and Old Standard TT, a 19th-century Scotch-Roman, fits the 19th-century shop-front and cinema lettering the owner asked for.

**Cycling and the roll (round 8).** A row no longer sits still after it has come in. Each row follows ONE continuous trajectory (`rowX`, `engine/caption.ts`; `CAPTION.cycle`, `CAPTION.enter`, `CAPTION.drift`): it enters (`enter.ms` 900, a cubic Hermite from behind the planet that lands at `+amp` with the roll's own velocity: no ease to rest), ROLLS through the central meridian at a constant speed for `cycle.dwellMs` (5.5 s) from `amp` before it to `amp` after it (`drift.ampDeg` 8: 16 degrees of longitude in 5.5 s, 2.9 degrees a second), then accelerates and leaves round the orbit and behind the limb in `cycle.exitMs` (480, an ease-in that starts at the roll's velocity), and the next phrase set comes in the same way on the same parallel. The direction is the side the row came in from (drawn per row and per cycle from the session's seed, `CaptionMotion.sideAt`): a row that came in from the west rolls east through the centre and leaves on the east. The entrance's stagger (`enter.staggerMs` 450) carries into the roll and the exit, so the rows are at different phases and the block shears gently (row 1 is exactly row 0 450 ms later; the parallels, and so the gaps, are those of a still block: the roll is along longitude only). Seams: position and velocity are continuous at entrance to roll and at roll to exit (unit test: one-sided slopes at each seam equal the roll's velocity within 1 %), the roll reaches `-amp` exactly at the end of the dwell, and a row is monotone (never turns back). The rows that stay (the call to action) cannot leave: they land at the centre with the pendulum's own speed and swing about it, `-amp sin(pi t / dwell)`, period twice the roll's, position and velocity continuous with their entrance, never leaving. There is no swap object and no timer any more: a row's phrase set is the cycle it is in modulo the sets (`cycleOf`), so a swap is simply each row, 450 ms apart, finishing its roll. The caption's own clock (`CaptionMotion.tick`) advances only when the caption is synced and by at most `drift.maxStepMs` (64) a frame, so it stands still while nothing is drawn (a hidden tab, the street map, a fade) and a stalled frame cannot make a row jump; `show()` starts it again from 0 (every showing begins with set 1). Reduced motion: still, at the centre, set 1 (no roll, no turns). An interaction FADES the canvas with the rows frozen where they were (the clock stands still in a fade) and tracking the camera, as before. The orbit exit (`hide(now, "orbit")`) is the old ease-in from rest and is not velocity-matched to the roll (it is the kept, unused path). **Frame cost.** The roll breaks "nothing runs at rest": the caption has its own loop (`GlobeRenderer.captionTick`), on `requestAnimationFrame` at the display's rate (round 10; it was a 30 and then a 20 a second timer), that draws NOTHING but the caption's canvas (`sync` and the crisp pass; the globe's canvas keeps its last frame, no labels, no tree, no ring): it stands down for a pending full frame (which draws the caption too), and stops entirely while hidden, lost, suspended by the street map, in a fade or not visible. The entrance and the orbit exit still ask for full frames at the display rate. Numbers: see docs/performance.md. All sets' textures exist from the start: nothing is allocated or uploaded at a swap. A phrase set is shown only when the caption is wanted (the showcase is on: not collapsed, not away, not while the user interacts). The page's hero text is the complete static copy (the caption is for the eyes; it is not in the accessibility tree and nothing is announced).

**The entrance.** Each row starts hidden behind the planet on a side of its own, drawn at random per entrance from a seed of the session (`entranceSides(seed, n, rows)`: west or east, it may repeat; deterministic for a seed, `captionSeed(n)` sets it for captures and checks), `enter.fromDeg` or, for a long line, as far as its parallel and the farthest camera the page has need to be wholly beyond the shell's horizon (`entranceStart(line, viewLat, d)`); it travels round the sphere to its place with `easeOutExpo` over `enter.ms` (900), row i `staggerMs` (450) after row i - 1 and, on load, `loadDelayMs` (250) after the first frame (the page's own fade-in goes first). The whole entrance takes `loadDelayMs + enter.ms + 3 * staggerMs` = 2500 ms from the first frame (it was 2130; the stagger is what the owner asked for), and the longest span with nothing at all on the globe is the load delay (250 ms, plus the faces and textures) since the first row is visible within a frame of its start. It plays on load and every time the showcase returns to rest. It is a function of time (`rowX(tau, start)`, tested; the clock's step is capped): the same at 30 and 144 frames a second. Reduced motion: no entrance, the final composition, instantly. The entrance asks for full frames while it is on; after it the roll has its own capped loop (above): **the caption costs a 30 a second caption-only frame at rest while it rolls, and nothing while hidden**.

**Leaving: a fade.** When the showcase ends (a gesture) the caption's canvas fades out (`CAPTION.fadeMs`, 180: a CSS opacity transition run by the compositor). Nothing else changes: the rows go on following the camera exactly as at rest (the same uniforms, one render a frame the globe draws anyway), so under a zoom the words grow with the planet (their width goes with the silhouette's radius; the shell is nearer the camera than the surface, so a big zoom gives the words a few percent more: checked, `hero.mjs`), the pointer's position and a two-finger pinch included, and under a drag they turn and tilt with the earth (the rows are left on the parallels they were on: they are part of the earth). When the fade is over nothing of the caption runs: no placement (the parallels are solved again by the first frame of the next showing), no render call, no depth pass, and the renderer returns before touching the camera's view offset (`CaptionLayer.visible`). Reduced motion: gone at once. The orbit exit is kept and tested (`CaptionMotion.leave(now, "orbit")`, `CaptionLayer.hide(now, "orbit")`, `swapExitAngle`): the rows go on behind the planet the same way; the swap between phrase sets uses it at rest.

**The flat ring gives way to the boxes.** With the showcase (the home page) the ring is shown at rest in the world view, exactly as on main, and the moment the boxes and labels come back (a gesture) it leaves with them: its presence is `ringAlpha(zoom) * (1 - overlayAlpha)`, where `LodTree.overlayAlpha` is one timed value (the same primitive, `FADE_MS` and ease as every node's own opacity, started by the same `setSuppressed` call, run by the same clock), so the ring and the labels cross-fade in the same frames, the tone ramp steps the ring down like any other fade, and when the showcase returns (the idle delay at the world view) the ring comes back as they leave. A panel closing or the hero collapsing cannot flash it: it is zero whenever the overlay is there, and by zoom whenever the camera is in. Without the showcase (a debug page) the ring is a function of the zoom alone, as it was. Reduced motion: at once.

**The showcase** (`GlobeRenderer.setShowcase`, `GlobeProps.showcase`; docs/landing.md "Showcase"). On: `LodTree.setSuppressed(true)` sends every node's target OFF (the cut is untouched, nothing is re-planned), the caption enters, and the camera comes back to the lowest zoom when it is within the idle rotation's slack of the fit. The hidden boxes take no pick (a node that is off is not a target; `pickAt` answers null while it is on) and the press that ends it (the engine's own capture listener, in the frame of the gesture) selects nothing. Off: the other way round. A debug page (`?globe-debug`) has none of it unless `?showcase`; `?no-ring` switches the ring AND the caption off (and the page opens at the fit, with its boxes).

**Lift** (item 11 of the renderer's list above): the planet's centre goes up by what clears the name, as far as the room above allows, the ring's top included (its flat outer edge, as `ringFloorZoom` has it).

**Debug.** `__globeDebug.caption()` is `{ built, phase, opacity, scale, viewLat, parallelsFor (always 0: the rows are fixed), baselines, band: { shown, snapping, deg }, lines: [{ text, parent, theta, visible, halfSpanDeg, baseLat, capR, side, styles }], cycle: { sets, current, swapping, waiting, paused, swaps }, texture, colors, showcase, overlayHidden, animating, zoom, fit, floor }` (null without a caption), `showcase(on)` drives the showcase as the hero does, `pinCaption(ms)` holds the entrance at `ms`, `pinCaptionAngle(deg)` holds every line at an angle, `captionRadius(r)` lays the shell on another radius (1.001: on the surface, the alignment check), `captionSeed(n)` the sides, `captionSwap()` the next swap now, `captionExit("fade" | "orbit")` the leaving (the measurements compare them), `captionProbe()` the real layout on the screen (each row's top and bottom edge through the camera, the planet's centre and radius), `captionExtent()` the widest line's ends and the radius, `inset()` also reports `lift`, `liftTarget`, `liftBuf` and `cover`. `ring()` is the ring's (with `presence`, `overlay`, `zoomAlpha`).

**Robustness.** No frame is asked for while the strips do not exist (a face is still coming, or a build failed: the caption then simply is not there); an entrance waits for the first frame; the textures are made once (their key is the words and the faces: the planet's size, the art cell, the camera's distance and the shell's radius only RE-PLAN the parallels and the letters' floor, `replan`; a window resize no longer rebuilds them); the second canvas's size, position and mask are written only when they change; while the street map owns the view the caption settles (`settle`: a swap is finished, the entrance is in place) so nothing keeps the frame loop alive; a lost context of the globe hides the caption canvas, and the caption canvas's own restored context asks for a frame; a build that is no longer wanted is cancelled; textures and per-strip materials are freed on every rebuild and the unit strip on dispose; a leaving that begins before a row has arrived leaves it behind the planet; an entrance that cuts a leaving short goes on from where each line is; `onOrbit` is false while the context is lost (the hero's how-to line shows). The cut of the hierarchy shares the planner's bleed clamp (`holdBleedOnScreen`), so a box within `bleed` of the canvas's edge keeps its slots above its corners; the planner's slide back onto the screen is not shared (the calibrated crowding sweeps of `open.test.ts` go over their limits with it).

**Not verified.** Safari and iOS (a second WebGL context, blending into a premultiplied canvas, texture sampling with a LOD bias, a CSS opacity transition on a WebGL canvas, `FontFace`), real phones and a real GPU (every number is a headless Chrome with the GPU of the development machine), and a screen reader (the caption is pixels in a canvas: the hero's how-to line stays in the page as text).

### The cinematic interlude (easter egg, 2026-10-22, issue #293)

Once in a long while, for a visitor who has been here before, the caption does what a studio logo does: every line leaves round the orbit, the globe is empty for a beat, and ONE big word, "UNIVERSAL" (the heavy serif, in the caption's own grey-white), on a higher orbit than the rows' and at least twice their cap height, centred on the equator, rises out of the planet's shadow from the west, slowly, settles for a long time, rolls gently, and goes on round the far side to the east, while the globe itself pulls back (see below); then the normal showing begins again from its first phrase set. Code: `engine/cinematic.ts` (the tuning `CINEMATIC`, the plan, the page's gate), `engine/caption.ts` (`CinematicPlan`, `EggShape`, `eggX`, the `egg` phase of `CaptionMotion`), `engine/caption-layer.ts` (the big row's strip, placement, dimming and canvas window), `engine/visit.ts` (what the device remembers), `engine/cinematic.test.ts`.

- **Sequence** (`CINEMATIC.shape`, about 21 s in all, made to the sound's length (2026-10-23: slower to come in, 10.8 s, and to go, 1.6 s)): the rows leave by the orbit exit's own path (`leavingAngle`, 0.5 s), the stage is empty for 0.4 s, the word comes in over 4.6 s (`rowX` with its own shape: a gentler start, `ease` 1 against 2.2, and the roll's own speed at its end), holds 2.4 s rolling through 2.5 degrees, and leaves in 0.65 s. Then `enter` runs the regular entrance (the globe's own loop, as at load) and `onResume` puts the phrase sets back at the first. The globe's pull-back and lean last as long as the word is on the stage and are given back only AFTER it has gone (`eggMs` includes the `zoomOutMs` that follow its exit).
- **When it begins**: the second time the first row would leave (`CINEMATIC.cycles`, 13 s into the showing); it is about 21 s long, so the renderer's idle hold (`ENERGY.idleFreezeMs`, 30 s) waits for an interlude under way (`Renderer.freezeWhenIdle`). The plan is a pure function of the session's seed (`planCinematic`).
- **Who sees it** (all of them): a visitor who has been here before (`engine/visit.ts`: `localStorage` `site-seen`, noted by `root.tsx` through `noteVisit` on every page, and `sessionStorage` `site-visit`, what this session found, so that a reload does not turn a first visit into a return; one bit on the device, nothing is sent; storage that throws means no return and no egg), not under reduced motion (`setReduced(true)` drops the plan), not on a debug page, once per browser session (`sessionStorage` `site-egg`, written when it begins), and the seeded draw (`CINEMATIC.chance`, 12 % of eligible sessions). A visitor can also ask for it at any time, the search palette's hidden entry "I like surprises" (see the last entry of this section); the `?cinematic` flag, the U key and `pinFlight` of the development builds are gone.
- **The globe pulls back** (`EggShape.zoomLevels` 0.7: to 62 % of its size; `zoomInMs` 2.2 s, `zoomOutMs` 2 s, smoothsteps; `CaptionMotion.eggZoom` is the timeline's value, 0 at the start and at the end): the word is as big as the studio's wordmark against its globe, which is far bigger than the page's other type, so the planet, the ring and the word all shrink together and the proportion is kept. The renderer subtracts `CaptionLayer.zoomOutLevels()` from the zoom it DRAWS with (`drawView()`, the seam the camera, the caption's window, the leg icons and the ring's radius all read): the camera's own zoom, the page's floor and the view the app knows are untouched. A cut-short interlude (a gesture) gives the size back at a fixed pace (`zoomCutMs`, 0.7 s) on the globe's own frames.
- **Cost**: none at rest, and about 21 s of the globe's own frames while it lasts (not the caption's 30 a second loop: the planet moves, so `CaptionMotion.animating` is true in the `egg` phase and `drifting` is not), the same kind of cost as an entrance, once per session at most and in 12 % of returning visits. The energy pass's hold freezes it where it is (the clock stands still, nothing begins while frozen, `animating` is false); the idle park leaves it alone (`park` refuses while it is under way). A gesture cuts it short like any showing's end: the rows are gone, the word fades where it is, the plan is spent. The strip's texture is made only when a plan exists, and the second canvas's window includes the word's extent (`extentTan`).
- **The word's size and place** (`placeEgg`, tuned by `CINEMATIC.radius`, `capScale`, `maxCapScale`, `fill`, `maxHalfScreen`): a planet filling the picture made a word of twice the rows' cap on the rows' own shell (1.25) look small, and shrinking the planet would have changed the whole landing layout, so the word has a shell of its own, `radius` 2: it floats well in front of the planet and its ends curve away round the limb like the studio's wordmark (a shell at 1.6 was cut off by the front-face limit at about 1.6 silhouette radii wide; the higher one reaches the width asked for), and it is as large as its chord allows (never under twice the rows' cap, never over `maxCapScale` times it), reaching `fill` (1.85, as the studio's wordmark is about 1.8 times as wide as its globe) of the planet's silhouette radius at most on the screen, and `maxHalfScreen` (47 %) of the stage's width (a phone's planet is nearly as wide as the screen). The rows' limit is 1.04; the word is on the equator, level with none of the page's text, so it may overhang the disc's sides. On a very small planet it falls back to twice the cap. Everything that depends on the shell's radius takes it as a parameter (`spanOf`, `lineOf`, `lineHidden`, `entranceStart`, the strip's `uR`, `extentTan`, the ellipse); the camera's near and far planes and the second canvas's window are sized to the largest shell (`CaptionLayer.maxRadius`: the nearest part of a higher shell was clipped by planes made for 1.25 until then). The dimming's ellipse follows the word (`updateEllipse(lat, egg)`).
- **The lean, the letters and the sound (2026-10-23, owner: "mimic the actual animation")**: while the globe is pulled back it also LEANS to the left (`CINEMATIC.tiltDeg`, 12 degrees, the north pole toward the left of the screen), with the same eased value as the pull-back (`CaptionLayer.rollRad` = `zoomK` x tilt). The roll is part of the drawn view (`ViewState.roll`, applied in `viewBasis` by turning the screen's right and up about the line of sight), so the planet, the words and the stars lean together and nothing else has to know; a view the visitor moves has none. The word is set in the page's display sans at its heaviest (`CAPTION_STYLES.black`: Bricolage Grotesque at weight 640, declared in app.css, so no new file; 800 read too heavy and soft; 1.079 times the serif's size so the caps agree; tracked wide), WHITE on either page (`CINEMATIC.face`), OUTLINED IN BSoD BLUE (`CINEMATIC.outline`: the site's accent, #0078d7 on the light page, a lighter step on the dark; gold first), with SHARP corners (its texture is drawn with a mitred join: the rows' round join rounds every corner of a glyph by half the outline's width, which at the word's size is a lot) on a flat spine only a step from the page (`sideColor`; `sideShade` is 1: a spine that darkens with depth, tried first, was far too high in contrast) and a thickness of 0.3 cap heights (`depthCaps`; the rows' is a fifth of one; first tried at 0.5, too much). The slices of the spine have hard edges (`uHardSide`: the soft edges of 28 stacked slices read as stripes at a steep angle). Every one of its materials' uniforms is its own (`eggFace`, `eggOutline`, `eggSide`, `eggShade`, and `uDepth` set when the word is placed), and its slices have their own budget (`CINEMATIC.slices` up to 160, one per `sliceStepDevicePx` device pixel of the thickness's length on the screen, with `sliceSafety` 2 for the perspective of its higher orbit; the strips of 40 to 160 slices are made only when an interlude is planned). The count is the worst case over the WHOLE span of the word (`wide` in `slicesFor`): the first version looked at the far end only, which at the limb is behind the planet and edge-on, and gave the word 16 slices where it needed over a hundred (the rings; `__globeDebug.caption().egg.slices` shows the count). The sound is the owner's own parody of the studio's intro, cut to 20.8 s, from where its sound begins to its end (`public/audio/universal-parody.mp3`, 250 KB), played at `SOUND.volume` 0.22 from the top of the file (its loudest hit, 11.7 s in, falls when the word lands: 0.5 + 0.4 + 10.8 s, the timeline is made to it). It is fetched into a plain audio element when the interlude is PLANNED (`prepareSound`), so that starting it is a `play()` (an `AudioContext` was tried first: opening an audio device is a 150 ms stall on the main thread, in the frame the interlude begins in). It is asked to play whatever the page has seen (a page nobody has touched is usually refused, quietly: with `?cinematic` the console says so, and the key U, which plays the interlude NOW at any time and lets the browser play the sound without leaving the presentation as a click does, is the way to test it), with the tab in front, and unless `?mute` or sessionStorage `cinematic-mute` = `1`; it fades in and out, and fades at once when the interlude is cut short or the globe goes away. The page's CSP allows it (`media-src 'self'`).
- **Frames while it runs (2026-10-23, the lag the owner saw: 5 a second, from the moment the roll's loop ended to the end of the interlude)**: the roll has its own rAF loop, which ends when the interlude begins (`drifting` is the rest phase's); the interlude is then the globe's own frames (the pull-back and the lean are the view's), and those went through `keepGoing(false)`, which, while the idle rotation is turning (always, on the home screen), chains frames at the rotation's pace (`ENERGY.spinFrameMs`, 100 ms: 10 a second at best, and every wake waited for a timer). Nothing woke the loop at the begin either. `pacedByRotation` (`idle-spin.ts`) is now false while the caption animates, so those frames are forced at the display's rate, and the layer asks for a frame when the interlude begins (`onCinematic`). Measured on a forced interlude (the clock was not advancing at all before, in headless): the pull-back reaches 0.70, the word crosses, and the globe draws 60 frames a second throughout, also at a 4x CPU slowdown; at rest the rotation still idles at 8 to 10 a second. A window that loses focus is held only after `ENERGY.blurGraceMs` (30 s, `engine/grace.ts`): the interlude plays through a click into another window and back.
- **Decorative only**: the caption canvas is hidden from assistive technology and the real copy is in the page; the egg changes no layout.

### Framing and cut checks (`scripts/globe/framing.mjs`)

`BASE_URL=http://localhost:5173 [SHOTS=1] node apps/web/scripts/globe/framing.mjs [reload|flight|cut|panel|reduced|mobile]`
These browser checks (this one and the street ones) look up the demo fixture's places by slug (`lisbon`, `paris`, ...): serve the app with `pnpm dev:demo`, not `pnpm dev` (which shows your preview or the empty published state).
(real OpenFreeMap tiles; `SHOTS=1` writes `docs/screenshots/framing-<slug>-{light,dark,mobile}.png`). `reload`: reload of
`/locations/{lisbon,paris,ho-chi-minh-city}`, light and dark, camera identical on every animation frame from the first,
one cut; `flight`: a list selection ends at the reload view; `cut`: through a flight in and back out, per frame the
street root and the globe labels are only ever 0 or 1, never both and never neither, Three.js suspended whenever the
street map shows; `panel`: framing with the panel open is wider by log2(900/720); `reduced`; `mobile`.

### Handover checks (`scripts/globe/handover-*.mjs`)

Need Chrome for Testing and the app on `BASE_URL` (default :5175) with `CATALYST_TILES_FALLBACK_URL=http://127.0.0.1:5240/places.pmtiles`
(`pnpm dev` with that variable, or a build started with `NODE_ENV=development`: production refuses plain-http tile
URLs); the scripts start the local PMTiles server themselves. The fallback source is pinned by default (deterministic,
offline); `SOURCE=auto` runs the real chain (OpenFreeMap).

The browser checks that zoom with the wheel declare what `page.mouse.wheel` emulates (`input-kind`, "Input model"); `node apps/web/scripts/input/check.mjs` tests the classification itself.

```
node apps/web/scripts/globe/handover-drills.mjs [direct wheel click failover retreat capped recover reduced keyboard mobile-slideover]
node apps/web/scripts/globe/handover-perf.mjs  flight|frames|idle|leaks|all [desktop|mobile]
OUT_DIR=/tmp/shots node apps/web/scripts/globe/handover-shots.mjs dissolve|seam|flight [desktop|mobile]
```

`?globe-debug` (or `sessionStorage["globe-debug"]`) also exposes `window.__handoverDebug` (both renderers, dissolve,
overlay owner, limit, registration error, `forceBlend`, `fly`); `street-opts` (JSON in the URL or sessionStorage)
passes options to the street engine; `?no-street` runs the globe alone (the older Three.js-only scripts below do).

### Browser checks (`scripts/globe/`)

Need a Chrome binary (Playwright "Chrome for Testing") and the production server running with demo content:

```
pnpm --filter @catalyst/web build
CATALYST_CONTENT=demo PORT=5174 pnpm --filter @catalyst/web start
CHROME_PATH=/path/to/chrome OUT_DIR=/tmp/shots node apps/web/scripts/globe/check.mjs    # desktop behaviours (JSON)
CHROME_PATH=/path/to/chrome OUT_DIR=/tmp/shots node apps/web/scripts/globe/mobile.mjs   # 390x844@3x touch, unmount/restore, 20 cycles
CHROME_PATH=/path/to/chrome node apps/web/scripts/globe/bench.mjs desktop|mobile [seconds] [panel]
CHROME_PATH=/path/to/chrome node apps/web/scripts/globe/markers.mjs [quick]              # markers whole-or-absent regression (exit 1 on failure)
```

These Three.js-only scripts run with the street map off (`no-street`). `BASE_URL` overrides `http://localhost:5174`. The places list is visually hidden, so the scripts open places like a
keyboard user (`page.focus` on `[data-place-link]` + Enter). `bench.mjs ... panel` opens `/locations/kyoto` with the
panel open. `check.mjs` also covers the inset (direct load, animation vs
panel position, picking, close, reduced motion) and the ocean colour in both schemes. The scripts install their instrumentation before app code runs:
a rAF call counter, WebGL context created/lost counters and a counter on `gl.clear` (Three issues one per frame).

## Detection boxes and pixel text (2026-10-06)

Owner: the map should read like a classifier's output. EVERYTHING is a rectangle, there are no dot markers, and the text is
pixel type on the same grid as the map. Code: `app/globe/engine/{lod-tree,box-scene,pixel-labels,pixel-buffer,country-names,group-square,node-screen}.ts`
and `engine/pixel-font/`, shared by the Three.js globe and the street map's `overlay/hud-layer.ts` (one `BoxScene` class, so
both draw exactly the same thing on both sides of the handover).

**Geometry** (`LodTree`, pure, unit tested). A place is the bounding box of its extent. With a published `bbox` it is that
box (`GlobePlace.bbox`): the box's own width and height in km at the local scale of the unified camera, centred on the box's
centre, not on the recorded point (a city's rectangle is the whole city, wherever the entry's point is). Without one it is a
square around the point, half side `viewRadiusKm / 1.25` (12 km by default), so the box of a place the camera is framed on fits
the free area with the framing's 25 % margin. A group is the union of the TRUE boxes of the visible places below it. All are
axis aligned in screen space, hollow, one art pixel thick, snapped to whole cells (`snapBox`), at least `LOD.floorCells` (the cut keeps `LOD.minBoxCells`)
across on each axis.

**Box outline and colour by state** (2026-10-07, sized by the box 2026-10-08, anchored and coloured by state 2026-10-08; `drawBox`, `edgeLit`, `dashingFor`, `labelTones` in `engine/pixel-labels.ts`). The stroke is the line style, and only SELECTION changes it. At rest AND hovered or focused the four corners are full lines: each corner has a solid ARM along both of its edges and the rest of each edge is dashes, `BOX_STYLE.dashOn` 2 cells lit then a gap of dark cells; the selected box is ONE uninterrupted solid line. The width never changes (one cell in every state, no inner ring, the interior mask always starts one cell inside the outline). **Dash origin** (owner: the dashes slid in from the middle of an edge while the box resized, because the pattern was symmetric): each edge counts its pattern from ONE corner, the top edge from its left corner, the bottom edge from its right corner, the left edge from its top and the right edge from its bottom (`edgeLit(i, n, ..)` counts from cell 0; `drawBox` mirrors the index of the bottom and right edges). So the box is rotationally symmetric (half a turn gives the same picture), the arm at the far end is the only thing that follows the end of an edge, and resizing a box by 1..N cells changes only the far end of each edge (tests: `pixel-labels.test.ts`, including a drawn box grown from each of its four sides). The arm and the gap still scale with the box's on-screen size, by its smaller side in cells (so the four corners match): arm 10 % of it between 3 and 14 cells, gap 3.5 % between 2 and 9; they are whole numbers, so a box that grows across one of those steps re-lays its dashes once (a handful of times over the whole size range) and not at any other moment. An edge too short to hold two arms and one gap (7 cells and under) is a plain solid outline: the arms never overflow or overlap. **A box at the floor is a closed rectangle that opens as it grows** (2026-10-13, owner; `boxOpenness`, `dashingFor(w, h, open)`, `BOX_STYLE.open`): its TRUE size (the smaller side of its projected drawn rectangle, a group's including its padding), as a multiple of the floor size, goes from 1 to 2 and the GAP of the dashes goes from 0 to its full value on a straight line, in whole cells (the arm is the full look's, which a zero gap hides: a corner arm of at least half a side with no gap IS a rectangle, so nothing pops). So a floor-sized box is a plain closed one-cell rectangle, then gets a one cell notch in the middle of each edge, a wider one, then its dashes between its corner arms; from twice the floor the look is the one above, unchanged. The openness is a function of the size only (unsnapped, so a floor-sized box that breathes by a cell while the globe turns keeps one look), the canvas signature sees the gap in cells (a size change inside one gap redraws nothing). Selected: the solid line at every size. Tests: `pixel-labels.test.ts` ("a box opens from a closed rectangle"). **Colour is the state, as a palette LEVEL (never opacity)**: at rest the outline is the palette's `peak` level (the loudest map tone: 5.2:1 light, 5.5:1 dark against the page, so a box is at least as visible as a coastline and 3:1 for a graphical object by a margin), hovered or focused it is the full ink (`ink`, 19:1 / 18:1), selected it is the ink and the solid line. The label follows the box (next paragraph). Same code in the globe and the street overlay (one `BoxScene`). The line-grid rules of `docs/pixel-line-rules.md` hold: whole cells, one cell thick, palette levels at the node's opacity, the dashes are cells that are simply not written. Tests: `pixel-labels.test.ts` (geometry at sizes 1 to 1000, rotational symmetry, anchoring, monotonic growth, limits, tiny boxes, the colour of every state).
**Minimum size and mask.** Two sizes (2026-10-13, owner: "the min height and width could be smaller"). The FOOTPRINT the cut keeps room for stays `LOD.minBoxCells` (9 cells, 22.5 CSS px at 2.5 px): which groups open, which boxes and labels fit are decided on footprints and were calibrated on the owner's data (`open.test.ts`). The rectangle that is DRAWN, labelled and picked is `LodTree.drawX0..drawY1`, concentric with the footprint and with the lower floor `LOD.floorCells` (5 cells, 12.5 CSS px at 2.5 px, 10 px on a phone; 6 cells = 15 px once snapped to the grid, about half of the old 22.5 px), the footprint or inside it, and is a closed rectangle at the floor (above). Its pointer target is NOT smaller: `HIT.minBoxPx` (24 px, `minTargetBox` in `hit-area.ts`) is the least the box part of a target is across, so the hit area keeps the size of the old floor plus the slop. A box smaller than the floor is drawn at that size, outlined, with its interior MASKED in the page colour (the colour of the ocean and the background: black in the dark theme, white in the light one, never a grey), so the small area reads as an outlined area that is empty and the fill does not catch the eye. As its true side grows from 1x to 1.6x the floor (`fillFadeFrom/To`, against `floorCells`) the mask is SWITCHED OFF (a binary state with a hysteresis band, `fillHyst`; the way out is a timed 200 ms opacity transition, "Binary visibility"), and only the outline stays, so you can see through it (`LodTree.fillAlpha`). A box bigger than 2.2x the smaller free side is hidden (shown again below 1.6x: you are inside it), also a timed transition with a hysteresis. Reduced motion: the same states, switched instantly.

**Fades are opacity, state is colour.** A node that is fully visible draws at opacity 1 in both themes; its colour is its state (above), so the map beneath, a recessive grey, is never dimmed by a box and a resting box is never a half-transparent grey. A fade is NEVER a darker or lighter shade of grey: shades have a visible minimum that occludes the map right after a node appears and right before it goes, which breaks the fade. Everything a node draws (outline, interior mask, label plate, text) is composited at the node's alpha (`LodTree.alpha`, the node's own timed opacity, never a function of the camera or of its parent, quantised to 1/64 for drawing) over what is underneath, per art cell: `PixelBuffer` holds straight-alpha RGBA per cell and does the "over" blend, the canvas composites it over the map. The art-pixel grid is untouched (no sub-pixel position, no smoothing). Fades are driven by TIME ("Binary visibility"), and the on-demand frame loop runs until the last one has ended (idle = zero rAF); under reduced motion they are switches. The hovered, focused or selected box is drawn on top, in the ink, at full opacity; it is never thicker than another box.
**The cut** (declutter, not a hierarchy overlay). The published group tree is the cluster tree and a dynamic, screen-space cut decides which nodes are WANTED drawn: only places that pass the visibility rule (front hemisphere, clear of the limb) count; a group is shown as ONE box until two of its most important children can be drawn, each as the box and the label it would be drawn with, clear of every other box and label, and then it is REPLACED by the children that fit (never drawn together with them: "Opening a group into its most important children" below); the size rule still opens it, with all its children, when its box covers `LOD.boxMaxTo` (0.6) of the screen, and every group is open from street scale. It closes again with an easier test and a zoom hysteresis, so a camera jittering around a threshold cannot flap. The decision is BINARY (a node is wanted or not): the cross-fade between a group and its places is two timed transitions that start together over the same 200 ms, their opacities summing to exactly 1 on the way (a smoothstep of a linear progress), whatever the camera does. A lone place is its own rectangle at every zoom, a country with two distant places shows two rectangles, ten places 50 km apart are one box ("10 places") until two of them fit, continents and subregions only appear on crowded views. A node's opacity never depends on its parent's: a node with no drawn ancestor is at full opacity ("Binary visibility"). Reduced motion: no transition, the same decisions. Cost: one projection of every place plus the trials of the groups whose box can hold two boxes and is near the screen (193 nodes of the preview 24 us, 1,000 nodes 35 us, 5,000 nodes 200 us per evaluation on the synthetic hierarchy, the size rule alone 22 / 31 / 182 us; the retargeting pass is one more loop over the nodes); cached until the camera, the forced nodes or the theme change; idle map = zero frames and zero canvas draws.

**Labels** (2026-10-08: moved off the pixel grid, see "Labels in device pixels" below; the boxes stay pixel art). A label is HTML text in the app's monospace stack on a TRANSLUCENT PLATE of the page colour with a 1 to 2 px feather (2026-10-12; was opaque with an 8 px halo), two lines: the NAME (14 px, weight 500) and a smaller SECOND LINE (11 px, regular): for a place its country (always, when known: `Intl.DisplayNames(["en"], {type: "region"})` of the place's `countryCode`; a missing or unknown code adds nothing, silently) followed by its linked entries split by kind with zero kinds omitted ("France · 2 articles · 1 artwork · 3 software"), for a group "<N> places" followed by the entries below it ("12 places · 2 articles"). Nothing is invented: today no entry is linked in the owner's preview, so the line is the country or "N places". Its place is one of 18 candidates around the box's PROJECTED rectangle with `LABEL_TYPE.boxGap` (3 px) plus one art cell of clear room from it, which is 3 to 3 + a cell from the drawn outline (the outline is snapped to cells and lies up to a cell outside the projected rectangle), and the TEXT on the box's left edge: the plate hangs its 6 px of padding outside the edge (`LABEL_TYPE.bleed`; 2026-10-13, "Text flush with the box" below); the plate follows the projected rectangle to the sub-pixel ("Labels move smoothly" below). Places and groups look identical (same colours, same states). The pixel-art label of the earlier versions (Fusion Pixel on the art grid, `LABEL_PAD`, `TEXT_GAP`, the level tones) is described under "Pixel text" below and kept in the code for text that IS part of the map.
**Label colour** (`.map-label` in `app.css`, 2026-10-08; replaces the levels of the pixel label; the plate became translucent on 2026-10-12, see "Translucent plates" below): the name is the page's `--foreground` (19:1 / 18:1 against the page), the second line the `--muted-foreground` (6.8:1 light, 7.9:1 dark on the plate: AA at 11 px), never an opacity. EVERY label has a plate of the page colour, resting, hovered or selected (translucent at rest and hovered, opaque selected and when drawn over another label), with a 1 to 2 px feather, so it reads over coastlines, borders and the graticule in both schemes. Hovered or focused: the label does not change at all (no underline, no plate change: the box's own colour logic, the ink, is what shows; only its stacking order rises); selected: INVERTED, the foreground as the plate and the page colour as the text (second line the page colour mixed 28 % toward the foreground, 10:1 light / 7.9:1 dark), no feather (high contrast already), the box in the ink with its solid line, and the card flush with its box (the plate moves by the padding that hung outside it: "Text flush with the box" below). A label drawn over another because nothing was free is on top on its plate. `readability.test.ts` pins the colours from `app.css` (plate in every state, its alpha token recomputed from the tokens, no underline, no text-shadow, no opacity, the contrast of both lines in both schemes).
A place opens its page, a group flies to frame it; which labels are drawn and what is clickable is "Targets and labels" below. The places list (`PlacesNav`) stays the dependable keyboard path and nests the places under their groups as headings.

**Targets and labels** (`engine/label-plan.ts`, `engine/label-track.ts`, `engine/hit-area.ts`, tests `label-plan.test.ts`, `label-track.test.ts`, `hit-area.test.ts`, check `scripts/globe/groups.mjs targets`, `at-rest` and `flicker`). The rule, in one sentence: EVERY box that is drawn is clickable and EVERY box that is drawn shows its label (owner, 2026-10-08: a box without a name, or a dimmed one, is a UX failure). There is no label that loses a collision and no dimmed box (`LOD.unlabelledAlpha`, the `dim` and `label` timed values and `labelHold` are gone).
- Placement (`planLabels`, pure, deterministic, in CSS px since 2026-10-08: `PX_UNITS`). Nodes are placed in priority order: a selected node first (its score is boosted), then the score (priority, places before groups), then the bigger box, then the slug, so the order of the input never matters. For each node, widest label first, the FIRST of its candidate positions whose plate collides with no plate already placed (`clearance` 4 px) wins; a position that also keeps clear of the BOXES of the other nodes near it (`PlanItem.avoid`, `boxesToAvoid`, 3 px wider) comes before one that touches a box, in EVERY wording (a shorter label beside a box beats the whole label over a neighbour's outline: a city's name is written below its box rather than over its neighbour's); only when no wording has one is the first position of the whole wording taken, rather than going over another plate. The last resort never covers the node's own box either (a displaced plate is outside it or nested in it). ONE anchor is preferred everywhere (2026-10-12, owner: labels sat above, inside, beside their boxes at random). Candidates (`labelCandidates` / `spotAt` in `pixel-labels.ts`, unit-free, priority order = id order in `SPOT`, each entirely on the screen and never over the node's own outline, `coversBox`): above the top-left corner (the anchor), the same above the visible part of the top edge when the corner is off screen, nested inside the top-left corner (the same corner, the other side of the line; only when the plate fits inside the box; `inset` = the outline plus 3 px), and then, as last resorts, above-right, shifted along the top edge at a quarter, a half and three quarters, the other inner corners, below-left, below-right, shifted along the bottom edge, left of the box and right of the box. Outside positions keep `boxGap` (one art cell, 2.5 px; 3 px before 2026-10-12) of clear room to the box and the TEXT is on the box's edge (`bleed` 6, `bleedY` 3 since 2026-10-13: the plate hangs its padding outside the edge; the cut's plates in `lod-tree.ts` and the planner use the same numbers). A box bigger than the screen is positioned against its VISIBLE part, so its label is on screen. If no position is free the label is SHORTENED and every position tried again (`labelVariants` in `label-text.ts`: the whole two-line label, without its entries, without its country / number of places, then the name truncated one character at a time with an ellipsis `…`, narrower each time, each truncation keeping its second line (country or number of places) before it loses it (2026-10-12), down to `MIN_LABEL_CHARS` = 6 characters including the ellipsis, never fewer; a name of six characters or less is never truncated; widths are measured with the real font, so a monospace's wide letters shorten sooner than the pixel font did). If even the shortest is free nowhere, it is the LAST RESORT: the label is drawn ANYWAY, on top, on a page-colour plate (`data-over`), at the position, among all the candidates and the displacements of the first one in steps of 6 px, that overlaps the placed plates least (then the smallest displacement). Hover, focus and selection are NOT inputs of the plan (below).
- Stable and in motion (no flicker, no hopping, no snapping; appearing and disappearing binary and timed as in "Binary visibility": a label is on with its node and has no fade of its own, its CSS opacity follows the node's). A label has a SLOT (candidate id and way of writing, kept in `SlotMemory`, shared by the globe's and the street overlay's scenes so a handover keeps the labels where they were), and what is drawn is not the slot's place but a FOLLOWER of it: `drawn = slot place around the box's CURRENT rectangle (spotAt, O(1), no collision test) + glide`, the glide a remainder in px, RELATIVE TO THE BOX (so the plate sticks to its box as the box moves with the camera: a plate never lags behind its box; the glide is not a position in the world). The glide is taken to zero by a CONTINUOUS ease-in-out in time (2026-10-13; on 2026-10-08 a critically damped spring, on 2026-10-12 a staircase of whole art cells, taken back: "Labels move smoothly" below): `TRACK.glideMs` 200 ms, a smoothstep of the elapsed time (it sets off from rest and comes to rest, no leap in the first frame), a function of time only so the same path at 30, 60 and 144 fps; reduced motion: none) and is exactly 0 from `glideMs` on, so AT REST EVERY LABEL IS EXACTLY AT ITS TARGET. The plan runs continuously at a bounded rate: when a rectangle has changed and `TRACK.replanMs` 100 ms have passed since the last plan (at most 10 plans a second while the camera moves), and once more after the camera has stopped (a timer, because an idle map draws no frames; `dueAt`), so a slot changes WHEN it has to, in the middle of a zoom, and the label glides there instead of snapping at the end (owner: the Paris label stayed right-aligned while zooming in, then snapped to the left at the end). The plan is sticky as before (a choice that is still free is kept and given up for a better one only when that one is free with `upgradeMargin` 10 px of room), and while the camera moves a slot that was chosen less than `TRACK.dwellMs` 350 ms ago is kept as long as it is free (`PlanItem.hold`: voluntary changes are at least 350 ms apart per label; a slot that stops being free is left at once); after `TRACK.restMs` 160 ms of stillness the next plan has no dwell and no upgrade margin (a plan that ran while the camera was still moving, and kept slots from before, is followed by one at rest) and puts every label on its FIRST free position, so the labels at rest do not keep a worse anchor for want of 10 px of room to spare; a kept position that lies over another node's box is given up for a clear one at once. A plan that changes a label's slot sets its glide to "where the plate is now minus the new place", so the plate stays where it is and sets off for the new one; a plan in the middle of a glide RETARGETS: the velocity goes on (no restart), capped at `omega x |glide|` per axis so that the spring can never carry the plate past its new target. A node that appears is labelled AT ONCE in the free places left by the labels that stay (their slot places, not their drawn ones; no other label moves; no glide, the node's own fade shows it); a slot that becomes impossible (a nested label no longer fits) is replaced at once and the plate glides to the new place; at the left and right edges of the screen a plate slides along its box instead of changing slot, but never over its box: a plate that the screen's edge would push onto its own outline (a box at the top of the screen) leaves its slot for the inside of the top-left corner (a reduced-motion hop; with the glide it moves smoothly). A shortened label becomes whole again at the next plan that has room (a text change keeps the plate's anchored edge where it is). A node that is fading out keeps the slot it had, goes with its node and finishes the glide it had. The hit hull and the targets follow the DRAWN plate. Reduced motion (`BoxScene.setReducedMotion`): no glide, and the plan runs only once the camera has stopped (a plan that moves a label is a hop). The frame loop: while a label has not arrived (`BoxScene.animating`, `LabelTracker.gliding`) frames keep coming, and not a frame longer. On the globe the renderer's loop (`labelsAnimating` option) draws them, and a plan that the labels' own timer runs on a map at rest wakes it (`setWake`). On the STREET map they do not repaint the map: a glide is a DOM `transform` and changes nothing on the WebGL canvas, so the scene runs its own frames (`BoxScene.driveGlides`, `engine/frame-driver.ts`: a `requestAnimationFrame` chain that exists only while a label glides, never runs twice in a display frame, and is withdrawn by a frame of the host's that moves the labels anyway); only the transitions of the BOXES (`HudLayer.boxesAnimating`) still keep the map repainting. The first version of the glide woke the map for every glide frame (commit 97d4ab2: `hud.setWake(() => map.triggerRepaint())`), and the street map's full render per frame cost GPU time for a label that moves a few pixels (perf row s5-open, below). Idle costs nothing: `groups.mjs idle` waits for the arrival, then 0 rAF calls, 0 frames, 0 draws.
- Targets. A node's target is the CONVEX HULL of its box and its label plate (wherever the plate went), grown by `HIT.slop` (4 px mouse, 14 px touch, so a touch target is at least about 44 px across even for a minimum box; the box part is at least `HIT.minBoxPx` 24 px across whatever the drawn floor): the box including its interior, the label, and the gap between them (the cell of clear room is inside the hull) (when a label is wider than its box the hull fills the triangle under the label's right end down to the box's bottom corner; a nested label is inside the box). Hover over the whole target shows the pointer cursor and the hover state (the box in the ink with the same dashes; the label does not change); a click selects. A BIG box (wider or taller than `HIT.bigBoxFrac` = 0.45 of the map's smaller side, where a group opens anyway) is an outline you are inside, not an object: its interior is not a target, only its border band (6 px mouse, 12 px touch) and its label.
- Overlaps. Of the targets that contain the point the SMALLEST (by box area) wins, so a group never steals a click on a place inside it; when none contains it, the NEAREST within the slop wins, then the smallest, then the highest priority, then the slug (so the order of the nodes never matters). A node below `LOD.pickAlphaMin` (0.3) is not a target, and one on its way OUT (the cut no longer wants it) only while it is at least `LOD.pickFadingMin` (0.5) opaque (`pickAlpha`), so a group that cross-fades into its children never takes a click that belongs to a child.
- Regression. `label-plan.test.ts` (every item gets a plate in a crowd, priority order and its independence of the input order, nesting, shortening, the last resort, hysteresis in both directions, determinism), `label-track.test.ts` (slots follow the box, the plan runs once at rest and on the safety interval, new boxes in the gaps, impossible slots, shared memory, and the flicker sweep below), `label-text.test.ts`, `label-dom.test.ts`, `pixel-labels.test.ts` (the candidates, the pixel text's variants, spacing and tones), `hit-area.test.ts` (hull, gap triangle, slop, nesting, big boxes, order independence) and, in a browser, `groups.mjs targets`, `at-rest` and `flicker`: over 288 views (the world, continents, Europe, the Balkans, a city cluster, and a sweep of lon / lat / zoom) 100 % of the drawn boxes have a label that is a real element in the DOM with the planned text and place, fully opaque, none is half-faded, no two plates overlap unless one is the last resort, and hovering the box, the label and the gap shows the pointer and the hover state. On the owner's preview: 701 boxes, 7 labels shortened, 6 drawn over another as the last resort, 38 nested. Root cause of the earlier bug where a name was missing for a place next to a bigger one (a priority floor withheld names) and its negative control are in the 2026-10-08 history of this section: the floor is gone for good.
**Pixel text (kept, but no longer used for the box labels).** The box LABELS left the pixel grid on 2026-10-08 ("Labels in device pixels" below); `PixelBuffer`, `PixelOverlay`, `pixel-font/`, `drawLabel`, `labelLayout`, `labelVariants` and the level tones of `pixel-labels.ts` stay, with their tests, for text that is part of the map itself (route labels, later). The boxes are still drawn with this pipeline. `PixelBuffer` is a level image whose cells are the art pixels; `PixelOverlay` shows it on a `cols x rows`
canvas upscaled with `image-rendering: pixelated`. Boxes, plates and glyphs are written into it at whole-cell positions, one
cell thick, as a palette level and an opacity: no sub-pixel position, no anti-aliasing grey. The canvas is only touched when the
set of boxes and labels, their cells or their tones changed (frame signature), and only the changed region is uploaded.
- Font: **Fusion Pixel 10px Proportional** (TakWolf, github.com/TakWolf/fusion-pixel-font, version 2024.05.12, SIL Open Font License 1.1 with the Reserved Font Name 'Fusion Pixel', from `@fontsource/fusion-pixel-10px-proportional-sc`, a pinned dev dependency; licence text in `engine/pixel-font/OFL.txt`, no attribution needed at runtime; the baked table does not use the reserved name). Owner (2026-10-08): the Tiny5 type, stretched taller, read as condensed and was not suited to this kind of text. It is drawn natively on a 10 px em with 7 rows for a capital, 5 for a lowercase letter, 2 below the baseline, 1-cell stems, and it is proportional (a letter advances its width plus one cell: 5 for most lowercase, 6 for a capital), so a 22-letter label such as a city and its country ("<City>, <Country>") is 109 cells (272 CSS px at 2.5 px) wide and nothing is stretched: `stretch.ts` is gone. `scripts/font/bake-pixel-font.mjs` (`pnpm --filter @catalyst/web bake:font`, reproducible: the same file gives the same table) rasterises the outlines at the pixel centres (100 font units per pixel) for Latin, Greek, Cyrillic, punctuation and currency ranges into `pixel-font/pixel-font-data.ts` (460 glyphs, 14 KB; the baked bitmaps are a derivative of the font and fall under the same OFL, kept with `OFL.txt`). The package ships the whole CJK font (928 KB) as a single file, which cannot be subset without making a Modified Version that may not carry the Reserved Font Name, so the font itself is a lockfile-pinned dev dependency, as Tiny5 was, and not vendored; the licence text is vendored.
  Candidates rendered on the real label pipeline (box, plate, name and counter, light and dark: `font-candidates-light.png` / `-dark.png`, kept out of the repo): Tiny5 as it was (5 rows stretched to 7, bold name: condensed), **Fusion Pixel 10px** (chosen: 7 / 5 rows, light 1-cell stems, proportional, real lowercase, accents drawn on the grid), Fusion Pixel 12px (9 / 6 rows: the same design, 29 % taller and wider than the art grid wants), Pixelify Sans (OFL; designed on a half-pixel grid, so at the art resolution its accents collapse to one dot and several letters lose their shape), Jersey 10 (OFL; 10-row capitals, heavy and tall: the bold the owner removed), Press Start 2P (OFL with a Reserved Font Name; 8-cell monospace, about twice as wide), Silkscreen (OFL; capitals only, 5 rows) and DotGothic16 (OFL; 13 rows, thin and tall, 7 wide). Fusion Pixel's limits: it has about half of Latin Extended-A (see the next bullets) and Greek and Cyrillic only in their basic ranges.
- One weight. There is no bold: the derived double strike (`emboldened`, `glyphWeight`) and the `bold` flags of `measureText`, `forEachInk` and `PixelBuffer.text` are removed. The hierarchy between a name and its counter is colour (above), not weight.
- Coverage (`pixel-font.ts`): the font's own glyphs (Latin-1, the Latin Extended-A letters Ă ć č ě ğ ń ž ..., Greek and Cyrillic letters, the dashes, apostrophes and the ellipsis; é è ê ç ã ñ ï ü ö ô ø å and their capitals are all its own). What it lacks is COMPOSED here from its own glyphs, in its style, with a donor letter per mark: the caron of š ř ž (cut out of č), acute, ring, circumflex and breve for any letter, the cedilla and comma below of ş ș ţ ț (cut out of ç), the ogonek of ą ę, the double acute of ő ű, the dotless ı and the dotted İ, the strokes of ł Ł, the apostrophe-like caron of ď ť ľ, the ligatures œ Œ ĳ Ĳ, the Ukrainian letters that are look-alikes or mirrors (і ї є Ґ ...) and Vietnamese letters (Huế, Hà Nội: base letter, horn and tone marks, stacked above the circumflex or breve). Missing glyphs after that (Arabic, CJK, the rest of Latin Extended-B and Greek extended, Serbian Cyrillic ђ ћ ...) fall back to the system font rasterised at 8 px and thresholded at 50 % at art resolution, no grey, uneven strokes: documented, rare in place names. On the owner's preview this affects only the Arabic names; every other letter of its 193 names is the font's own or composed (tests: `pixel-font.test.ts`, 36 names).
**Art pixel size** (`ART_PIXEL` in `engine/tuning.ts`, the ONE constant the globe, the street map, the pixel text and the
the pixel text read; the street pass takes it through `STREET_TUNING.pixelSize`; constant at runtime, see "Pixel density is constant" above). Owner: "a little higher resolution, the pixel
effect a little less visible". 3 CSS px (6 device px at DPR 2) became **2.5 px** on desktop (5 device px at DPR 2, 2 px at DPR 1
because ties go down, never coarser) and stays 2 px on phones; `pixelSize()` always rounds to whole device pixels, so
nearest-neighbour scaling never shimmers (DPR 1.25, 1.5, 2.625 get the nearest whole device size). One line to change it;
`?globe-debug&art-px=3` overrides it for comparisons. `docs/screenshots/resolution-comparison.png` compares 3, 2.5 and 2 px on
the demo (map and text): 2 px reads almost smooth and makes the type small; 2.5 px keeps the character and the text is clearer
than at 3. GPU-synced globe frame on an M4, p50 over 60 frames: 0.6 / 0.7 to 1.3 / 0.8 to 1.1 ms for 3 / 2.5 / 2 px, all noise at this
level; `pnpm --filter @catalyst/web perf` passes every frame-time budget at 2.5 px (see `docs/performance.md`). The street pass
internals (line gate thresholds, crawl budget) belong to the street style: after the change `perf` reports
`desktop crawl(snapped) changedShare 0.35 > 0.35` (the budget sits exactly on the value) and nothing else.

**Far-zoom border flicker (investigated).** At far zoom the Alaska / Canada border flickered and the middle of the USA / Canada
border was missing, and it came back when zooming in. Cause: a GL line is a straight chord through the globe and the occluder disc
is 0.002 under the surface, so any border segment longer than about 7 degrees sank into the disc in its middle; the globe's border
data had 10 to 40 degree segments (the 49th parallel is one straight segment), and which pixels survived changed with the camera:
flicker. It is a DATASET cause first (fixed in `packages/geodata`, whose borders now have no segment over 4 degrees), and the
engine now also cuts every segment into pieces of at most 1 degree (`engine/geometry.ts MAX_CHORD_DEG`, tested in
`geometry.test.ts`), so it no longer depends on the data. `scripts/globe/borders.mjs` samples the 49 N border (122.5 W to
97 W) and the 141 W meridian over 96 views per theme and size and over 60-frame drags, and requires a pixel at every sample and no
blink; on the current dataset it passes with and without the engine cut (the data no longer has long chords), so
the unit tests are the regression guard for the engine part.

### Binary visibility, timed transitions (2026-10-08)

Owner: appearance and disappearance of boxes, labels and other map content was driven continuously by the camera, so a camera that came to rest between two states left labels half transparent and greyish. The rule now, everywhere in the map:

1. **A thing is on or off.** The camera only decides a TARGET, from the camera with a HYSTERESIS band wherever a threshold exists (no flicker when the camera jitters around it).
2. **Its opacity runs to the target by TIME** (`engine/fade.ts`: `FADE_MS` 200 ms, a smoothstep of a linear progress, so two things that swap and start together sum to exactly 1 on the way), whatever the camera does. A reversal turns around from where the value is. A pause (a hidden tab) counts as one frame, not as the whole transition.
3. **The resting frame is always fully on or fully off.** The on-demand frame loop keeps running until every transition has ended (`LodTree.animating`, `GlobeScene.animating`; the street engine asks MapLibre for repaints while `HudLayer.animating`), then stops: idle is still zero frames. While the street map owns the view the globe's loop does not spin on transitions it cannot advance.
4. **Reduced motion** is an instant switch (`step(.., instant)`), the same decisions.
5. **Geometry is not faded.** A place that crosses the globe's limb, or a group left without a visible place, vanishes at once (a fading box would be drawn mirrored behind the globe); positions and sizes follow the camera continuously.
6. **A node's opacity never depends on its parent's, and a group and the children it opens into are one cross-fade.** A node with no drawn ancestor is at full opacity (a city under an open Europe); a group that is opening does not count as an ancestor of the places replacing it. No node is ever drawn together with a drawn ancestor at rest, with no exemption (a place that is selected or is a stop of a shown route opens the groups above it: `LodTree.mustOpen`). A group and its children are retargeted in the same `update`, so they start together and run on one clock, one duration and one ease: the group goes 1 -> 0 exactly as its children go 0 -> 1, the opacity of a group plus that of any child below it is at most 1 all along (a smoothstep of a linear progress; a child that starts later only lags), and a reversal keeps the pair in step. The labels follow: a label has no fade of its own and no CSS opacity transition, the box, its mask and its label are all drawn at the node's own `alpha`, whatever is hovered or selected.

Per node the tree keeps two timed values (`LodTree.life`, `mask`): the node itself and its interior mask (both set by the cut). A label is on with its node and has no transition of its own (the former `label` and `dim` values and the `born` flag went with the rule that dropped and dimmed labels: every drawn box has its label). Hysteresis values: group open/close the gap to every other box and label `LOD.open.gapPx` 8 / 3 and the zoom `stickyZoom` 0.4 (and the size rule's `boxMaxFrom/To` 0.45 / 0.6), box hidden for being bigger than the screen `sizeFadeTo` 2.2 / back `sizeFadeFrom` 1.6, mask off at 1.6x the minimum / on again at `fillHyst` (0.3 / 0.7 of its smoothstep), label position and wording kept with `UPGRADE_MARGIN` 2 cells of room, borders `TUNING.borderZoom` 3.25 +- 0.05, street layers 0.05 zoom each side (`street/style/layer-switch.ts`). Tests: `fade.test.ts` (every property of the primitive), `lod-tree.test.ts` ("timed transitions": opening over the fade time, a camera that stops mid-way still ends resolved, frame-length independence, reversal, pause, idle), `open.test.ts` ("the cross-fade": the sum of a group and of a node below it never exceeds 1, over a scripted opening, a reversal and random camera paths with random frame times, on a synthetic and on the real tree), `label-plan.test.ts` (the label hysteresis), `layer-switch.test.ts`, `flatness.test.ts`, `palette.test.ts`. Browser: `groups.mjs timed` (a camera that opens a crowd and stops: 12 frames mid-transition, done at 204 ms, the last frame resolved and the loop stopped) and `groups.mjs at-rest` (wheel bursts with momentum, flings, every threshold of four paths landed on from both sides, the borders' threshold: every box, mask, label and layer fully on or off, nothing dimmed, no frame pending), `street/lod-check.mjs` for the street map.

**Sea texture and graticule: the flatness of the view.** The street map is a MapLibre globe up to its zoom 12, so the earth is visibly curved for a long stretch of the street map's zoom range (the street map takes over from the Three.js globe at the cut, internal zoom 3.7). The sea texture (dashes in rows, anchored to the screen) used to ease in from map zoom 5.2 while the graticule eased out between 6.5 and 9.5: both showed together on a curved earth, a weird flat look. Now the graticule (parallels and meridians) is on while the earth is curved and the sea texture takes over once the view is close to flat, one binary swap that the street map's temporal ease cross-fades by time. "Close to flat" is a measure on the screen, not a bare zoom: the BULGE, `(h/2)^2 / (2R)` CSS px, the height by which the surface falls away at the top or bottom edge of a view `h` px high (R the apparent radius of the globe at the unified zoom). The view is flat at a bulge of 4 px or less (`FLAT.flatPx`), curved again at 6 px or more (`curvedPx`, the hysteresis): unified zoom 8.28 on a 900 px tall view (the latitude cancels in the unified zoom: map zoom 8.28 at the equator, 7.78 at 45 degrees), 8.1 on a 844 px phone, 9.6 on a 1400 px screen. `street/core/flatness.ts`, `layer-switch.ts`; the sea texture is further held back to `handoff + 0.7` for a tile source that only covers a place.

**Inventory of every continuous function of the camera that controls opacity, width, tone or visibility of map content, and what was decided** (2026-10-08):

| Where | Was | Decision |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Detection boxes: group to places (`lod-tree.ts`) | opacity a smoothstep of the camera ("openness"), partition of unity | binary cut with hysteresis, timed 200 ms swap (sum 1 on the way) |
| Box bigger than the screen | opacity fade between 1.6x and 2.2x | binary with hysteresis (hidden at 2.2x, back below 1.6x), timed |
| Interior mask of a minimum box | opacity fade between 1x and 1.6x the minimum | binary with hysteresis, timed |
| Labels (`box-scene.ts`, `label-plan.ts`) | label alpha followed the node's, dim applied to every collision loser | no dim and no label transition: every drawn box has its label (candidate positions, a shorter label, then drawn on top), kept between frames with a margin (hysteresis in cells) |
| Globe country borders (`scene.ts`) | grey level a staircase over zoom 3.0 to 3.5 | on/off at 3.25 +- 0.05, tone ramp run by time |
| Street road, river, lake, border classes (`street/style/lod.ts`) | tone a staircase over a zoom range (12 classes, up to 4 zoom levels long) | binary layer switch at `on` with hysteresis, the ease fades the cells by time |
| Street fills: parks, green, buildings | tone ramps | the same |
| Street sea texture | tone ramp from map zoom 5.2 to 10 | binary, by the flatness of the view |
| Street graticule | tone ramp out between 6.5 and 9.5 | binary, on while curved, off when flat |
| Tile arrival and departure (`street/core/ease.ts`) | already timed: a level per 24 ms, run to the end by the settle loop | kept; verified to converge (`lod-check.mjs`, `tile-fade.mjs`, `cut-fade.mjs`) |
| Globe to street cut and back (`handover/`) | cross-fade of 300 ms by time, hysteresis 3.7 / 3.45 | kept (already binary and timed) |
| Overlay owner switch, reload fade, panel inset | CSS transitions or time-based tweens | kept (time) |
| Line widths along their ramps, the 1 to 2 px step of main roads at z9, hollow roads from z16 | continuous in zoom | legitimately continuous: geometry. The z9 step is within 0.01 of zoom (a width, never an intermediate one) |
| Sea outline and country borders: world lines to tile lines at the hand-over zoom | a hard switch between two data sources | kept: binary, cross-faded by the ease |
| World placeholder lines while tiles load | binary on `areTilesLoaded()` | kept |
| Lift of route arcs on the globe (`routeLift`), route draw-on (2.2 s) | geometry, and a time-based animation | kept |

Fixed on the way: (a) the first version of the street layer switch set `layout.visibility` on EVERY tile layer from the switch's `on` set, so the always-on layers (coast, country borders, the erasing interiors of hollow roads) were hidden: from space only the graticule, the outline and the boxes were left, layers came back one by one while zooming in, and at street scale every big road was a solid band with no hierarchy. Only the switched layers (`switched(spec)`) take the state now; regression tests in `lod.test.ts` and, on the real app, `scripts/street/lod-check.mjs` (always-on layers visible, map never empty, along the whole path). (b) While the street map owns the view the globe's frame loop is suspended; the new transitions must not keep it spinning (a stuck border fade did, found by `settleApp` timing out).

### Labels in device pixels (2026-10-08)

Owner: the pixel labels were too big (a capital was 17 px tall), too heavy and flickered while the camera moved. The boxes stay pixel art (the map's own grid); the LABELS are HTML text. Code: `engine/label-text.ts` (type constants, measurement, variants), `label-sub.ts` (the second line: entry kinds and counts), `label-plan.ts` (the planner), `label-track.ts` (when it runs, slots), `label-dom.ts` (pooled elements), `box-scene.ts` (ties them to the boxes and to `hit()`), CSS `.map-label` in `app.css` (the `.map-halo` feather it once had is gone: "Translucent plates" below). Shared by the globe and the street overlay (one `BoxScene`).

**Type and spacing** (`LABEL_TYPE`, one place; the CSS takes the family from `--font-mono`, the monospace stack of the credits line and the dev badge). Two lines (owner feedback, 2026-10-08, after a screenshot in the light scheme where the labels were illegible over coastlines and borders): line 1 the NAME, 14 px, weight 500, tracking 0.01 em, line height 18 px; line 2 the SECOND LINE, 11 px, weight 400, tracking 0.02 em, line height 14 px (`LABEL_TYPE.name` / `.sub`). Plate: 6 px padding left and right, 3 px above and below, so 24 px high with one line and 38 px with two (`LABEL_H_ONE`, `LABEL_H_TWO`), as wide as the wider line. Spacing: the TEXT is on the box's outer left edge (`bleed` 6 = `padX`: the plate hangs outside the edge by its padding; the right-aligned candidates likewise on the right edge, the left and right ones with the text's top line on the box's top edge, `bleedY` 3 = `padY`); 3 px of clear room between a label and its box (`boxGap`, above, below, beside; it was 7 px); a nested label 3 px (`nestInset`) beyond the outline; 4 px of clearance between two labels. The hit hull and the cut (`LodTree.labelW/H`) follow these numbers. The 12, 13 and 14 px sizes were compared on screenshots of the first monospace version; the name is now 14 px because the plate has a real ground and a second, smaller line gives the hierarchy.

**The plate and its feather** (as built on 2026-10-08; SUPERSEDED on 2026-10-12 by "Translucent plates" below: the plate is translucent, the feather 1 to 2 px on the plate itself, no `.map-halo`). Every label has a plate of the page colour behind its text (so the text never sits on a map line) and a soft edge around it: `.map-halo`, a transparent twin of the plate with `box-shadow: 0 0 8px 3px var(--background), 0 0 16px 3px var(--background)` (`LABEL_TYPE.feather` blur 8, spread 3; two layers so the falloff has no visible step), which fades the map out over about 8 to 12 px around the text. The feather is a SEPARATE element in a layer BELOW the boxes' canvas (first child of the label root): it dims coastlines and the graticule but never a box's outline, which stays crisp 3 px from the label; a single element with a shadow would have washed out the box edge next to every label. Two elements per label, moved by the same `transform` (one write): the cost is a second compositor layer per label (not measured; no performance run). A selected label has no feather (the plate is inverted, high contrast). Checked on screenshots of the owner's preview, light and dark, over the Mediterranean, the Balkans (about 35 labels), a selected place and a phone-width view (390 px): the names and the second lines stay legible over dense coastlines and borders.

**The second line** (`engine/label-sub.ts`, pure, tested in `label-sub.test.ts`). A place: its country (when known), then its linked entries split by kind; a group: "<N> places" ("1 place"), then the entries of the places below it; kinds with no entry are left out, the line itself when nothing is left (no invented data; today no entry is linked, so a place says its country and a group "N places"). The kinds are a TABLE, `ENTRY_KINDS`: articles, artworks, software (the project kind's word), in that order, each with a singular and a plural ("2 articles · 1 artwork · 3 software"); poems are the next kind: add one row. A kind that is not in the table is ignored by the labels.
