The entries come from the web app's own projection, no contract or API change: `lib/projection.ts` `placeEntries` collects, per place, the content items whose `placeSlugs` name it and its own `related` refs, each (kind, slug) once, only for items that exist (`PlaceSummary` has no `related`, so the full projection is read), and `buildPlaceIndex` hands them to the globe as `GlobePlace.entries` (optional). `LodTree` counts them (`nodeEntryCounts`: a group counts the DISTINCT entries below it, so an article about two of its cities counts once). When there is no room the plan shortens the label in this order (`labelVariants`): drop the entries, then the country / number of places (one line), then truncate the name with an ellipsis down to 6 characters; a step that would not make the plate any smaller (a name wider than its second line) is skipped. Hover and selection never change the text (the old "Name, Country" expanded label and `anchored` are gone).

**Measurement.** Widths come from a 2D canvas `measureText` in the resolved `--font-mono` family, once per string and run (cached; tracking added per character, rounded up to half a px), so the plan, the cut (`LodTree.labelW/H`: a label counts as part of its node, now in CSS px with the clear room) and the hit hull use numbers in CSS px; the DOM is never read back per frame. Without a canvas (the unit tests, a server) a deterministic monospace approximation is used (`setTextMeter` replaces it).

**Labels move smoothly, translucent plates, a second line that stays (2026-10-12, motion revised 2026-10-13).** Owner, on seeing the labels after the restraint pass: (1) "The labels glide so smoothly, which is jarring because all the other content moves at a constant pixel size. Maybe animate them with a minimum displacement equal to the current map pixel size." (2) "Let's not dim the content completely behind the labels, just a bit, enough to meet WCAG standards." (3) a shortened label lost its country while its bigger neighbour's showed it: a shortened label keeps its second line whenever it fits.

*(1) Motion: tried on the art-pixel lattice on 2026-10-12, REVERTED for the labels on 2026-10-13.* The 2026-10-12 version put every label on the map's cell lattice (a plate a whole number of cells in size, its position a lattice point, a slot change a staircase of whole cells, `SpotUnits.lattice` and `pxUnits(cell, left, top)`). It was consistent with the boxes and the map, and it was wrong for the labels: the drawn box steps by a whole cell (2.5 px) as the camera moves, so a label that follows it steps by 2.5 px too, and with the idle rotation turning, the world labels jumped 2.5 px at a time while the map itself is redrawn at the display rate. Owner, 2026-10-13: "I'd like to backtrack on the feature which makes labels on the map move with a resolution equal to the pixel resolution of the map: let's make it smoother, so when the earth spins they move smoothly." What the labels do now:
- A label follows the PROJECTED rectangle of its box, not the snapped one: `NodeScreen.fx0..fy1` (container CSS px, NaN when a renderer did not fill them; the globe's `syncNodes` and the street overlay's `HudLayer.update` do), read by `BoxScene` as `Item.cont` and handed to the tracker as the box's rectangle. The boxes are drawn exactly as before (`snapBox`, on the pixel canvas); the hit target is still the drawn box plus the plate.
- The planner and the tracker work in exact CSS px: `smoothUnits(cell)` (`label-plan.ts`): no lattice, `quant` 0 (the positions shifted along an edge are exact too), the gap `boxGap` + one cell from the projected edge (so 3 to 3 + a cell from the snapped outline, never touching it: the outline lies within a cell of the projected rectangle), the nested inset `cell` + `nestInset` (clears the outline's inner side). Plates are the measured size again (24 / 38 px high), as the cut (`LodTree.labelW/H`) counts them. `pxUnits` and `SpotUnits.lattice` stay as an option, unused by the labels.
- While a slot is kept the plate is at the slot's place around the box's current projected rectangle, so it moves by exactly what the box does, to the sub-pixel (`groups.mjs flicker`: 0 of 3000 to 3900 kept-slot label-frames out of step per view, worst 6e-14 px; `spin`: below). A slot change eases continuously (200 ms smoothstep relative to the box, above); reduced motion is instant.
- The DOM position is EXACT while anything moves (a fraction of a device pixel: `LabelLayer.begin(false)`), and rounded to whole device pixels at rest so the text is sharp: `BoxScene` sees "moving" when a projected rectangle moved by 0.01 px or more or a glide is running, and a 150 ms timer asks for the resting picture after the last moving frame.
- Stability that was not about the lattice is unchanged (sticky slots, 100 ms plans, the dwell, the plan at rest, `fixed` plates for new labels, no teleports). The tracker's other API (`glideRemainder(g0, elapsed, cell, opts)`, `TRACK.stepMs`) is kept; `stepMs` is 0 (continuous), more is a staircase, `cell` > 0 rounds to cells.

*(2) Translucent plates.* The plate is the page colour at `--label-plate-alpha` (app.css, in the four scheme blocks; `LABEL_PLATE_ALPHA` in `engine/label-plate.ts`), the least alpha at which BOTH lines are AA (4.5:1: the 14 px name and the 11 px second line) against the worst tone behind a glyph. The worst tone is the loudest the map draws: the `peak` level of the ramp, the colour of a box's outline at rest (one level above the coastline; it is what a plate over another node's outline sees). The contrast is computed with the compositor's own blending (per channel in sRGB, `alpha x page + (1 - alpha) x peak`) by bisection, `minPlateAlpha`; the second line (muted foreground) is the binding one. Measured from the tokens: light (page #fbfbfb, text #0a0a0a / #595959, peak 106 grey): name 0.098, second line 0.6972 -> token 0.70; dark (page #0a0a0a, text #f5f5f5 / #a3a3a3, peak 135 grey): name 0.179, second line 0.6150 -> token 0.62. Over the coastline tone (one level below) the second line needs 0.59 / 0.44; over every quieter tone less. A full-ink line (a route, a hovered or selected outline) is not the design case: it would need 0.82 / 0.80, a plate is kept clear of every outline by the planner (`avoid`) and a route is one cell thin; not covered, and said so. `readability.test.ts` recomputes it per scheme from the stylesheet (token equals the rounded minimum, one percent less fails the second line, the blended colours of every ramp tone pass). The feather is a `box-shadow` of the plate's own colour, `LABEL_TYPE.feather` blur 2 px / spread 1 px (a few pixels of falloff, nothing that dims the map around the text), on the plate itself: the separate `.map-halo` element and its layer below the boxes are gone (one element per label instead of two; the feather no longer needs to stay below the boxes because it is 3 px wide and the plate is 1 cell from them). The selected plate (inverted) and a plate drawn over other labels (the last resort: a translucent plate would show the text under it) are opaque with no feather; hover keeps its rules; reduced transparency makes the plate opaque. The compromise: during a glide a plate that passes over another label shows through it for the 180 ms (the planner keeps plates clear at rest, and a plate never glides onto one that is still). **Round 3 (2026-10-13, owner: "the background of the labels on the map is not opaque enough, a bit less see-through"): the token is a CHOSEN value above the floor, 0.86 light / 0.80 dark** (`LABEL_PLATE_ALPHA`; the floor is `LABEL_PLATE_FLOOR`, 0.70 / 0.62, recomputed from the tokens by `readability.test.ts`, which now requires the token to be at least 0.12 above it and at most `LABEL_PLATE_MAX` 0.9). Looked at on the owner's preview in both schemes over the pixel coastlines, the dense Balkans, the Alps and the street map: still a plate (a coastline under it reads as a ghost), no longer a window. The text contrast only grows with the alpha (second line over the box's rest tone 5.7:1 light, 6.2:1 dark; was 4.5:1), the feather is as it was. Round 2 asked whether the plate could be more transparent, which the floor answers (below the floor the second line is under AA): at 0.60 / 0.52 the second line over the box's rest tone is 3.89:1 / 3.74:1, under AA, so the tokens stay 0.70 / 0.62 (the minima are 0.6972 / 0.6150); only against a COASTLINE tone, without a box outline under the text, the minima are 0.5926 / 0.4446, i.e. 0.60 / 0.45 would pass: that is a change of the worst case the owner has not been asked about (the planner keeps plates off outlines but takes a touching position as a last resort).

*(3) The second line.* `labelVariants` (`label-text.ts`) now orders the ways of writing a label as: the whole label; without the entries; without the second line (the whole name, the same width and a line lower: the second line is dropped as the LAST step that keeps the whole name); then the name truncated one character at a time, each truncation FIRST with its second line (the country, or the number of places) and then without it, as long as the second line does not make the plate wider than the truncated name. A variant is dropped only when an earlier one is at most as wide AND as high. So "Lisbo…" now reads "Lisbo… / Portugal" when that fits, and only loses "Portugal" when the taller plate does not. The last resort (drawn over others) weighs the whole label, the narrowest way that still has its second line and the shortest, takes the one that overlaps the placed plates least and, at equal overlap, the one that keeps its second line (before, it was the shortest, which is what made a place lose its country in a crowd).

*Numbers* (this machine, Chrome for Testing 153, headless, the owner's preview, 1440 x 900 at DPR 2, 60 Hz rAF for `spin`; sweeps of `label-track.test.ts`, `open.test.ts` and `groups.mjs`; 2026-10-13, smooth labels): synthetic sweep of 72 labels (boxes at fractions of a pixel), 180 frames of pan and zoom then rest: 125 slot changes (1.74 per label), 0 teleports, 1090 label-frames gliding, the largest step of a plate against its box 6.7 px in a frame, 13 frames to settle, 0 of 7674 kept-slot label-frames out of lockstep, 9506 plate-frames between cells (the same plan with no glide: 67 teleports). Browser `groups.mjs flicker` (3 s scripted pan and zoom, 120 Hz): Europe 38 labels, 102 slot changes (2.68 per label), 0 teleports, 0 of 3878 kept-slot label-frames out of step with the projected box, 4905 of 5055 plate-frames between cells while the drawn box stepped by whole cells 3643 of 3643 times; the Balkans 41 labels, 155 (3.78), 0 teleports, 0 of 2971; the Atlantic, Iberia and Morocco 49 labels, 156 (3.18), 0 teleports, 0 of 2163; the largest step of a plate against its box in a frame 20.8 to 32.2 px (the middle of a 200 ms glide across the screen; 6.7 px in the synthetic sweep). IDLE ROTATION (`groups.mjs spin`, `?rotate&spin-idle=400`, 4 s at 60 fps, 1330 kept-slot label-frames): a label moves a median 0.33 px and at most 0.55 px per frame, 0 of 1330 moves a whole cell; the drawn box moves 2.5 px per move (179 of 179 whole cells); the difference between a label's step and its projected box's step is 0 (exactly), and the label's element is exactly where the label is (0.000 px). `groups.mjs targets`: 288 views, 1360 drawn boxes, 100 % labelled, 56 shortened, 15 last resorts, 17 nested; every plate on the projected box's left edge and 3 px + one cell above it; the plate's computed background alpha equal to the token.

*Not verified.* Safari and iOS (the `color-mix` with `calc` of the plate colour is Chrome 111+, Safari 16.2+, Firefox 113+; a browser without it gets the opaque plate through an `@supports not` rule: written, not tested); a real phone; the perceived feel of the 200 ms eased glide and of the sub-pixel motion on a real display (measured, not judged on a hand-driven map; sub-pixel text during a move is rendered by the compositor and may be a little soft until the resting picture 150 ms after the last move: not looked at on a high-DPR panel by eye, only in screenshots at rest); a label's visible gap to a box varies by up to one cell as the drawn box steps under a smooth label (by design); the contrast of text over a full-ink line (above); the street overlay (same `BoxScene`, not driven: the local tile server is needed).

**DOM, not canvas.** Both were built and measured (Chrome for Testing, headless, M-series Mac, 1440x900 at device pixel ratio 2, CDP `Performance.getMetrics` per frame over 300 frames, every label moving every frame): main-thread task per frame (script + style + layout + the browser's own rAF overhead) 100 labels 1.38 ms DOM / 1.46 ms canvas, 200 labels 1.80 / 1.17, 400 labels 1.75 / 2.48; style recalculation 0.4 to 0.6 ms and layout 0.00 ms for the DOM (a label moves by `transform: translate3d` only, so the browser rasterises each label once and the compositor moves it), the frame interval stayed 16.7 ms (p95 16.8 to 17.3) in every case. So at 100 to 200 labels the two are equal on the main thread, the canvas wins by a little at 200 and loses at 400, and the canvas additionally uploads a full 2880x1800 texture whenever a label moves (not visible in these numbers; GPU cost unmeasured). The DOM was kept: it has real text (selection and find-in-page are disabled on purpose, the overlay is `aria-hidden` because the places list is the accessible path and a screen reader should not read every place twice), CSS states and transitions come for free (hover plate, inverted selection, the glide), the theme is the page's own CSS variables with no JS plumbing, and its cost does not grow with the screen's pixel count. On the real app (the preview, 5 to 26 labels in view) one frame of the label scene writes 4 to 17 elements and costs about 0.1 to 0.25 ms of script plus 0.1 ms of style. Not measured: phone GPU and CPU (a phone-size viewport was only run on a desktop machine).

**The first label costs 30 ms, once (2026-10-09).** On macOS the first layout change after the first mouse move blocks the renderer's main thread for 30 to 35 ms (Blink recomputes the hover state, which initialises the system's window-server client once per process); on the world view, whose DOM never changes, that is the first label that appears, in the middle of the first drag (perf budget, mobile S1: one 33 ms frame). Not a cost of the labels: any layout change does it. `engine/hover-warm.ts` takes it on the first mouse move, in an idle period (an invisible 1 px element added and removed), where nothing moves. Measurements and what did not help: `docs/performance.md`, "Mobile S1 long frame".

**Text flush with the box, the card, the floor box (2026-10-13).** Owner, after the first publication: (1) the label plate is "not opaque enough" (the alpha: "Translucent plates" above); (2) "the padding from the card (useful when the element on the map is selected) looks odd when unselected: the text should be aligned vertically with the edge of the bbox"; (4) "when a bbox reaches its minimum floor width and height, instead of the chevron/corner style make it a rectangle, which splits up into the look we have once it starts growing; the min height and width could be smaller too".

*The rule (2).* A label that is not selected has its TEXT on the box's edge: the plate hangs the padding that surrounds the text outside the edge, `LABEL_TYPE.bleed` 6 px = `padX` on the side of the edge it is aligned with, `bleedY` 3 px = `padY` for a label beside its box. Per slot (`spotAt`, `SPOT`): above-left, visible above-left, below-left: the text's left edge is the box's left edge; above-right, visible above-right, below-right: the text's right edge is the box's right edge (the plate's right edge is `bleed` past it); left of the box and right of it: the text's top line is on the box's top edge; nested (inside the outline) and shifted along an edge: no edge to align with, the plate is where it was (nested: the padding is inside the outline, `inset`). A plate whose hanging padding would leave the screen stays on it (the text is then up to 6 px from a box edge that is itself at the screen's edge). The vertical room to the box (`boxGap` plus a cell) is unchanged. One geometry: `labelCandidates` / `spotAt` (units `PlanUnits.bleed` / `bleedY`, `smoothUnits`), which the planner collides, the cut of the hierarchy (`LodTree.plateAt`, `lay`) reserves, `follow` places for a fading node, the hit hull (`hit-area.ts`) takes as the plate and the DOM draws.

*The card.* Selected, the label is the card and keeps its look (plate flush with the box, the padding inside it): it moves by `selectedShift(slot, bleed, bleedY)` (left-aligned slots right by 6 px, right-aligned left by 6, beside slots down by 3, others 0). It is written once per slot change as two custom properties (`--map-label-dx`, `--map-label-dy`, `LabelLayer.put` `dx`, `dy`, only when they change) and applied by the stylesheet to `.map-label[data-mode="selected"]` as a CSS `translate`, which composes with the `transform` that positions the label and runs on the compositor with a 120 ms transition (`--duration-fast`, `--ease-standard`; none under reduced motion): selecting and deselecting slide the card by 6 px, no layout, no paint, no jump, and no new per-frame DOM read or write (the label layer's per-frame write count is unchanged: `label-dom.test.ts`). The planner reserves the room of a selected card too (`PlanItem.reserve`, `occupied`): no other plate is planned over the 6 px the card takes, and the hit target of a selected label is the card (`BoxScene.present`).

*The cut* keeps its calibration: it keeps room for the plate where it will be drawn (`plateAt` uses `LABEL_TYPE.bleed`), and a plate 6 px further out moves its measured numbers a little. Re-measured on the owner's preview (`open.test.ts`, `OPEN_REPORT=1`): pairs closer than 24 px 98 / 647 / 141 (zoom in / out / fresh tree) against 940 / 1772 / 866 before the clearance rule (it was 94 / 709 / 87 as the limits stood: the limits for "in" and "fresh" went from a tenth to a fifth), pairs under 12 px 2 / 76 / 0, overlapping 0 / 1 / 0, slot changes under jitter 27 and 15, nodes that come and go under jitter 76 / 74 (limit 1.7 x before, was 1.5), and "zooming in takes nothing away" 6 events over 14 views x 650 steps (limit 6, was 3 with 2 measured: a group closes at Maghreb 3.94, a place at the same zoom, others at 5.02, 5.44, 5.5, 7.71). That is the cost of the alignment; reported to the owner as such.

*The floor box (4).* Footprint 9 cells for the cut, drawn floor 5 cells, closed rectangle at the floor opening continuously into corners and dashes at twice the floor, a pointer target of at least 24 px: "Minimum size and mask" and "Box outline" above. Checks: `lod-tree.test.ts` (the drawn rectangle is concentric with the footprint, at its floor, inside it at every zoom), `pixel-labels.test.ts` (openness monotone and continuous in the size, the gap non-decreasing in whole cells, a closed ring at 0 for any size and strip, no pop on a growing box, selected solid at every openness; flush text per slot, `selectedShift`), `hit-area.test.ts` (`minTargetBox`), `label-plan.test.ts` (flush text, reserved card), `groups.mjs pixels` (the lit cells follow the scene's own arm and gap) and `groups.mjs targets` (the text of every label is on its box's edge, within half a device pixel).

**Inertia and flicker** (the spring described here was replaced on 2026-10-12 by a stepped glide on the art-pixel lattice and on 2026-10-13 by a continuous eased glide, see "Labels move smoothly" above; the metric and the sweeps below are as measured with the spring). Mechanics: "Targets and labels" above. Owner feedback (2026-10-08): "The labels wait until everything settles to move, so they seem to snap into place, and sometimes way too fast. Could they have inertia so they move all the time, smoothly, and arrive at their final position?" The earlier version froze the slots while the camera moved, re-planned after 160 ms of stillness and moved the labels that changed with a 150 ms CSS transition: no hops during the motion, but everything that had to change did so in one lump at the end, with a short, fast move. Now see above: continuous plans, a per-frame spring on the plate's offset from its box. Metric (`label-track.test.ts`, a deterministic sweep of 72 labelled boxes over a swinging pan and breathing zoom, 180 frames then 60 at rest, run four ways): a TELEPORT is a plate that moved in one frame (its nearer edge on each axis, so a text that gets shorter and keeps the edge its plate is anchored on does not count) by more than its box did (its fastest corner) plus 4 px plus what its own glide may move it by (1.1 x omega x dt x the glide left): a slot change that is not glided, or a hop, is a teleport; a glide never is. A CHANGE is a label shown in two consecutive frames with another slot (informative now: a change is a glide). Appearances and disappearances are counted apart (the camera and the cut decide them: 202 for all). Results: the plan on EVERY frame with the pixel version's own distances: 219 slot changes (3.04 per label), 81 teleports; the continuous tracked plan with its spring replaced by an infinitely stiff one (a slot change snaps: what the labels did on a re-plan): 121 changes, 64 teleports (so the metric sees a snap); reduced motion (plans only at rest, no glide): 0 changes while moving, 0 teleports; the tracked plan with the glide: 121 slot changes (1.68 per label, 13 labels in the worst frame, a crowded sweep), 0 teleports, the largest step of a plate against its box 4.5 px in a frame, 1755 label-frames gliding (about 20 % of all), the last plate arrives 0.55 s after the camera stops, at rest 0 glides left and every plate exactly at its target, no two plates overlapping. Without the dwell (`dwellMs` 0) the sweep makes 127 changes, with an infinite dwell (only forced changes) 75: the dwell stops swaps back and forth without keeping a label from moving when it must. Unit tests also cover: the spring (exact for any step, monotone, never crosses 0), a slot change as a glide (monotone offset, no overshoot, bounded step per frame, a straight line, 250 to 700 ms, exact arrival, the same position at the same time at 30, 60 and 144 fps), a plan during a glide (towards a place further on: the speed carries on; back to where it came from: no overshoot, no jump), the dwell, `settleNow`, reduced motion. In the browser (`groups.mjs flicker`, the same metric on the real app, a scripted pan and zoom of 181 frames over crowded views of the owner's preview; this machine's rAF runs at 120 Hz, so the sweep lasts 1.5 s, twice as fast as intended): Europe 32 labels 35 changes (1.09 per label) 0 teleports, the Balkans 36 labels 58 changes 0 teleports, the Atlantic / Iberia / Morocco 46 labels (many rural "area" labels in a crowd with the last resort overlap) 103 changes (2.24 per label) 0 teleports; 19 plans in the 1.5 s (bounded: at most one per 100 ms and the plans after the stop); the loop was quiet 0.82 to 0.93 s after the camera stopped; every view ends with a label element for each box, no glide left, none overlapping another. A zoom in on the owner's preview (deterministic frames at 60 Hz, a fake clock) where a place label goes from right-aligned to left-aligned: a city, `aboveRight` to `aboveLeft`, 61 px, the plate's offset from its box goes -61.0, -57.5, -52.8, -47.5, -42.2 ... -8.5 ... -0.3, 0 (steps of 1.5, 3.6, 4.7, 5.3, 5.4 px at most, then decaying; arrival after 533 ms; the box grows during all of it and the plate follows). Hover never re-plans: `groups.mjs flicker` hovers 14 labels one after the other and requires that no other label changes slot, text or place and that no plan runs.

**Flicker check recalibrated (2026-10-09), with the data.** `groups.mjs flicker` failed 7 of 7 runs on the owner's preview (the Balkans 3.27 to 3.37 slot changes per label against a limit of 3, the Atlantic / Iberia / Morocco view 3.00 to 3.08, and one "teleport" in about half the runs). Question: real flicker of the planner, or a stale threshold after the early opening of groups? Measured with a scratch script that logs, per label and per frame, every slot change with the plan, the group changes of that frame and the box (preview, 38 / 41 / 49 labels in the three views): (1) NONE of the 267 slot changes of the Balkans and Atlantic sweeps happened in a frame in which a group opened or closed (0 of 267), and every one of them happened in a frame in which the plan ran (the plan runs every 100 ms; there is no other source of a change); so the "group open or close re-plans the children" explanation does not apply to this sweep, which keeps the zoom within 0.45 levels. (2) With the early opening switched off (quota 9999, the old rule: a group opens when ALL its places fit) the same sweeps give 18 / 22 / 26 labels and 1.6 / 2.7 to 2.9 / 2.9 to 3.0 changes per label; with it, 38 / 41 / 49 labels and 2.3 to 2.6 / 3.2 to 3.4 / 2.9 to 3.2. So the planner is the same, the average already sat at 2.7 to 3.0 in the two crowded views before the early opening (a limit of 3 had no margin there), and a view with twice the labels in the same area makes about 0.3 to 0.7 more slot changes per label (more plates to dodge). (3) The part that is more than density is the WOBBLE: a label going back to the slot it left less than a dwell (350 ms) before, 5 to 8 % of the changes with the old rule (4 to 5 of 61 to 79), 9 to 14 % with the early opening (12 to 17 of 126 to 132), and changes less than 350 ms after the same label's previous one 25 to 30 % with the old rule, 33 to 40 % now. A longer dwell (700 ms) trims it by 8 to 13 % only (the changes that remain are forced ones: a slot that stops being free, a neighbour that moved into it, a text that has to be shortened; probably the greedy plan hands a forced move on to the next lower label; not traced further), so it was not taken. Not a defect that a one-line planner change removes; a global plan with a cost for every change would, and was not attempted. (4) The sweep itself was frame-driven (181 frames): at 120 Hz it took 1.5 s with 19 plans and at 60 Hz 3 s with 33 plans, about half the slot changes per label for the same code; the earlier numbers of this section (1.09 / 2.24 per label) were taken at 120 Hz. It is a function of time now (3 s whatever the display rate).

Decision: recalibrate, with the numbers above. Limits of the check: slot changes per label on average below 4 (measured 2.3 to 3.4 on the preview; the 72-label synthetic sweep of `label-track.test.ts` stays the strict reference at 1.68), at most 25 % of the changes go back to the slot just left (measured 5 to 14 %, the old rule 5 to 8 %), no frame changes more than max(16, 55 % of the labels) (measured 7 to 13), teleports 0 with 6 px of slack instead of 4. The slack: the one "teleport" in the failing runs was an area label (a long name, in the Moroccan crowd) whose text was shortened in a plan (270 px to 72 px wide, slot `0/0` to `20/0`) while its large box moved 30 px in that frame: its left edge moved 34.5 px against the box's 30 + 4 allowed, a 0.5 px excess from the box being snapped to art cells (2.5 to 3 px) and the plate to device pixels; it appeared in 0 to 1 of the three sweeps from run to run on the same code. A hop of a plate is tens of px (52 px for the every-frame plan in `label-track.test.ts`), so 6 px still sees it. Result: 5 of 5 runs pass (Europe 2.3 to 2.4 per label, the Balkans 3.3, the Atlantic 2.9 to 3.1; 5 to 14 reversals of 87 to 154 changes; 0 teleports). On the demo content the check is not meaningful (6 to 8 labels in a view: the average swings with 2 labels and the Atlantic view has 2 boxes), as before.


**Country in labels: why one place said its country and its neighbour did not.** The text of a label was composed in `LodTree` by `placeLabelTexts` (`engine/country-names.ts`): the country was appended only to a place that is the ONLY place of its country in the whole projection. One place was the only one of its country in the preview (1 place), the other had a second place of its country next to it (2 places): the rule, not the data, made the difference. The data is clean: the editorial files (`catalyst-content/editorial/places/<slug>.json`) store the plain name from the Polarsteps step (no country in it) and the preview projection has a `countryCode` for them (146 of 146 places have a code, no name contains a comma). The rule is gone: the country is the first part of the second line of EVERY place whose code is known (it was shown only on hover, focus or selection between 2026-10-08 and the two-line label). `stripCountry(name, code)` (`country-names.ts`, used by `lib/projection.ts` `toGlobePlace`) removes a trailing ", <own country>" from a name that already carries it ("Lisbon, Portugal" with `PT`; case-insensitive on the country; another country, a different position or no code leave the name alone), for the globe only: the list, the page and the content are untouched. A normalisation in the private content repo (names without the country, the code in `countryCode`) would be cleaner than the display-side strip.

### Opening a group into its most important children (2026-10-08)

Owner, on seeing the peeks (a first answer to "Western Europe stays a group with nothing visible inside until you zoom drastically onto France and it nearly occupies half the screen"): "I can see both the Western Europe bounding box and the cities inside it visible at the same time. That is confusing: a group is supposed to open up and show its cities. It seems to be a sync issue: when the parent bounding box fades out, the cities inside it must fade in. This is really important." The peeks (places drawn INSIDE a closed group) are removed, `LodTree.peekPass`, `LOD.peek`, `peeksToDrop`, the host and ban machinery and the `peek` flag of the debug hooks with them; their ranking, gap and budget measurements, hysteresis helpers and sweep metrics live on below. The rule now: a group opens EARLY into the most important of its children that fit, its box and label fade out exactly as those children fade in, and the rest of its children come in as the zoom goes on. The measured cause of the owner's complaint (a group closed until its box was 60 % of the screen: the children are 7 to 30 km apart, their labels cannot fit, and lowering the thresholds only moves the clutter, 3x to 8x more overlapping pairs) is unchanged; it is the all-or-nothing cut that goes.

**The rule** (`LodTree.cut`, `engine/lod-tree.ts`). A closed group OPENS when its first `LOD.open.quota` (2; all of them when it has fewer) children, in order of importance, can all be drawn, each as the box and the label it would carry, with the plate at one of the planner's own positions (above and below on each side, right, left; the whole label), `gapPx.enter` (8 px, the larger of the gaps along x and y) clear of every other drawn box and label, and the screen under its box budget. When it opens ONLY the children that pass that test are drawn, the others stay hidden (still counted in the group's total, "6 places") and come in one by one as the zoom lets them in, until the group's box covers `boxMaxTo` of the screen and the size rule shows all of them (as before). Importance is `labelPriority`, then the true size of the place in km, then the slug (camera independent, so the result never depends on the order of the input); a child group is as important as the most important place below it, and it is a DRAWABLE UNIT judged in this order (`LodTree.tryKid`): (1) it opens into its own top two children when they fit, else (2) its own box is taken when it fits, else (3) it opens THROUGH, replaced by its single most important unit, recursively to any depth (a nested area is replaced by its place, or by the smaller area inside it, and that by its place). With the multi-scale hierarchy (a region group > an area group > places) the third step is what makes the early opening work: an area's box is small but its label long (an "<name> area" label is 124 px wide against the 55 px of "Paris"), so the box of France's second child did not fit next to Iberia's box, France could not open, and Western Europe, whose top two children are France and a place of a neighbouring country, stayed closed until 5.7 (the size rule: 5.73). Now France opens through Paris (and a second area when it fits) and Western Europe opens at 3.69 (3.5 in the browser). Only the first of a through group's children is required (a unit is one thing on the screen); the rest come in as extras. A closed group that is the top of a trial still needs two. (Zoom 3.6 Iberia shows Portugal's box with two Spanish cities, Spain's box is never drawn; France's box is never drawn when Paris and a second place fit.) The first two must be taken together: a group never opens leaving out its most important child for lesser ones. The old size trigger stays as the upper bound (a group is always open by the time its box covers 60 % of the smaller side of the free area, `boxMaxFrom` 0.45 to close again, every group from zoom 13), and the old nearest-gap trigger (`sepPx`, `sepClosedPx`) is gone: it is a special case of the rule. A group with one visible child is that child's rectangle, as before. A place that is selected, focused or a stop of a shown route opens every group above it (`mustOpen`, even when its children found no room), so no group is ever drawn together with a place below it, at rest, with no exemption.

**Open through: hysteresis** (the same two sweeps; the rules that keep a through group from flapping). (a) A group that is open keeps its incumbents' LEAVE tests and is no longer asked whether its box could hold two boxes (`holds` is a newcomer's test: a box growing across 48 px flapped a group open at Iberia 4.75). (b) A group that was open (also through) is not traded for its own box: it goes back to the box only when the camera is zooming OUT, is `stickyZoom` (0.4) below the zoom it opened at, and the box fits with the newcomer's 8 px (`LodTree.stayThrough`; with the leave test at 3 px for staying a box, a box that just fits cannot flip it; the first version merged back after 0.003 of zoom jitter, a city's group at Europe 4.30, and on a zoom-in in the browser check, balkans at 3.80). A group opened by the size rule keeps its through children the same way (`present`). (c) A plate must be on the canvas: a box that touches the canvas only takes a plate position that is inside it (the planner has no other), which removed a plate over a neighbour at the bottom edge (Andes 5.5).

**Two sweeps, so that what is drawn is never displaced by what is new.** The cut is a depth-first walk in importance order with a trial per group (obstacles, plan and budget are rolled back when a trial fails): (1) KEEP, the groups that were open and the extras (the children after the first two) that were drawn in the last evaluation, with the LEAVE thresholds; then (2) GROW, the groups that are boxes try to open with the ENTER thresholds (most important first, the groups that just opened tried in the next round), then the other children are added in importance order across the whole screen (a less important child never takes the place of a more important one in the group next to it). The decision of a group never depends on a group decided after it, and the extras never decide whether a group opens.

**Parameters** (`LOD.open`, one table; every length in CSS px):

| What | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Quota | 2 children (or all of them when it has fewer) |
| Clear gap to every other box and label | 8 to take a child (`gapPx.enter`), 3 to keep it (`leave`); the label's plate is clear by the same |
| Box budget | 30 per 1440 x 900 free area, scaled by the free area, 8 to 60 (`budget`, `budgetMin`, `budgetMax`); a kept child may go up to `budget + budgetKeep` (4). It limits what the early opening adds: the size rule opens its groups with all their children, budget or not |
| Zoom hysteresis | a drawn child is kept WITHOUT a geometry test while the zoom is at most `stickyZoom` (0.4) below the zoom it came in at (the true extents of two neighbours grow into each other faster than the gap grows, so a test at every frame would take places away on a zoom-in); below that it keeps while it passes the LEAVE test |
| Judged | groups whose box is within `judgeMarginPx` (160) of the free area and can hold two boxes (`2 * minBoxCells + gapPx.leave`); a child off the screen is judged like any other (the screen only counts for the budget), so a box that pans in is already there |
| Candidates | at most `maxTried` (64) children per group |

**Hysteresis, per number.** Gap 8 / 3, budget 30 / 34, zoom 0.4, and the size rule's own band 0.45 / 0.6. Zooming in never takes a node away (a place stays, a group box goes only by opening), checked on the real tree over every centre and group, 0.01 of zoom at a time; zooming out, a group merges back once its places no longer pass the LEAVE test below the sticky zoom. The label position an incumbent took is tried first (`plateCand`), and while the incumbents are judged their boxes are reserved against each other's plates, which removed the two flaps the stress runs of the peeks found.

**The cross-fade, in lockstep** (owner: "when the parent bounding box fades out, the cities inside it must fade in"). The group's box and label and the children it opens into are retargeted in the same `update` (`LodTree.retarget`), so they start in the same frame and run on one clock: the node's `FadeArray` value, `FADE_MS` (200 ms), the same smoothstep of a linear progress, instant for reduced motion. The group goes 1 -> 0 exactly as its children go 0 -> 1; the opacity of a group plus that of any node below it is at most 1 all along (a child that starts later only lags; a reversal turns both around from where they are). The tests prove it with a scripted opening, a reversal, and a random camera walk with random frame times (4000 steps on a synthetic deep tree, 6000 on the preview), the pair sum never above 1 + 1e-6, with a negative control that the checker does see two nodes at full opacity. The label layer follows the same state: `BoxScene` draws the box, its mask and the label at `lod.alpha` in EVERY state (a hovered, focused or selected node used to be drawn at 1 whatever its fade), a label has no CSS opacity transition (only its position glides), and the browser check reads the CSS opacity of the label elements against the nodes' (largest difference 0.008, the quantum 1/64). Hit areas: a node on its way out is a click target only while it is at least `LOD.pickFadingMin` (0.5) opaque, a node on its way in from `pickAlphaMin` (0.3), and the smallest target wins, so a fading group never steals a click from the children that replace it (`pickAlpha`, `hit-area.test.ts`).

**Label planner.** The opening test reserves a plate position that is clear of every other BOX and label by 8 px, but the planner picks its positions by score and only looked at plates, so a label could land on a neighbour's outline (the cause of most of the overlapping pairs the first version of this rule produced). `PlanItem.avoid` (kept from the peeks, now for every node) lists the boxes of the other nodes within reach of a plate (`boxesToAvoid`, 3 px wider, built only for the nodes that are planned); a position that touches one comes AFTER every position that does not (2026-10-12: of every wording, not only of the same one; before, it was taken rather than shortening the label) and the whole wording's first such position is taken only when no wording has a clear one (so the number of shortened labels and of labels drawn over another as the last resort does not rise: `groups.mjs targets` on the preview, 288 views, 100 % of the boxes labelled: size rule alone 620 boxes, 10 shortened, 7 last resorts; early opening 1234 boxes, 31 shortened, 9 last resorts, labels nested in their box 85 -> 29; re-run on the multi-scale hierarchy on 2026-10-09: 1360 boxes, 41 shortened, 15 last resorts, 17 nested, 100 % labelled: more nodes, and areas with long labels).

**Measured** (the owner's preview, 146 places, 1440 x 900 free area; the opening zoom of `groups.mjs open` in a real browser, a slow zoom in steps of 0.05 at rest, with the app's label metrics; before = the size rule alone, which is the old behaviour to within 0.01 of zoom):

| Group (view centred on it, a fresh page) | Opens at zoom, size rule alone to now | Places drawn at the opening (of the total) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Western Europe | 5.73 to 3.50 | 3 of 6 |
| Balkans | 5.50 to 3.30 | 3 of 32 |
| Iberia | 5.40 to 4.05 | 4 of 11 |
| Central Europe | 6.02 to 3.30 | 1 of 6 (through its most important city), 2 a third of a level later |
| Italy | 6.99 to 4.30 | 2 of 3 |
| Morocco (country) | 4.69 to 3.50 | 3 of 21 |
| A rural region of Morocco (area in area) | 7.61 to 3.50 | 1 of 5 (its most important place; the group's box does not fit) |
| An area of Morocco (southern, 4 places) | 7.61 to 3.80 | 2 of 4 |

Re-measured on 2026-10-09 on the multi-scale hierarchy of the private export (73 groups, areas nested to depth 3 or more: a region group > an area group > places); the browser now sweeps each group on a FRESH page, because a group's opening depends on history and a sweep that starts where the last one ended measures that history. Before the open-through step Western Europe opened at 5.67 (the size rule 5.73: no early opening at all), the Balkans at 3.42, Iberia at 5.40, Central Europe at 3.32, Italy at 5.74; the cause is in "The rule" above (France's second unit was an area whose long label did not fit, so France, and with it Western Europe, could not open).

The unit sweeps (`open.test.ts`, label sizes estimated, zoom 2 to 9 in steps of 0.01, a fresh tree per group; size rule alone to now; `open-sweep.ts` `SWEEP_GROUPS`, `sweepAreasOf`: the nested areas of one country, read from the projection): Western Europe 5.73 to 3.69 (3 of 6 places, boxes on screen 15 to 12, overlapping pairs 5 to 0), Balkans 5.50 to 3.30 (3 of 32; 15 to 8; 8 to 1), Iberia 5.40 to 4.05 (4 of 11; 9 to 26; 2 to 2), Central Europe 6.02 to 3.32 (2 of 6; 12 to 7; 2 to 0), Italy 6.99 to 4.30 (2 of 3; 6 to 28; 0 to 0), Morocco 4.69 to 3.53 (3 of 21; 12 to 18; 3 to 1), a rural region of Morocco 7.61 to 3.47 (1 of 5; 6 to 15; 0 to 0), a coastal-city area 7.79 to 6.82 (3 of 3; 3 to 10; 0 to 0), a southern area 7.61 to 3.80 (2 of 4; 4 to 19; 0 to 2), a small rural region 7.61 to 5.30 (2 of 4; 4 to 17; 0 to 2). Over six view centres (Western Europe, Europe, Iberia, the Balkans, the Andes, the Maghreb) and 29 zooms each (2 to 9 in steps of 0.25), the size rule alone to the early opening: boxes on screen, mean 5.2 to 10.6, 5.1 to 10.4, 4.1 to 10.7, 7.3 to 15.0, 4.2 to 8.5, 4.5 to 9.3 (the most at any view 30, the budget; before 15); free label room (share of 100 x 18 probe plates on a 20 px lattice touching no plate and no box outline) 0.867 to 0.880, 0.869 to 0.886, 0.868 to 0.890, 0.802 to 0.825, 0.904 to 0.904, 0.873 to 0.886; overlapping pairs (box over box, plate over box, plate over plate; real planner) mean 1.14 to 0.55, 1.38 to 0.38, 1.14 to 0.41, 2.52 to 0.90, 1.38 to 0.24, 0.97 to 0.52, 247 pairs over the 174 views to 87 (7 views with more pairs than the size rule alone: a plate of a long area label against the boxes of its neighbours at the Maghreb, two incumbents whose true boxes have grown into each other at Western Europe 4 and 5.75; the test allows 5 %, 9 views). The first run on the new data failed with "15 views more" (a count of views with MORE overlapping pairs than the size rule alone, not the budget: the most boxes at any view was 30 all along). Before the early opening each of these groups was one box with nothing in it for one to two zoom levels.

**Tests.** `open.test.ts`: synthetic country of six places (opens as soon as the two most important fit, only those that fit are drawn, the box 8 px clear of everything when they come in; the rest come in and none goes away on a zoom-in; the first two are BOTH there, a blocked top place keeps the group closed although the others fit; fewer than two children; importance order and input order independence; a child group opens by the same rule, and a child group that does not fit as a box opens into its own places; nested areas (country > area > area > places, long names) open THROUGH, to any depth, with a negative control (without the through step the test fails); every group kind; the size rule shows all places; jitter in zoom and position never flaps; it closes later than it opens; the budget and its scaling with the free area); the cross-fade (lockstep, reversal, reduced motion, random walks, the negative control); and on the owner's preview, skipped without the git-ignored file: the six-centre sweep (boxes within the budget, fewer overlapping pairs, free room), the five groups open at least half a level earlier with two or more places, the Moroccan areas (area in area) at least half a level earlier with one or more, Western Europe is never drawn with a place of it and two or more of its places are drawn once its box is gone, at rest no node has a drawn ancestor and every node is fully on or off, zooming in never closes a group or takes a place away, determinism against a shuffled input, jitter stability over every sweep view (`OPEN_STRESS=1`: 16 times more views), a random walk of 6000 steps on the real tree. `lod-tree.test.ts` has the strict invariant back (no node drawn together with a drawn ancestor, no exemption) and the selected place / route stop under a closed group; `hit-area.test.ts` the pick rule of a fading group; `label-plan.test.ts` `avoid`. Browser, `groups.mjs open` (the preview): 856 resting frames over eight groups (the five, Morocco and two of its areas), each on a fresh page, no node with a drawn ancestor, every box fully drawn with its label element opaque, no plate over another, at most 33 boxes on screen; the zoom-in walk of every view is reached by zooming out from the last one (a jump leaves nodes half-decided); a scripted zoom through the opening of Western Europe with the clock running, the pair sum 1.000 at every sampled frame, the cross-fade seen mid-way, the label elements within 0.008 of the nodes' opacity, a click on a child that is fading in never picks the group fading out; a zoom on the way in takes nothing on the screen away except by opening its group. The other modes of `groups.mjs` (`targets`, `at-rest`, `flicker`, `timed`, `idle`, `pick`, `reduced`; `lod`, `cases`, `pixels`, `empty` on the demo) and `sky.mjs` pass.

**Not verified** (open through, 2026-10-09): (`groups.mjs flicker` is settled: see "Flicker check recalibrated (2026-10-09)" under "Inertia and flicker", 5 of 5 runs on the preview;) a group left open through one place at the world view when its box cannot fit with the newcomer's gap (Western Europe at the minimum zoom of the browser, reached by zooming out from 7.5: Paris, a German city and an area of France instead of the box; a fresh page shows the box when it fits); the cost of the through trials (not run, no performance run asked; the trial is one more `fits` per nested group). Also: Safari/iOS, a real touch screen and phones (the budget scales to 8 boxes on a 390 px wide free area, by arithmetic; not driven), the cost on a real device (the CPU numbers above are Node on the laptop; no GPU or phone performance run), the street scale and `groups.mjs handover` (they need the local tile server; the shared `BoxScene` path is the same), the label widths of other fonts than the app's monospace stack, content other than the owner's preview (the Western Europe of a real publication has other places and priorities). An opening depends on history (a group is tried against what was drawn when it was tried, and a drawn node is kept): the same zoom reached by a jump, a slow zoom and a fine one can differ by up to about a level for a group that sits next to others (Western Europe 3.5 to 4.25 in the two kinds of sweep above).

### Room between the nodes (2026-10-12)

The owner's complaint: labels crowd and look erratic, and a region or country should take over from its cities sooner. The cut therefore
asks for **room**, not only for fit. A child is taken when its box and its label plate keep `LOD.open.gapPx.enter` (24 px; the plate
`plateGapRatio` of it) from every other drawn box and plate, and kept down to `leave` (12 px). The clearance relaxes with the zoom
(`gapRelaxedPx`, 8 and 3 px from zoom 6.5 to 9) and is not asked from `crowdOffZoom` (10), where a cluster of overlapping places is drawn as
it is. A group that does not fit as a box and cannot open into two children is replaced by the first of its `throughTop` (3) most important
children that fits, the others come in as extras. Places whose own boxes overlap are not asked for room between them.

- **The label planner follows the cut**: the cut reserves a position for each box's label (`LodTree.plateSlot`), the planner takes it
  first (`PlanItem.pref`), so the labels keep the room the cut checked, and a label wanted again glides from where it was.
- **Trade-off**: more room means some children come in later than before the rule. Opening zoom of the second node of each group on the
  owner's preview, against its value before the rule (`groups.mjs open`): Western Europe 3.80 (+0.30), Central Europe 3.35 (+0.05),
  Italy 4.80 (+0.50), Morocco 3.70 (+0.20), a rural region of Morocco 5.10 (+1.6) and a southern area of Morocco 5.35 (+1.55): the last two are dense
  clusters of nearby places that cannot keep 24 px between plates any earlier. Earlier than before: the Balkans 2.75 (-0.55) and Iberia
  3.30 (-0.75), where a country now stands in for the group sooner (one node from zoom 2.20 and 3.20). If a region looks late, lower
  `gapPx` rather than special-casing it.
- **Checks**: `groups.mjs open` pins the opening zooms against their values before the rule; `groups.mjs crowd` zooms in and out on six view
  centres and counts overlaps and pairs under 12 and 24 px (preview: 0 overlaps, 0.27 pairs per view under 12 px, 2.65 under 24 px).
- Not kept: a "wish" mechanism (a refused important node reserving room against its lesser neighbours over several runs of the cut) was
  tried and dropped; it added about 500 lines for the two worst regions.

### Checks of the boxes (`scripts/globe/groups.mjs`, `markers.mjs`)

`idle-spin.mjs [start|cadence|input|coast|blocked|reduced|hidden|flags|mobile]` checks the idle rotation (above; any content). `groups.mjs [lod|cases|pixels|empty|timed|at-rest|reduced|pick|targets|flicker|open|cost|idle|handover|shots]` (demo content: `pnpm dev:demo`, `lod`, `cases` and `handover` need it; `targets` and `at-rest` run on any content, your preview included; `handover` also
needs the local tile server) checks the cut (`open`: the early opening of "Opening a group into its most important children" above, needs groups of several places, your preview included; `flicker`: a scripted pan and zoom, the slot changes and jumps of the labels, one plan when the camera stops, hover re-plans nothing; at rest every node fully drawn, exactly one level per branch, 10 close places = one box
that opens, a lone place, two distant places, the timed transitions), the pixels (every outline cell present, only palette colours), reduced motion,
picking (border and label plate yes, interior no), the label rule (`targets`: at rest 100 % of the drawn boxes have a label on a sweep of 288 views of the preview and of the demo), cost, idle (0 frames, 0 canvas draws) and that the globe and the street overlay
draw the same boxes in the same tones at one camera. `SHOTS=1 ... groups.mjs shots` writes `docs/screenshots/clusters-world-{light,dark}.png`
and `clusters-opening.png` (a cluster opening through a zoom). `markers.mjs [quick]` is the whole-or-nothing sweep for rectangles:
thousands of views rotating every place across the limb, at overview, mid and high zoom, desktop and phone, nothing selected and
selected: every shown outline complete, nothing on the canvas outside the shown boxes and label plates, the far side never drawn,
every box centred on the cell the place projects to. The places are those of the SERVED projection (read from the app's own loader data, so the owner's preview works as well as the demo; it used to read `fixtures/demo.json` and crashed on preview slugs the engine does not know: `d.project(slug)` is null for them, which the check now skips); `quick` walks 24 evenly spaced places to the limb when there are more (the preview has 146) and selects `reykjavik`, else the most important place (2026-10-09: `quick` passes on demo, 3 runs of 3348 views, and on the preview, 3 runs of 3816; the full run on the preview, about 90,000 views, was not run).

## Reload fade (direct load of a place)

A direct load or reload of `/locations/:slug` used to show the whole planet for a moment and then jump to the framed city.
Now the first paint is the page colour only (black in the dark theme, `--background` in the light one), and the stage
fades in once the final framed view is drawn. No camera motion, no layout shift.

- `Globe` renders nothing on the server and on the first client render; for a fit view (`isFitView(initialView)`, i.e. a
  direct load of a place or of the home page: `startsVeiled(initialView)`) the `Suspense` fallback is `null` and there is no
  placeholder of any kind (the old full-resolution disc outline, shown for a few frames before the pixel-art globe was ready,
  is gone), and `GlobeCanvas` renders its root with `opacity: 0` (`veil`, decided on the first render, so the server markup and
  the hydrated one agree). The WebGL canvas is itself created with `opacity: 0` and only made visible by the first frame it
  draws (`renderer.renderNow`), so an undrawn (black) or unfinished canvas can never be presented whatever the stage does.
  Only a saved view (the globe coming back from the mobile slide-over) is not veiled.
- The handover controller calls `onReveal` ONCE (`tryReveal`): when the street map has been cut in (a start at street
  scale: `restoring`, `shown >= 1`), or at the globe's first frame when the start needs no street map; a start that needs
  the street map waits at most `HANDOVER.revealWaitMs` (1500 ms), then reveals whichever correct frame exists (the globe at
  its own maximum, centred on the place); if the street map becomes ready later, the usual cut applies. A dead street
  map (no tiles, failure) reveals the globe at once. WebGL unavailable also reveals (the status message must show).
- The fade is `opacity` over `HANDOVER.fadeInMs` (500 ms, `--ease-standard`); under reduced motion there is no
  transition (instant). The place panel is untouched.
- Check: `scripts/globe/first-frames.mjs [path] [scheme]` samples every animation frame of a load of the home page: no
  placeholder or loading element at any frame, the stage and the WebGL canvas never on screen before the first drawn frame,
  and the globe does appear (also with reduced motion). `scripts/globe/reload-fade.mjs` samples every animation frame from navigation: first paint transparent; never
  visible away from the final framing; no camera motion; 450 ms fade (instant with reduced motion); the final frame is the
  street map; with the street chunk delayed by 4 s the globe frame is revealed after the wait (1.9 s), not before.
  Measured on the M4 (local PMTiles): reveal at 0.62 to 0.65 s after navigation.

## Production globe measurements

Environment: Apple M4 (macOS 27.0.1), Chrome for Testing 153.0.8010.12, headless, ANGLE Metal
(`ANGLE (Apple, ANGLE Metal Renderer: Apple M4)`), 60 Hz, WebGL2. Production build served by `react-router-serve`
with `CATALYST_CONTENT=demo` (11 places, 1 route). The "mobile" run is the same desktop GPU with a 390x844
viewport at DPR 3, touch and an iPhone user agent: **emulation, not a phone**. A fast desktop GPU at 60 Hz
vsync hits the cap, so the informative numbers are the JS and GPU-sync times, not fps. Not Safari.

Scenario: scripted continuous rotation (36 deg/s) plus a zoom oscillation across the border threshold, one view
change and synchronous render per rAF, 1.5 s warm-up then 10 s measured (600 frames). Times in ms, p50 / p95 / max.

| Viewport (drawing buffer) | fps | rAF interval | `renderer.render` JS | Render + labels JS | Render + labels + 1px `readPixels` (GPU sync) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1440x900 @2x (480x300) | 60 | 16.7 / 16.7 / 16.8 | 0.2 / 0.3 / 0.6 | 0.4 / 0.5 / 1.2 | 2.3 / 2.5 / 3.0 |
| 1440x900 @2x, panel open (inset 720, scissor on; historical) | 60 | 16.7 / 16.8 / 16.8 | 0.2 / 0.3 / 0.6 | 0.4 / 0.5 / 1.2 | 2.0 / 2.3 / 2.6 (second run 1.8 / 2.8 / 4.4) |
| 1440x900 @2x, panel open, scissor off (the shipped state) | 60 | 16.7 / 16.8 / 16.8 | 0.2 / 0.3 / 0.4 | 0.3 / 0.4 / 0.5 | 2.0 / 2.3 / 5.4 |
| 390x844 @3x, emulated (195x422) | 60 | 16.7 / 16.7 / 16.8 | 0.2 / 0.3 / 0.6 | 0.4 / 0.5 / 1.2 | 2.0 / 2.9 / 4.5 |

(Historical, the reason the scissor was removed.) The scissor changes nothing measurable on this GPU: the GPU-synced time with the panel open is 2.0 ms p50 with it
and without it (run to run noise is larger than the difference). The buffer is only 480x300 and the scene is
vertex-bound, so skipping the covered strip saves almost no fragment work. The panel itself adds the CSS cost of a
backdrop blur over the canvas, which does not show in these numbers (they time the WebGL path only) and which only
matters while the canvas repaints, since an idle page does no work.

Draw calls per frame: 7 (disc, graticule, borders, coastlines, route, markers, outline); 9,024 triangles
(the disc) and 13,557 line segments at zoom 3.6. JS heap about 8.5 to 11.5 MB. These match the prototype
(`docs/renderer-decision.md`: 0.3 / 0.4 / 0.6 ms JS, 2.3 / 3.0 / 5.5 ms GPU sync) within noise.

Measured behaviour (same build, same environment):

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Idle, 4 s without input (also after moving the pointer) | 0 rAF calls, 0 `gl.clear` (no frames), `isAnimating() === false` (measured in debug mode; a visitor's page starts the idle rotation after 8 s, see "Idle rotation") |
| Drag with fling, then rest | frames while flinging, then 0 rAF over the next 1.5 s |
| Route draw-on | animating while drawing; idle again after it ends |
| Reduced motion (`emulateMedia`) | selecting Hanoi landed on its exact coordinates in under two frames, `isAnimating() === false`, 0 rAF over 2.5 s, route static |
| Rotate to place | places list (keyboard) on Kyoto: view ends at `135.77, 35.01`, zoom 3.2; about 60 frames for the flight |
| Far side | at `lon 100, lat 10`, 7 of 11 places report `visible: false`; clicking each of them navigated nowhere; labels shown: Kyoto only |
| Context loss | `WEBGL_lose_context`: status message shown, 0 rAF while lost; after restore `data-state="ready"`, same view, correct background (screenshot) |
| `visibilitychange` | hidden: 0 rAF, 0 frames after a view change; shown: 1 repaint |
| No WebGL | status message, 0 canvases, 11 list links, the list is visible (288 px wide) and navigates, no console output |
| Mobile unmount | tapping a marker opened the dialog and removed the canvas (live contexts 0); closing restored the globe with the identical view (`2.35, 48.86, zoom 4`) |
| 20 open/close cycles (mobile) | WebGL contexts created 23 / lost 22 (live 1), canvases in the DOM 1, view unchanged; 23 = 1 WebGL probe (lost at once) + 22 globes (initial, one restore, 20 cycles) |
| Panel, direct load (`/locations/kyoto`, 1440x900) | inset 720, centre shift 120 buffer px, Kyoto drawn at (362, 452) CSS px: the middle of the left half is (360, 450); the 2 px offset is the snap to the 3 px art grid |
| Panel opening from `/` | inset ramps 0, 390, 607, 698, 720 at 0, 88, 173, 257, 343 ms while the panel's left edge goes 1440, 1052, 834, 742, 720: the two sum to 1440 throughout |
| Panel closing | inset back to 0, Kyoto returns to (722, 452), the middle of the full width |
| Panel, reduced motion | after two frames: inset 720 at once and the panel at x = 720 (no animation) |
| Panel, idle | 0 rAF over 2.5 s with the panel open |
| Panel, picking | a click at the marker's shifted position keeps the place selected (no navigation elsewhere) |
| Keyboard flow | Tab order (measured before 2026-10-13, when the first item was still called "Catalyst"; it is "Globe" now): Skip to content, Skip to places, Catalyst (home), Projects (now Software), Articles, Artworks, Poetry, Search, then the places (list revealed, 288x528 px); Enter on Reykjavík opens it and focus moves to its heading (list hidden again); Escape closes and focus returns to its link (list revealed); tabbing out hides it |
| Reduced transparency | with `prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce` (CDP) the blur layers are `display: none` and the gradient is the longer solid one |
| Mobile (390x844) | panel is a modal dialog (`aria-modal`, body scroll locked, focus on the heading), globe unmounted |
| Console | no errors or warnings in any scenario, no hydration warnings (production and dev server) |
| Touch (CDP touch events) | one-finger drag rotates, pinch out zooms in, pinch in stops at the minimum zoom, tap on a marker selects; the places list and the globe root keep `touch-action: auto` |
| Built-in browser pane (Chrome 152, pane hidden) | globe renders, marker click opens `/locations/paris`; `document.hidden` was true, and the globe correctly produced no frames |

Not verified: Safari / iOS (including `image-rendering: pixelated` on canvas), real phone frame times, thermal
behaviour, a real `devicePixelRatio` change (the emulation does not raise the `resolution` media-query
event; the watcher is unit-tested with a fake `matchMedia` and the resize path was checked by changing size and
DPR together: buffer 375x250 at pixel size 3.2), real GPU context loss.

Also not verified for the floating UI: a real screen reader (VoiceOver/NVDA) on the places list, Safari and Firefox rendering of `:has(:focus-visible)`, CSS `mask-image` on a pixelated canvas and progressive `backdrop-filter`, and the cost of the panel's backdrop blur on weak GPUs. The `/articles` scrim screenshot repeats the single demo article in the DOM (test-only) so the page can scroll.

Screenshots (1280x800, demo content): `docs/screenshots/overview-light.png`, `overview-dark.png`, `panel-open.png`, `scrim-articles.png`.

### Marker clipping fix

Bug: markers were depth-tested against the globe disc (radius 0.998; markers at 1.0). A GL point has ONE depth
for all its pixels, while the disc's depth changes across the footprint, and on a surface tilted toward the
camera that change is larger than the 0.002 gap. The pixels on the side nearer the screen centre failed the test, so
markers in the upper half lost their bottom and those in the lower half their top (the 9 px selected ring lost
whole edges), and the cut also disagreed with the CPU test used for labels and picking. Measured on the old build over
about 1,150 views of the 11 demo places, 512 of 805 on-screen markers were missing 1 to 7 of their 9 pixels.

Fix (`engine/visibility.ts`, `marker-layer.ts`, `renderer.ts`): the renderer projects each place on the CPU (the
projection labels and picking already used), snaps it to its art pixel, and decides visibility from the CENTRE only:
front hemisphere and at least `TUNING.markerLimbClearance` (4 art pixels, the half-size of the selected ring) inside the
globe's silhouette, so a drawn marker never reaches the limb. The marker is then drawn at that pixel centre with no
depth test, last of all (after the horizon outline), so nothing can cut it; a hidden one is clipped away entirely.
`project()` returns that same cell and flag, so drawing, label anchors and picking cannot disagree (before, the GPU
point was unsnapped and could sit one pixel off the label anchor). The decision uses the unsnapped centre, so it does not
flicker with pixel snapping, and the markers are placed on a pixel centre, so a 3, 5, 7 or 9 px point always covers its
whole block. Markers are hidden about 12 css px before the limb: earlier than the geometric horizon, later than labels
(which drop at `facing < 0.08`).

Routes had the same cause in a milder form (1 to 3 route pixels missing in 26 of 100 tilted views). They now
skip the depth test and are hidden by an analytic ray-vs-disc test on the centre line (`routeMaterial`), so a dash is
cut across its length or not at all; the pixel set equals the depth-off render in every sampled view and is empty beyond
the horizon. Not changed: the graticule, borders and coastlines (1 px, no footprint), (the GL scissor under the detail panel that
was listed here is gone, see "Stale edge").

Evidence and checks: `docs/screenshots/markers-before.png` / `markers-after.png` (same 9 views, 3x enlarged crops:
centre, north, south, near the limb, selected, high zoom; before and after). `scripts/globe/markers.mjs` draws markers
in pure red/blue (`__globeDebug.setMarkerProbe`) and compares the whole drawing buffer pixel for pixel with the expected
block of every shown marker (and nothing for hidden ones) over 9,252 views per run: full rotations at 7 latitudes and
three zooms, each place walked from 50 to 100 degrees off-centre along four bearings, desktop and 2 px mobile art pixels,
nothing selected and Reykjavik, Cape Town and Hanoi (route stops) selected. Old build (quick mode): 1,440 failures over
2,256 views; new build (full mode): 0 over 46,260 views. Unit tests: `visibility.test.ts`. Frame time unchanged (bench desktop, GPU-synced
p50 2.1 to 2.4 ms before and after, JS render p50 0.2 to 0.3 ms); idle still 0 rAF, 0 `gl.clear`.


## Dev server hygiene (2026-10-21)

- **React Router 8 flags**: `future.v8_middleware`, `v8_splitRouteModules`, `v8_viteEnvironmentApi`, `v8_passThroughRequests` and `v8_trailingSlashAwareDataRequests` are on (`react-router.config.ts`), so the five "Future Flag Warning" notices are gone and the upgrade is a version bump. Only `v8_passThroughRequests` touched the code: a loader now gets the request as it came, so a client-side navigation's data request for `/projects/x` is `/projects/x.data?_routes=...`; the two legacy-redirect loaders (`lib/legacy-paths.ts` `withoutDataRequest`) take the suffix and `_routes` off, or the redirect pointed at `/software/x.data`. No other loader reads the request's address.
- **Chrome DevTools' probe** (`/.well-known/appspecific/com.chrome.devtools.json`) is answered with 204 by a dev-only plugin (`vite.config.ts`): it logged a 404 with a stack trace each time.
- **Dependencies are pre-bundled** (`optimizeDeps.include`: the lazily imported ones, `three`, `maplibre-gl`, `pmtiles`, `motion/react`, ...): found on demand, each wave re-optimised, reloaded the page and answered the open one with 504 "Outdated Optimize Dep" (a console error on every cold start).

- **Shading, the interlude's ends and the ring (2026-10-23, owner: "make it feel 3D")**: the word's fragment shader (`uHardSide`, `CINEMATIC.shading`) is lit from the upper left of the SCREEN: a wall takes its tone from the way its edge faces (the screen-space gradient of the glyph's coverage, `dFdx`/`dFdy`, taken before the discard) and darkens towards the back of the thickness (`sideShade` 0.8), the face is lighter at the top of the letters than at the foot. SEEN FROM BEHIND the word looked flat (one tone for the plate and the walls, and the walls, long and slanted, lie under the next letter, so only the first letter shows any): the plate has its own tone, the walls round it are set apart from it (darker on a light page, lighter on a dark one: `backWall`), the thickness grows with how far behind the planet the word is (`depthBehind`: perspective makes it all but vanish there; much more than 2x and the slices alias to hatching), and the plate is BEVELLED (four taps of the coverage `bevelPx` device pixels to each side give the nearest edge's direction and closeness; the rim takes the light as a wall does), which is what reads as solid from there. A gesture that cuts the interlude short tells its sound at once (`CaptionMotion.leave` calls `onCinematicEnd`, which fades over `SOUND.fadeCutMs`; the file's own timer used to run on, 20 s of it), and a tab put behind fades it too (a hidden tab keeps playing while its interlude stops drawing). The flat ring is out while the globe is pulled back (`CaptionLayer.ringShare`: out over the first fifth of the pull, back over the last fifth of the way home), and after a cut it stays out for the boxes' arrival (`Renderer.ringCut`). The boxes and labels, placed from the page's own camera (not the pulled-back one), wait until the globe is home before they fade in (`Renderer.overlayHeld`, released in the step when `CaptionLayer.pulledBack` is false), so they never appear where the planet will be.

- **The word is solid (2026-10-23, owner: "full 3D letters, not layers")**: the interlude's word is no longer a picture of text stacked in up to 160 slices; it is a MESH (`engine/cinematic-word.ts`) with a front, a 45 degree front bevel, walls, a back bevel and a back, made from outlines baked from the page's display face at weight 640 (`engine/cinematic-glyphs.ts`, by `scripts/font/bake-word-outlines.mjs`, `pnpm --filter @catalyst/web bake:word`: the word is drawn in the perf scripts' Chromium at 800 px of cap height and traced with marching squares, because fontkit cannot instance a variable woff2). Outer contours and holes are told apart by winding, each outline is mitred inwards by `CINEMATIC.bevelCaps` for the front and back faces (triangulated with `ShapeUtils`), walls and chamfers are quads with smooth normals along curves and split ones at corners (35 degrees), and every triangle is wound by its normal so ordinary back-face culling and depth testing apply. A vertex is the strip's own `(u, v, z)`; `EGG_VERT` puts it on the shell exactly as the rows' strips are (the same placement uniforms, so `place` and the sync loop treat it as another variant) and turns its `(east, north, radial)` normal into the camera's. `EGG_FRAG` lights it with a key light from the upper left and a weak fill FIXED IN THE CAMERA'S FRAME (`CINEMATIC.light`, so the word is lit from the same side wherever it is on its orbit) and a faint highlight; the face is white, the front bevel is the BSoD blue outline, the walls and the back are grey (`sideColor`). It is one opaque draw of about ten thousand vertices: the slice budget, its governor, the depth-behind fudge, the bevel-from-coverage shading and the texture it was drawn from are gone. What is hidden is hidden by geometry: from behind, each letter's walls lie under its neighbour, as a real extruded word's do, and what shows is the lit back with its bevel and the walls in the gaps; a camera tilt is the way to show more (not done).

- **All capitals, no bevel on the back (2026-10-23, owner: "UNIVERSAL, less round"; "through the back it looks too bevel-y")**: the word is `UNIVERSAL`, in the same face at weight 700 and the optical size 12 cut (wide and sturdy: the 96 cut is narrower and finer; the lowercase was what read as round), tracked 0.05 em, traced to within 0.0022 cap heights (256 points for the whole word, nine outlines and the holes of the R and the A). The bake script fixes the optical size through the `FontFace` `variationSettings` descriptor, because a canvas sets it from the font size, which at the bake's size is always the finest cut (`pnpm --filter @catalyst/web bake:word UNIVERSAL 700 12`). The BACK has no chamfer: the walls run from the front bevel straight to a flat back at the true outline, with sharp edges (the back bevel I first gave it made the word look rounded from behind).

- **The studio's own sound, a squarer face, tight letters (2026-10-23, owner)**: the sound is the owner's copy of the studio's intro (`public/audio/universal-intro.mp3`, 24.1 s, re-encoded at 112 kbps stereo, 339 KB, with a 0.15 s fade-in; the parody file is gone). Its loudness (RMS per quarter second): quiet strings for 4.5 s, a hit at 5.5 s, a long crescendo and the final chord at 20.25 s (`SOUND.hitSec`), then a decay of about three seconds. The timeline is made to it: the word lands on the chord, 0.5 + 0.4 + 19.35 s in (`enterMs` 19350, so the entrance is a very slow glide), holds 1 s, leaves in 1.45 s during the decay and the globe straightens over the last 1.4 s: `eggMs` 24.1 s, as the file (measured in the browser: lands at 20.1 s, over at 24.15 s). The word's letters come from the site's mono face at weight 800 (JetBrains Mono: straight sides, flat terminals, a squared bowl on the R, a rounded-rectangle S and U; the display sans's capitals read too round and too thin on the R), baked by `scripts/font/bake-word-outlines.mjs` letter by letter: the I is drawn as a plain bar (the mono's has slabs; the stem of the L, a full cap high), and each letter is set so that the nearest points of its outline and the previous letter's are `GAP` 0.085 cap heights apart, ROW BY ROW (a V tucks its arm over the E, an A its foot under the L), which is the tight colour of the studio's wordmark. 210 points for the whole word, 6.2 cap heights wide (the previous face's was 9).

- **Squarer letters and polished metal (2026-10-23, owner: "the reference is squarer and glossier")**: the mono face's capitals are tall and narrow, a studio's nearly square, so the bake draws every letter 1.28 times WIDER than the face does (a canvas stretch) and adds a thin stroke (0.01 cap heights) so that the horizontal bars keep up with the stems (a larger one, 0.03, closed the counters of the R and the A); the word is 7.9 cap heights wide (`pnpm --filter @catalyst/web bake:word UNIVERSAL 800 0.085 1.28`). The material is metal: `EGG_FRAG` mixes the diffuse lighting with a REFLECTION (`CINEMATIC.light.metal` 0.55 of the surface, `reflect` 1.1) of a procedural studio of the reflected direction in the camera's frame (`studio`: a bright sky, a darker band just under the horizon, a dimmer floor, two soft boxes), tinted by the albedo (white, blue and grey metal), plus a sharp highlight (shininess 90). The reflected direction uses the vector to the FRAGMENT (`vView`, not one for the word), so a flat face is a sweep of light and shade, which is what makes it read as polished metal; the first version, with a darker horizon band, made the white faces grey.

- **The interlude's camera flight, first prototype (2026-10-23, branch `feat/cinematic-camera-flight`, stacked on `feat/cinematic-caption-egg`; owner: "follow the reference's camera")**: the word now comes in from the EAST (`CINEMATIC.side` 1) and the camera FLIES (`engine/flight.ts`, a pure function of progress `u`, 0 at the interlude's start, 1 where the word lands: `CaptionMotion.eggFlight`, linear in the clock): from the page's own camera it dives to a camera 0.08 earth radii over Greece, aimed 29 degrees below level at the ground towards Turkey (the horizon in the upper part of the frame: from that height it is 22 degrees below level, so aiming at the ground puts it at the top), a slight right lean, then climbs and pans west over Italy and Spain, looking down more and more, to land exactly on the pose the renderer would draw without a flight (the orbit camera with the interlude's pull-back and lean; a test holds the end and the start equal to `viewBasis`). Keys are cubic Hermite splines over (longitude unwrapped to the short way round, latitude, ln altitude, look point, how far the look goes towards the centre, roll). A pose is a position, a look-at point (`lookK`: 0 the ground, 1 the centre, negative a point above it) and a roll about the line of sight (applied to the local north squared to it, the same sign as `viewBasis`). The renderer (`syncCamera`) uses it when `u` < 1: position and `lookAt` from the pose, the horizon ring from the POSITION (a circle of the sphere seen from there), the sky from the camera's own axes, the caption's canvas window the whole picture (the window round the shell's silhouette assumes the camera is on the axis), the zoom (line density, borders) from the altitude, the near plane from it. A gesture that cuts the interlude short flies the rest at the pace the globe gives its size back (`flightProgress`, to be softened: it compresses the path). The overlay's hold also waits for the flight (`pulledBack`). `__globeDebug.pinFlight(u)` holds the camera at a point of it. FINDING: the globe is vector lines on a plate of the page's colour (coastlines at 110 m, borders at 50 m), so a camera 0.08 radii over the ground sees only a few coast lines and no ground: the opening shot is the weak part (the middle of the flight, over Italy and Spain, reads well). Needs: finer coastlines for the Mediterranean, something for the ground and the horizon.

- **The flight follows the word (2026-10-23, owner's feedback on the first prototype)**: the first version looked at the PLANET and hid the letters. Now the camera looks at the WORD (`engine/flight.ts`: the look is `follow` of the way from the planet's centre to a point of the word, which is `lead` of the way from its middle to its LEADING end, the west one; `CaptionLayer.flightWordAt(u, pageLon)` gives the word's middle and leading end for any progress, from the same timeline the word runs by; at the end `follow` is 0 and it is the page's own camera). The opening: 0.15 earth radii over Greece, the horizon level (the camera's UP is the local VERTICAL, `level` 1, blended to the planet's north by the end: with the north up and the camera looking east the horizon was vertical), pitched 7 degrees down from the leading letter so that the earth fills the bottom of the frame, a slight lean right. The word's start is geographic, not the page's: its leading end begins at `CINEMATIC.leadStartLon` (96 E: the equatorial shell is seen at the horizon's height from Greece at about 110 E), so the U comes over the horizon in the first moments whatever longitude the page is at (`CaptionLayer.sync` works out the start relative to the page's meridian). The word stays anchored to the PAGE's longitude and latitude while the camera flies (`sync` takes the camera's own for what is hidden and for how thick the strip needs to be). Four more fixes: the flat ring is gone at the dive's first moments (`ringShare` takes the flight too); the page's LAYOUT OFFSET (the planet shifted aside and up) is faded out through the middle of the flight and back for the final shot (`flightOffsetShare`: the opening is centred); the planet's DISC takes a tone of its own while the camera is near (`GlobeScene.setGround`, `flightGroundShare`: the ocean is the page's colour, so without it there is no horizon to see); and the key altitudes are capped at 0.9 of the end's, so the climb is monotone whatever zoom the page is at. Seen so far (headless, dark theme, 1440 x 900): the U over the horizon with the earth along the bottom, the camera backing off over the Mediterranean with the letters in view, the word lined up in front of the planet at about 17 s. Open: the letters are seen along their line (the camera is on the word's track, so they are foreshortened and diagonal; the studio's is beside it), the hero title covers the bottom third of the picture, the ground is a flat tone (no limb glow), the cut-short path.
