- **The flight is a spline, not a lock; the hero over the word; no ground tone (2026-10-23, owner: "it snaps"; "keep everything looking the same")**: the camera no longer follows the word. `engine/flight.ts` is now only keys: for each, a position (longitude, latitude, altitude), a point looked at (longitude, latitude, radius from the planet's centre: the word's shell is 2, 0 is the centre), `level` (up as the local vertical or the planet's north), a pitch down and a roll, interpolated by cubic Hermite splines with slopes that never overshoot (zero at a turning point, so a minimum stays a minimum). The follow-the-word version inherited the word's own pace (a slow start, a long ease) and the changing weights of the look, which moved the picture in steps. The word's path is nearly even in the flight's progress (134 degrees east of the page's meridian at the start, 3 at the end), so the aim keys run a little ahead of it and to one side (the letters sit towards the right of the picture) and a test holds the word's middle, and its leading end at the start, in the picture all the way (`flight.test.ts` measures the camera from the pose and the word from the interlude's own timeline). A second cause of the snap: the ALTITUDE was interpolated in logs, which front-loaded the first dive (most of the drop in the first third of its time); it is linear now, the dive is 10 % of the flight (2 s) and a test holds the speed and the change of speed of the camera and of its line of sight under a bound (no steps in 2000 samples). The planet's disc is the page's colour again (`setGround` is gone: the horizon of the opening is the coast lines and the graticule only). The caption's canvas goes BEHIND the hero (z-index 9 against the hero's 10, `CrispCanvas.setZ`) for the length of the interlude, so the word and the planet are under the name and its wash, as the page's own text is; it is 15 otherwise. Seen (headless, dark theme): the planet is in the picture from about 45 % of the flight (while the camera is inside the word's ring, 0.15 to 1 radius up, the word fills the picture and the planet is below the frame); the pitch is raised through the middle (5 to 8 degrees) to bring the planet in sooner.

- **The keyframes, second pass (2026-10-23, owner, from a screen recording)**: (1) the word was seen to APPEAR during the camera's dive in (a high camera sees the far side of the word's shell beside the planet): it is not drawn before `CINEMATIC.wordFromU` (0.1: the camera is in place), and its leading end now starts at 135 E (`leadStartLon`), beyond what the first camera (0.15 radii over Greece) can see of the shell (about 111 E), so it comes up over the horizon about three seconds in. (2) The camera no longer pans up after the first letters: a key can look ALONG THE HORIZON (`horizon`, `bearing`: the point looked at is the planet's limb in that bearing as seen from the camera, wherever it is, a tangent point), with a negative pitch (tipped up 6 degrees, the horizon a little below the middle), so it can back away over Italy and Spain and climb while the horizon stays in the lower part of the picture and the planet stays in view as the letters rise from the limb (held to 0.4 of the flight; a test holds the horizon's place in the picture). From 0.4 the horizon weight falls, the camera turns towards the word and the planet together, and (3) it reaches the space view EARLY (0.82, with the word still on its way in): from 0.88 the planet is in the middle and the whole word is across the picture in front of it, as the studio's logo is (the earlier keys had the word to the right of the planet and the planet to the left for most of the flight). Seen (headless, dark theme): the horizon held to about 9 s with the letters towering over it, the planet clear from 14 s, the word lined up in front of it from 16 to 18 s.

- **Filled land, smoother and wider letters, glints, the sound off the media keys, a clean exit (2026-10-23, owner)**: (1) For the whole interlude the CONTINENTS are filled with the ink (dark on the light page, light on the dark; `CINEMATIC.land` eases it in over 0.9 s and out over 1.4 s; `CaptionLayer.landShare`, `GlobeScene.setLand`). The coastlines the globe already has are rasterised once into a 2048 x 1024 mask (`engine/land.ts`: an even-odd scanline fill of the 121 closed rings, the 7 cut at the antimeridian joined along it or, Antarctica, through the pole; pure, tested against known places and the earth's land share; 2 MB, made in an idle moment when an interlude is planned) and the planet's disc looks it up by the direction of each fragment (`occluderMaterial`: `uLandMap`, `uFill`, `uLand`); the fill is the ink 86 % of the way from the page (`LAND_SHARE`) and the borders go to the page's colour over it (`lineMaterial` `uAlt`, `uAltAmt`) so they stay seen; the coastlines are the fill's own tone, its edge. (2) The letters: the front bevel is ROUNDED (a quarter circle in `bevelSegments` 3 steps, `bevelCaps` 0.05; a few hundred more triangles for the whole word, about 2 000), the back stays flat and sharp; they are 1.45 times as wide as the face draws them (was 1.28; the word is 8.8 cap heights, `fill` 2.05 so it spans more at the end); the BACKS of the letters (towards the planet, not in the sun) keep half of the light and are less of a mirror (`light.inside`); the shine is less (`reflect` 0.85, `specular` 0.22, the faces mirror less than the rest, `faceMetal` 0.3, or the dark band under the studio's horizon turned them to steel against the bright land); and the EDGES shine as the word moves: the studio has five THIN strip lights a few degrees wide (`strips`), which a face does not reflect (it is one direction) but the bevel, whose normal turns quickly round every contour, crosses one after another as the word turns (`edgeShine` 1.6). (3) The sound goes through the WEB AUDIO API, no element: an `<audio>` element is a media the system knows (the media keys, Now Playing and the headphones' button pause it and play it again). The file is fetched and decoded by an `OfflineAudioContext` when the interlude is planned (no device opened), the real `AudioContext` is made in an idle moment after the first gesture, a context that will not run within 400 ms (a page nobody has touched) leaves no sound, a slow one starts where the interlude has got to, the gain does the fades (`setValueAtTime` and `linearRampToValueAtTime`, no interval) and a test holds that the file has no media element or media session in it. Seen in Chrome: the file is fetched once, no media element, `mediaSession.playbackState` "none", the context runs after a key press. (4) The word's EXIT ended where its entrance began (177 degrees round), and the shell it is on is higher than the planet: its trailing end was seen beside the disc for the last few frames and then the word was dropped. It now ends 14 degrees farther (`exitExtraDeg`: the exit's share of the way is stretched, `eggX`), at 191, where the nearest point of the word is behind the planet for any camera it can have, so it goes behind the limb and is hidden before it is dropped (a test holds both).

- **Tuned against the reference, kept minimal (2026-10-23, owner away: "subtle but noticeable metal, as close to the reference as possible")**: compared side by side with the studio's frame. The blue outline was a sticker, much thicker than the reference's thin rim: the bevel is `bevelCaps` 0.036 (still a quarter circle in 3 steps, so it catches the light). The walls were almost black on the dark page (a reference's walls are steel): `sideColor` is `#646b79` dark and `#c3c8d1` light, mid-tones that read as metal on either page and stay quiet. The faces mirror a little more (`faceMetal` 0.38) so they carry a soft sweep of light, and the backs keep 78 % of the light (`inside`; at half they were near black on the light page, and the page is minimal). The letters were crowding (the A and L merged: at that angle each letter's left wall fills the gap beside it): the baked gap is 0.16 cap heights (0.085 before; the word is 9.4 caps wide, and fits as before: `fill` 2.05). Seen in both themes at the landing and mid-flight; 60 fps, no console output.

- **A cut-short flight glides (2026-10-23)**: the first version flew the REST of the path in the time the globe takes to give its size back, a sweep over Africa in about 300 ms. The flight is now HELD where it was and the camera glides from that pose to the page's own camera (`flight.ts` `blendPose`: position, look point and up, eased; the page's camera is the flight's pose at 1, so it ends exactly on it) over `zoomCutMs` (1 s, the globe's size and the lean come back with it); the layout offset (`flightOffsetShare`) goes the same way, the filled land fades in `CINEMATIC.land.cutMs` (0.6 s, with the glide, so the boxes do not come up over a filled planet), and the flight is over when the glide has arrived. Checked by clicking at 5, 12 and 18 s: the camera comes up to the whole planet in one move, the fill goes out, the boxes come in after it.

- **Design pass: the hero a ghost, a lit 1-bit planet, neutral metal, the places' lights (2026-10-23, owner away; direction from a design review and an outside design opinion)**: (1) The hero steps BACK for the interlude: the engine writes `--interlude` (0..1, the filled land's own easing) on the root and the hero is `--hero-quiet` (0.06) of itself with its plate gone (`app.css`): the page's text is a ghost under the picture and returns with it; before, the name and the wash sat over the best frames and the wash left a band across the land. (2) The PLANET is a 1-bit picture lit by ONE sun, the same direction as the letters' key light (`CINEMATIC.light.key`, in the camera's frame, turned into the earth's each frame, `GlobeScene.setSun`): the land (the mask of `land.ts`) is the ink in the lines' own ordered dither (`dither4` on the buffer's pixels, which are the art's) whose density follows the light, solid on the day side, falling across the terminator (d from 0.10 to -0.20) to a fifth at night so the continents stay legible; the sea near the limb on the lit side has a thin dithered atmosphere (0.45 at the limb, thinning inwards); no smooth gradient anywhere (the caption layer's dimming ellipse behind the word, which had become a soft band across the filled land, is not drawn for the interlude). (3) The METAL is neutral and follows the ink's side: SILVER on the dark page (`#d4d7dc` face), GRAPHITE on the light one (`#5d626a`; a darker graphite lost the letters against the dark land), steel walls, a darker back, a two-stop vertical studio gradient instead of the chrome band, thin strip lights for the edge glints; the blue is the RIM only (a decision against the outside opinion, which would have none: the owner asked for a BSoD blue outline) and the places' lights. (4) At the CHORD (the word lands) the PLACES' LIGHTS come up through the dither over 240 ms (`CINEMATIC.city`, `cityShare`, `GlobeScene.setCity`): a dot for each of up to 16 of the owner's places (one per 2 degrees), 3 x 3 art pixels on the night side, 2 x 2 on the day side, in the accent: the studio's cities at night, made of the site's own content; the only accent at the chord.

- **The ring closer to the planet (2026-10-23)**: the word's shell is 1.5 earth radii (`CINEMATIC.radius`, was 2), as the design review advised (1.3 there; 1.5 keeps the bevel clear of the surface). At 2 the word floated far off the planet and the camera spent the first half of the flight inside the ring, seeing the backs of the letters ("LASREVINU" at 7 to 10 s); at 1.5 the word hugs the planet like the studio's, wraps round it in the middle of the flight, and is readable from about 10 s instead of about 17 s. Two flight keys moved with it (`u` 0.38 and 0.54, altitudes 0.62 and 1.5). Not done from the review: a hand-built polygon wordmark (the baked mono face widened 1.45 times stays), a camera held outside the ring at all times (the opening is inside it by design: the horizon rise needs it), and the cities' overshoot frame.

- **The wordmark is drawn (2026-10-23, owner away; from the design review)**: the nine capitals are no longer a face stretched 1.45 times (horizontals thinner than stems, a soft S and R): `scripts/font/bake-word-outlines.mjs` draws each from rectangles, a few diagonals and a few rounded corners, in cap heights: verticals 0.225, horizontals 0.19, diagonals 0.28 along the horizontal, U and S with 0.4 radii, a square-bowled R with a straight leg, A and V with flat apexes (0.26 and 0.22), a plain bar I, the E's middle bar at 86 % of its width (the S is two rounded boxes, the upper open at the right and the lower at the left, sharing the middle bar). It draws them in Chromium, traces them (marching squares, 0.0016 cap tolerance) and sets them 0.17 cap heights apart row by row, as before; 207 points for the word, 8.7 cap heights wide, no font or licence in the chain (`pnpm --filter @catalyst/web bake:word UNIVERSAL 0.17`). On the light page the metal is a LIGHT steel (`#b3b9c3` face over a studio from `#f4f5f7` to `#8a9099`): the first graphite merged with the dark filled land beneath it.

- **Feedback round on the design pass (2026-10-24, owner)**: (1) The planet keeps its OWN look through the interlude: the filled land, its sun-lit dither, the atmosphere and the places' lights are OFF (`CINEMATIC.planet`: `fill`, `cities`, both false; the code, `land.ts` and the sun uniform stay for a later pass on the lighting; the hero still steps back with the land's easing). The ugly layering was the fill under the coastline. (2) The word is PIXELATED to the globe's resolution: while the interlude is under way the caption's canvas is drawn at one drawing-buffer pixel per art pixel (`CrispCanvas.render` `pixelated`, dpr `1 / artPx`, `image-rendering: pixelated`), its window snapped to the art's grid (`renderCrispCaption`), and the word's shader quantises to `CINEMATIC.levels` (7) tones with the lines' own ordered dither on `gl_FragCoord` (which is then the art's pixel). (3) COLOURLESS word: every surface is a step from the page's colour towards its ink (`CINEMATIC.tone`: the face 3 % (light) and 9 % (dark) of the way, so lit it is nearly the light page's white and on the dark page a deep grey; the walls 9 and 13 %; the back 20 and 4 %), the outline (the front bevel, 5 % of a cap wide) is the ink (82 to 88 %), a thin bright line like the globe's, hardly lit; the strip glints are signed (a light edge on the dark page, a darker one on the light); no blue left in the word. (4) The letters are Bricolage Grotesque again, at weight 800 and optical size 12, tracked 0.02 em (`bake:word UNIVERSAL 800 12 0.02`; the drawn logotype looked odd, the S above all): a plain bold sans with a classic S. (5) The opening DIVE eases in and out: the first key is a STOP for the spline (zero slope there: the camera arrives at rest and departs gently), at 0.16 of the flight (3.8 s, was 2.4), the word is not drawn before it (`wordFromU`); a test holds the speeds at the ends.

- **Metal in space, and the exit really goes behind the planet (2026-10-24, owner)**: (1) The word's surfaces are now almost the page's own tone (`CINEMATIC.tone`: face 1.2 % of the way to the ink on the light page and 3 % on the dark, walls 4 and 5.5 %, backs 9 and 1.5 %, the outline 50 %), and what shows them is REFLECTION only: the fragment shader adds `uGlint * (studio(reflected view) * gain + strips)`, `studio` being a few soft boxes (a broad one upper left, a tight bright one at the right, one below, a sky share), signed by the page (a light sheen on the dark page, a darker one on the light, where lighter cannot show), with the bevel taking the most of it (`edgeShine` 1.5), the walls next (`wallShine` 1.1), the faces less (0.8) and the backs hardly; the old albedo-times-environment mix (a plastic look) is gone. The reflected direction comes from the vector to the fragment, so the sheen slides across a face and runs along the bevels as the word turns, drawn in the dithered tones of the globe's own pixels. (2) THE EXIT BUG, for real this time. Cause (found by working the geometry through, not by timing): the word is about 114 degrees long on a ring at 1.5 earth radii, and the planet hides only about 80 degrees of that ring directly behind it, so a word sliding round the ring can NEVER be hidden by the planet; and the 14 degrees I had added to its end (to hide it) made its centre pass the back and its far end wrap round to the OTHER limb, where it was seen until it was dropped. Now (a) the exit ends exactly BEHIND the planet, half a turn from the view, wherever the word came in from (`eggX` stretches the exit's share of the way to end at pi), and (b) the ring TUCKS IN towards the planet as the word goes round (`eggRadius`: from 0.3 of the exit, smoothly, to 1.08 radii, `CINEMATIC.tuck`), so that at the end all of it is behind the disc even from the far cameras the interlude ends on (a test works the visibility out on the sphere, for four page longitudes and four camera distances, and holds that on the full ring a sliver is seen). Checked in the browser frame by frame: the word is culled as hidden 3 frames before it is dropped (at -167 of its 180 degrees) and never reappears.

- **Bands not lobes, a dissolving exit, a lighter light page (2026-10-24, owner)**: (1) The reflections were two round lobes of a studio, which a large flat face reflects as DISCS (two planets on the letters in the far view, with nothing there): they are BANDS now (`band`: the directions within a half-width of a great circle, so a straight sweep across a face and a line round a bevel), with a thin set for the bevels. (2) The exit no longer shrinks the word (the ring's tuck-in is gone, with `CINEMATIC.tuck` and `eggRadius`): the letters are UNLOADED as they pass behind the planet. The word's shader gets each fragment's angle from the PAGE's view axis (`vCosPsi`, the strip's world position against `uAxis`, which is the page's longitude and latitude, not the flying camera's) and, on the way out only (`uDissolve`, from the middle of the word's pass), discards it in the lines' own ordered dither as that angle goes from 96 to 124 degrees (`CINEMATIC.dissolve`): the ring is 1.5 radii above the planet, so a letter reaches the disc's limb at about 125 degrees round, and they are eaten AT the limb, the farthest round first, one letter after another, at full size. From `final` (0.9) of the exit everything dissolves, whatever the word's span (a phone's is longer, a test holds both); `eggExitShare` is the share of the exit. The word is still dropped at the end only when nothing of it is drawn, so there is no pop. (3) Light page: the metal was a mean 0.69 grey (measured on the lockup); the reflections' strength is halved (`glint` -0.06), the tones are a hair nearer the page, and `tone.exposure` lifts the lit surface (1.18 on the light page): measured on the lockup the word's pixels now average 0.72 grey with a median of 0.74 (from 0.69 / 0.67), the faces mostly near white and the dither and the outline carrying the form; it can go lighter still (`glint`, `exposure`).

- **Light page: near-white material, high-contrast reflections (2026-10-24, owner: "bland")**: the previous fix greyed the whole word to lower the reflections' cost; the owner wanted the opposite, a material very close to the page's colour with MORE contrasty reflections. So on the light page (`CINEMATIC.tone.light`): the reflection strength is up to -0.62 (crisp dark chrome streaks, was -0.06), the broad "sky" share that lifted every face is OFF (`sky: 0`, it was a veil of grey over the faces; the dark page keeps it), and the FACES take only 0.3 of the reflection (`faceGain`: a flat face reflects almost one direction, so it fell wholly inside a band or outside it, a grey or a white face at random), while the bevels and the walls, whose normals turn quickly, carry the contrast. Seen mid-flight: white metal with sharp dark streaks; at the lockup: near-white faces, dark bevel and wall lines. The dark page is unchanged.

- **No stop at the opening shot; an atmosphere (2026-10-24, owner)**: (1) The "stop" was geometric. The opening key was a turning point of the path (the altitude fell to it and rose from it, the longitude went out to it and came back west), and a path that reverses at a point has to be at rest there: no easing can help, and the speed there measured 0.13 against 34 either side. The fix is a path that turns THROUGH the point as an arc: an APPROACH key out over the Levant (`FLIGHT.keys[0]`: 56 E, 29 N, 1.5 radii up, u 0.2) from which the camera swings in WEST along the surface, so it arrives at the opening shot already moving west and goes on west over Italy; the altitude is the only turning component there and the longitude and latitude carry on. Only the START eases in (zero slope at the page's camera; the opening key is no longer a stop for the spline). The dive is longer (the opening shot at u 0.35, 7 s, its peak speed 30 against 47), the later keys move to match (u 0.49, 0.61, 0.75, 0.87), and the word is held back to match (`wordFromU` 0.35, its leading end starts at 180 E). Measured speeds (radii per unit of the flight): up from rest to 30, down to about 3 through the opening shot, then 5 to 8 over the Mediterranean; a test holds that the slowest moment from the dive to the middle is at least 0.3 of the cruise speed. (2) ATMOSPHERE, for the interlude only: a thin glow of the accent round the planet's limb in the lines' own dither (`atmosphereMaterial`): the inside (BackSide) of a shell 4 % above the surface, so it shows only beyond the disc, brightest at the planet's edge (a power of the angle to the eye), stronger on the sunward side, covering at most half the pixels (0.34 on the light page, where the accent is loud), faded with the interlude (`setAtmosphere`; the shell is not drawn at all outside it). Close to the surface in the opening it is also the haze along the horizon.

- **The atmosphere is a mist (2026-10-24, owner: "a mist above the earth, covering it up, fading away smoothly above the surface")**: the first version was a dotted halo OUTSIDE the limb with a hard inner edge; a mist lies ON the earth and thins upward. Two layers in the lines' own dither (the density of the dither is the gradient; `engine/materials.ts`, `GlobeScene.setAtmosphere`, drawn only during the interlude): (1) `mistMaterial`, a shell a hair (0.4 %) over the surface, over the lines: its density grows with how grazing the ground is seen (a smoothstep from 0.4 to 1 of `1 - n.eye`), so the coastlines and borders go into the haze as they recede towards the horizon and come out as they near, in the page's colour with 22 % of the accent; from a low camera (the opening) it covers the whole far ground. (2) `atmosphereMaterial`, the inside of a shell 6 % up, seen only beyond the disc: its density is 1 at the planet's edge (normalised by the sight line that just grazes the planet, `edge = sqrt(1 - 1/shell^2)`: the first version was not, and was a hundredth as dense as it should be) and falls smoothly to 0 at the top of the shell (a 1.7 power), a little denser on the sunward side; 0.8 of the pixels at its densest on the dark page, 0.5 on the light, where the accent is loud. Seen from the opening to the lockup, both themes; the frame rate under a 4x CPU throttle is unchanged (60 fps, nothing over 50 ms).

- **The mist is monochrome and quiet (2026-10-24, owner: "subtle, NOT blue, monochrome, more subtle still")**: no accent in it any more: the glow above the limb is a grey half way from the page's colour to its ink, the veil on the ground the page's colour with 12 % of the ink; their densities are about a third of what they were (glow 0.28 dark / 0.22 light of the pixels at its densest, was 0.8 / 0.5; veil 0.42 / 0.34, was 0.85 / 0.7), set in `GlobeScene.applyTheme`. The shape (a veil thickening to the horizon over the lines, a glow that falls smoothly from the planet's edge) is unchanged.
- **The mist is grey, not dots, and hugs the limb; faint beams rise from the land (2026-10-24, owner: "the dither reads as ghosting, black or white; use grey; a few pixels above the globe; faint beams to space like the original")**: the glow and the veil are now a smooth GREY alpha (normal blending over what is behind), not a dither. The glow's shell is sized each frame to stand `BEAMS.glowPx` (4) drawing-buffer pixels over the limb at any distance (`GlobeScene.frameAtmosphere`: `px * sqrt(d^2 - 1) / focalPx`, kept between 0.4 % and 6 % of the radius), the unit-sphere shell scaled to it. 20 beams (`prepareBeams`, seeded, on land from a 512 x 256 mask, none at the ice caps): a quad each laid out in the vertex shader across its projected direction (1.6 px wide, `beamMaterial`), 0.22 to 0.6 radii tall, fading with height, each swelling and ebbing at its own pace; grey at 0.30 dark / 0.26 light alpha at the foot. Hidden behind the planet by the depth buffer; drawn only while the mist is.
- **Physical mist, more beams, a level lockup (2026-10-24, owner: "the mist should shrink as you zoom out; more beams; the final frame upright and level, the word wider than the planet, as the reference")**: the glow's shell is a FIXED height over the surface (`BEAMS.shell`, 0.022 radii), not a fixed number of pixels, so it shrinks with the planet; 48 beams (was 20). The word's ring is 2 radii (was 1.5) and the pull-back is a full level (planet at half size), so the word spans about 1.8 planet diameters at the lockup (UN and AL in space, IVERS over the planet). The lean (12 degrees) and the camera's latitude stay through the animation and are put right over the last `shape.levelMs` (5.5 s) before the word lands (`CaptionMotion.eggLevel`, `CaptionLayer.levelShare/rollRad`, camera latitude to `CINEMATIC.lockLat` 4 degrees in `Renderer.drawView`): the lockup is upright and the word on the equator is a nearly straight line; the pull-back then gives it all back after the exit.
- **The mist is pixel-stepped, bleeds onto the planet, and the beams are a land grain (2026-10-24, owner: "keep the pixelation, bleed onto the globe, lose the beams for a faint pixel texture on the land")**: the glow and the veil are quantised to 3 flat grey levels, the steps between them taken by the lines' ordered dither on the art's pixels (`MIST_GLSL` `stepped`): grey pixels, not the ink's black and white. The veil begins at 0.18 of the way round from the middle (it was 0.4) so the glow bleeds well onto the planet, in a grey 30 % of the way to the ink. The beams are gone; in their place `grainMaterial`: single art pixels, hashed on the fragment's position, over the LAND only (the land mask, now prepared whenever an interlude is planned) in patches (two octaves of value noise on the ground), grey at 0.32 / 0.28 alpha, about 16 % of land pixels at the most. (`patch` is a reserved word in GLSL.)
- **Grain on the ground, rows gone, the word rises, the lockup from below (2026-10-24, owner's review of the phone build)**: (1) the land grain is a hash of the third-of-a-degree CELL of the ground (`grainMaterial`), not of the screen pixel, so it turns with the planet; a cell is as many art pixels wide as the camera is near. (2) The interlude's rows are not drawn once they have left (`CaptionMotion.eggRowsOut`, after `leaveMs`): the flight's camera could see them go on round their ring behind the planet and out again. (3) `CINEMATIC.leadStartLon` 205 (was 180, for the 2-radius ring): at `wordFromU` (the camera in place over Greece) the word's leading end is behind the planet and rises over the horizon (frames at 6.9 to 7.5 s) instead of standing in the picture. (4) The lockup's camera is 4 degrees BELOW the equator (`lockLat` -4), looking slightly up: the word bows a little, as in the reference.
- **The land's own picture replaces the grain (2026-10-24, owner: "a pixelated satellite view, for the unzoomed views, fading out as the borders come in")**: `public/media/earth-grey.png` (61 KB: NASA's Blue Marble land, grey, 16 steps, 1024 x 512, sea 0; notice beside it) is looked up by the fragment's direction in `surfaceMaterial` (so it turns with the planet), NEAREST magnification (crisp flat blocks however near the camera comes) and linear minification, in four flat steps of the mist's grey at 0.5 / 0.42 alpha. `engine/surface.ts` `surfaceShare(zoom, landShare)`: whole below zoom 1.0, gone by 2.8 (the borders come on at 3.25), at 0.6 of its strength at rest and 1 in the interlude; set each frame in `Renderer.syncCamera`. Loaded in an idle moment after construction (`GlobeScene.loadSurface`), drawn only once in. It is also there on the page's normal view (faint): `SURFACE.rest` 0 removes that. The close shots of the flight read as zoomed in, so it is gone there; it is there at the lockup.
- **No pop at the word's entrance (2026-10-24)**: `leadStartLon` 205 to 218: at `wordFromU` (7.1 s) the word's leading end is now well behind the planet (frames every 0.2 s: nothing at 6.8 to 7.2 s, a speck on the limb at 7.4 s, then the first letters climbing), so being drawn from that frame on is not seen. The word cannot rise sooner than the camera arrives at the opening shot: from the dive's earlier cameras the ring is seen beside the planet.
- **The land's picture is a dot matrix, lighter (2026-10-24, owner: "a dot matrix, denser where darker, visible until the borders; lighter and fainter, it read as noise")**: `surfaceMaterial` draws one grey dot per cell of the ground (the picture's texel, the lines' `dither4` on the cell's coordinates, so it turns with the planet) where the ordered dither passes the cell's DENSITY, `0.05 + 0.4 * dark^2` (dark = 1 - brightness: sparse deserts and ice, denser forests), thinned by `uAmt`. The picture is smoothed (Gaussian 2.2 px at 1024 x 512, 12 steps, 22 KB) so that the density follows regions, not texels. Grey 50 % of the way to the ink at 0.40 / 0.34 alpha. It stays whole up to zoom 2.2 and thins out until 3.2, where the borders (3.25) take over (`SURFACE`).
- **The dots are single art pixels, unaligned (2026-10-24, owner: "interference with the pixelation; too square")**: the world-fixed cells (1024 x 512) did not line up with the art's pixel grid and beat against it. `surfaceMaterial` now puts every dot on exactly ONE drawing-buffer (art) pixel, chosen by interleaved gradient noise on `gl_FragCoord` (no square matrix in it) against a density that follows the ground: the picture is filtered (linear, smooth regions) and thins out to nothing at the coast. The regions turn with the planet; the dots, like every dither of the page, are the screen's.
- **Dots on the ground, borders held, a quieter atmosphere (2026-10-24, owner's review)**: (1) the land's dots are chosen by gradient noise on the cell of the GROUND (2048 x 1024, about an art pixel at the resting view), so they move with the planet instead of staying on the screen (the noise has no square matrix, so the interference with the art's grid is not back). (2) During the interlude (`Renderer.flying` or the land share above 0) the country borders are HELD as they were (`GlobeScene.syncCamera` `hold`): the flight's camera passes through the borders' zoom range and they neither come nor go; the land's picture is likewise not faded by the flight's zoom. (3) The glow and the veil are fainter and, in the light theme, paler: glow alpha 0.40 dark / 0.26 light (was 0.55 / 0.45) in a grey 50 % / 30 % of the way to the ink; veil 0.36 / 0.24 in 30 % / 18 %.
- **The land is flat patches in a four-colour palette (2026-10-24, owner: "instead of the dot matrix, basic colours, limited, 8-bit")**: `surfaceMaterial` no longer draws dots: the sea is the page's colour and the land is one of THREE flat tones by the brightness of the ground (thresholds 0.42 / 0.62 on the picture; tones 0.20 / 0.13 / 0.07 of the way from the page's colour to its ink in the dark scheme, 0.17 / 0.11 / 0.06 in the light), SOLID colours (no blending) sampled with NEAREST from the 1024 x 512 picture, so the patches have square pixel-art edges, belong to the ground and stay crisp as the camera nears (big blocks in the close flight shots). The layer is drawn UNDER the lines (render order 0.6), which stay over it. `uStrength` (`SURFACE.rest` 0.8 at rest, 1 in the interlude) scales how far the tones are from the page; the zoom fade (`surfaceFade`, 2.2 to 3.2) takes pixels out in the lines' dither. The tones are monochrome, like the rest of the page; hues, if wanted, are the three `uTone` colours set in `GlobeScene.applyTheme`.
- **The land fill is the coastlines' own, at the art's resolution (2026-10-24, owner: "the texture is too low-res: gaps between the outline and the fill, and the pixels become huge when you zoom")**: the land/sea edge of `surfaceMaterial` is the land mask built from the very polylines the globe draws (`landMask`, now 4096 x 2048: 7 ms, 8 MB, made once the satellite picture is in, `GlobeScene.prepareLand`), sampled LINEAR and cut at a half, so the edge falls between the texels, on the outline; the tones come from the 1024 x 512 picture, also sampled linear (its sea side is filled outwards from the coast, so there is ground on both sides of the line), and every fragment is cut on its own: the patches and the land are one art pixel fine at any distance, with no blocks. The 2048 grid of the occluder's fill (off) is unchanged in code; it shares the new texture.
- **The land is always the half-faded look (2026-10-24, owner: "the look it has half way through its fade is what we want all the time")**: the patches are no longer drawn whole: `SURFACE.cover` 0.5 of the land's pixels (0.62 in the interlude: `SURFACE.egg`) are drawn, picked by the lines' ordered dither, in the tones themselves (strength 1): a checkered pixel-art stipple, in the three tones. The zoom's fade (`surfaceFade`, 2.2 to 3.2) thins that further to nothing before the borders. `surfaceCoverage(landShare)` replaces `surfaceStrength`.
- **Letters dim behind the atmosphere; the sky stays on (2026-10-24, owner's review)**: (1) the word is drawn in its own canvas, over the globe's, so the atmosphere cannot dim it by being in front: `EGG_FRAG` does it itself. A fragment whose sight line (camera to fragment, in the camera's frame; `uPlanet` is the planet's centre there, set per frame in `renderCrisp`) has passed within `CINEMATIC.atmosphere.dim.reach` (0.16) planet radii of the limb before reaching it is mixed towards the page's colour (`uPage`) by up to `amount` (0.85), smoothly, so the letters fade into the page as they go behind the limb and come out of it as they rise (7.9 to 9.6 s); the mix is before the quantisation to the few levels. (2) The galaxy was going OFF in the flight's close shots: `skyWanted` is a hysteresis on how far the picture reaches from the planet's centre in radii, and a low camera has the planet filling it. `GlobeScene.syncSky`/`SkyLayer.sync` take `hold`: while the interlude flies the sky stays on (its fade-in is the timed one). The hero ghost (`--interlude`) only touches the page's DOM, never the canvas.
- **The studio's small line, and a sound that follows the interlude (2026-10-24, owner's review)**: (1) `CINEMATIC.tagline`: "This is a parody, please don't sue me", the page's light sans in wide-spaced capitals (0.35 em tracking), set under the globe where the studio's "A COMCAST COMPANY" is (its middle 1.24 planet radii under the centre, 0.06 radii high, at least 9 px), a DOM element of the page's own (not in the canvas, not 3D) placed each frame from the planet's centre and radius on the screen (`Renderer.syncTagline`); it comes in over 700 ms after the word lands (`CaptionMotion.eggTagline`), stays through the hold and goes with the exit. (2) The sound no longer lets go when the tab is put behind: it lives as long as the interlude (the end of the interlude, a gesture or the globe going away are what fade it, as before), and a suspended context (a phone's, with the page behind) is asked to run again on `statechange` and when the page is visible again. To keep the picture with the music, the interlude's clock runs on the WALL clock (`CaptionMotion.tick`: no `maxStepMs` cap during the egg): a stall or a tab left behind puts it where the music is.
- **On request, and the debug hooks gone (2026-10-24, owner's review)**: (1) The search palette has a hidden entry: typing at least three letters of "I like surprises" (its words, `lib/interlude.ts` `matchesSurprise`: "impress", "surpr", "easter egg", "parody", ...) lists "I like surprises, Play the studio parody" in its own last group, "Elsewhere", after anything the query found; nothing shows it otherwise, and not under reduced motion. Choosing it closes the palette and, inside that click or key press, `unlockSound` makes the audio context and asks it to run (the one moment a phone lets it) and `requestInterlude` (`globe/interlude.ts`, a window event the home globe answers when the showcase is up) plays it: `CaptionLayer.summonInterlude` arms a plan (`onDemandPlan`) if none was drawn, builds the word's mesh then (the strips are built again and replace the old ones; the sound is fetched then), and starts it as soon as the rows are rolling. When the home globe is not the page (a place or an entry is open, another route) the visitor is taken home and it is asked again (every 300 ms, for about 9 s). (2) Removed: the `?cinematic` flag and sessionStorage `cinematic` (`cinematicForced`, the `forced` plan), the U key, `pinFlight` (renderer, caption layer, debug interface), the caption state's `egg` field and the sound's console hint. A debug page (`?globe-debug`) still never draws it by chance. (3) The lockup now holds 3.4 s (`shape.holdMs`, was 1 s) so that the studio's small line can be read: the interlude is 26.5 s, 2.4 s longer than the sound file, whose chord has decayed by then; the tagline is larger (0.085 radii, 0.22 em tracking). (4) In the light scheme the letters behind the atmosphere dim into a grey half way to the ink (`atmosphere.dim.lightTone`), not into the page's white, which lightened them.
- **Asking for the interlude costs no stall (2026-10-24, measured on the production build, a phone-sized viewport at 4x and 6x CPU slowdown)**: building the word's mesh and the strips in the frame the request started it was a hitch of 230 to 300 ms at the first frame, and creating the tagline's element in the frame the word lands in another of about 270 ms. Now the palette announces the entry when it lists it (`prepareInterlude`, a window event, `CaptionLayer.prewarmInterlude`: the mesh is built and the sound fetched, nothing planned or played) and the tagline's element is made when the interlude begins. Over the 26.5 s of the interlude no frame is over 50 ms at either rate (p99 16.8 ms); the remaining slow frames (up to 150 ms at 6x) are while the palette is open and the mesh is built, behind its blurred backdrop. The sound, with a hidden-tab event in the middle, starts in a running context, is never faded by it, and plays to its planned end; a tap on the globe fades it over 0.9 s and stops it.
- **The word is on the GPU before the interlude may begin, and a stalled frame does not make it jump (2026-10-24, owner: "the word loads too late and pops in")**: the word's mesh and program reached the GPU at its first DRAW, in the middle of the flight, and on a phone that is a stall of hundreds of ms (shader compile, buffer upload); the interlude's clock follows the wall clock (to stay with the sound), so the stall advanced the word by its length and it was drawn already risen over the horizon: a pop. (1) `CaptionLayer.warmEgg`: at the first frame after the word is built, `CrispCanvas.warmMesh` renders the scene once with the word made visible and everything scissored to a pixel of the hidden canvas (on the canvas itself, not a render target: the program is keyed by where it renders), and `CaptionMotion.eggReady` keeps the interlude, the random trigger and the palette's request from beginning before that is done. (2) `CaptionMotion.eggStep`: a frame of over a second (a tab left behind) still puts the clock where the music is; a shorter stall moves it by at most `EGG_HITCH_STEP_MS` (100 ms), the rest is owed and repaid at half as much again as each following frame's own time (the picture runs a little fast for about a second and is back with the music). Checked with a 500 ms stall injected as the word first shows: the old code has the word risen; the new one rises gradually. A real GPU compile stall cannot be reproduced on a Mac.
